## 0. One-paragraph summary — the flow

The library folder (**one** — R7 · G36) belongs to the **daemon PC** and is read and written only by the newly created crate **asset-library** (`engram-dashboard-asset-library` — G16 · G24 · §1-4) inside the daemon process — on a first start where no root has ever been written, an empty default root is created and written down (G35). The screen does not look at the disk; on slot open · focus · folder move it sends a **bus command** (`library.list` etc.) and draws the answer (request style — asset-library answers from the in-memory list and scans the disk only at the first time · `library.refresh` · root change · no watching · no broadcast). `engram` the CLI and the LLM call the same commands — the daemon-side function (commands · CLI) is always on and the screen is a layer above it that can be turned off and on, so implementation is also **server first** (G20 · G22-RESOLVED). Editing and file operations are also commands and asset-library applies them to the latest file (hand-written comments are kept as far as `yaml-edit` keeps them — T2 · G48) — but the screen's preset edit area gathers changes into a draft, and [Save] overwrites the whole list with the new command `preset.save` (no edition check · the last save wins — user 2026-10-31 G52 · G53), while the `preset.*` operations of the LLM · CLI apply immediately. At launch, `AgentManager` finishes the check through the new port `LaunchKitSource` (real thing = asset-library's `LibraryService`) **before** deriving the session, receives the expanded preset as a **backend-neutral launch material** in a volatile folder, and only `backend` translates it into claude · codex flags. Backend · mode are agent values (fixed at creation), and of the settings only that backend's raw field is loaded (the model is inside it too — user 2026-10-18) · instructions · skills may carry a selected-backend tag per item in the preset (G29 — the order is per preset: that preset's common ones · then that preset's ones for that backend · user 2026-10-31 G51) · there are no settings injected in secret — the Engram channel key (`allowedMcpServers`) is also visible in the raw text of the built-in 「기본」, and the claude raw texts are merged in the attached order into a single `--settings` (T10). The codex terminal loads only the priming of 「기본」 and the settings and does not load the preset's instruction file · skills — what is missing is told only to the caller in the notice of the launch reply (P4-FINAL · G28). The working folder is chosen at creation (required — as-is today) and changed through the agent-slot 「작업 폴더」 field · `agent.setWorkspace`, but it is locked after a conversation (a persisted session id) · while running (creation without a path etc. = next topic §8). Launch is **one request**, and when blocked, whoever issued it (screen · CLI · LLM), it is cut off with the same structured error reply carrying the four block reasons (G15 · M19) — if a human issued the launch, the screen uses that reply to raise that agent's preset edit area as a separate floating window next to the agent-tree row (the block window — closed only by × · Esc · fixing happens inside it · its lifetime beyond that is the next topic — P11), while the LLM · CLI receive code + arguments without a window (G13). Settings are checked by asset-library inside the process every time the content is read (syntax · schema — the CLI is not run · knowledge that differs per backend sits behind the backend settings-check port · schema = one copy in the distribution + background refresh — M2 · P10). A file that disappeared from outside is left as 「찾을 수 없음」 (launch is blocked · the scan does not suggest candidates), and when the user · LLM picks one with [Relink], the rest with the same old path · same old folder are fixed together (edition 1 — P1). Moving · renaming inside the app only keeps the order without a progress record, and if interrupted midway one relink fixes it (G38). Delete moves to `.trash`, a hidden fixed place inside the library root (the OS trash is not used for delete · a reference is kept or dropped — human = modal · LLM = argument · default keep · P3 · G31 · ADR-0339), and restore (`library.restore`) is also done by the LLM · emptying · permanent delete is by a human only — handed to the daemon PC's OS trash and decided by the OS (whether it goes into the trash or is deleted outright follows Windows' behavior · what the OS refused (open file · permission etc.) is not deleted and is left in `.trash` with a notice — user 2026-10-31 G47 · the old reason distinction · guard (G45 · M59 · M70) withdrawn) as a shell-only bus command, so one that arrives at the daemon through the control route is refused (G12 · M6 · G21) · it never empties by itself. The view state of numbers 1~4 is held with authority by the **shell**, and OS operations pass through `platform` (junction · path comparison · file identity · OS trash) and the shell's local request. Favorites (collections) and their catch-up material · export · import are left out of this edition (G34 · G37). Implementation ships in two editions (edition 1 = read · edit · attach · launch · relink · edition 2 = file operations · copy · trash · drag-and-move — §5) · after the server stage covers the server share of both editions, work moves to the client (M24-1) · the T8 optional field is handed to the next topic (G10).

**Folder entries (ADR-0341) in the flow** — besides the four kind lists, a preset can hold a `folders` list of absolute folder paths (inside or outside the library), used in place by the backend and never written into; a folder entry is not a library asset — no `Catalog` item, no reference handling, no relink, no move rewrite, no `.trash` (§1-2). At launch claude gets `--add-dir <folder>` per entry plus `CLAUDE_CODE_ADDITIONAL_DIRECTORIES_CLAUDE_MD=1` and loads the folder itself; codex JSON gets only what it lacks — the folder's `AGENTS.override.md` (else `AGENTS.md`) in the merged instructions and its `.agents/skills` · `.codex/skills` as extra skill roots; codex terminal carries nothing from the folder and names it in the notice when it holds something codex would load (§1-5). A missing or unreadable folder blocks every launch with reason ① — codex terminal included, intended (§1-3). The launch reply's notices are delivered once per agent — the same set is not repeated on later launches or resumes (§1-5). Our own parser previews what each backend will load from a folder (`preset.previewFolder` — §1-7).

## 1. Architecture

### 1-1. Ownership — what lives in which process (recommended)

「데몬(asset-library)」 = the asset-library crate inside the daemon process takes it (§1-4 — the process stays the daemon).

| Data | Owner | Persistent location | Basis for the split |
|---|---|---|---|
| Library root path (**one** — R7 · G36) | daemon (asset-library) | `daemon\state\library.json` | A value tied to the daemon PC (memo 58) · on a first start where no root has ever been written, an empty default root is created and written (G35 — §1-4 「루트 · 기본 경로」) |
| Daemon identity `daemon_id` | daemon | `daemon\state\identity.json` (UUID at first boot) | The key of the client's personal data (memos 71 · 72) · current use = the screen query key (§1-10) · `library.status` answer — asset-library only receives it as a value and carries it (§1-4) · favorites, which are personal data, are deferred (G34 — deferral row below) |
| Library scan result · preset-side reference reverse index · parse cache · settings-check result | daemon (asset-library) | memory (scanned only at first · root change · refresh, and only changed files are re-parsed — 2026-10-16) | The client does not read the disk (PRD invariant constraint) · **agent-side references are not held** — read straight from `ProfileRegistry` at every plan · query (M5 · review #6 — so they do not go stale even when clone · create · delete bypass asset-library) · the settings-check result is attached to the content tag (P8) |
| (withdrawn 2026-10-19) the old relink index (path → kind · name of the previous scan) · the record of paths Engram deleted · relink candidates — old `daemon\state\library-index.json` | — | none | All three were used only to produce candidates at scan time (「바깥에서 없어진 것」 = was in the previous index and is not now · delete record = no candidate for what Engram deleted). With the candidate suggestion withdrawn (B11 · user 2026-10-19 「ㅇㅇ 그게 더 자연스러운것같네」) no use remains — 「찾을 수 없음」 is decided directly as **the reference path is not in `Catalog` now** (not compared with the previous scan · at the moment before launch the disk is checked again) · the kind of [Relink] = the field that holds the row · the old name = the last name of the reference path · 「같은 옛 폴더」 = the parent of the reference path, so no index is needed (a consequence of B11) |
| (withdrawn 2026-10-31) the old 「진행 중 파일 조작 기록」 (which finished the remaining steps at boot) · the old 「클라이언트 즐겨찾기를 따라 고치는 이동 쌍 기록」 | — | none | The former = G38 (user 「일단 이건 이대로 두고 나중에 생각하자」 · survey = `docs/research/multi-file-rename-crash-safety-practice-2026-10-31.md` — no peer found that protects a multi-file fix with such a record): file operations only fix the order (Obsidian shape — §1-4 flow ④) and if interrupted midway the rows pointing at the old path remain as 「찾을 수 없음」 and one relink fixes them (R19) · the latter = G34 (favorites are deferred, so there is no place that uses it — deferral row below) |
| Settings schema (claude · codex) — **dropped by ADR-0359 (2026-11-11): no schema is shipped, fetched or cached; the row below is history** | daemon (asset-library — the fetching itself is the fetch port the daemon plugs in · §1-4) | one current copy per backend in the distribution (the built-in side — location · name are an implementation decision · always stands offline) + the refreshed copy = `daemon\cache\schemas\…` (e.g. `claude-<판>.json` · `codex-<판>.json` — the name is an implementation decision · the path is given by the daemon `DataLayout`) | P10 · P10-ADD (user 2026-10-20 「일단 지금 하나 넣어두고 알아서 버전비교해서 업뎃하라고 하면 되지 않음? … 실패하면 기존걸로 하고」) — when the installed CLI version changes, the refreshed copy is fetched **in the background** (outside the library lock · nobody waits — §1-4 `validate.rs`) · on failure what is held (the refreshed copy or the distribution's one copy) is used as it is. **`daemon\cache\` = only what can be made again · may be deleted any time** (once deleted it is fetched again or rebuilt when needed — data that can be made again in the future also goes here) · kept apart from `daemon\state\` = what is protected · `daemon\run\` = runtime volatile (swept at boot) (user 2026-10-19 「cache로 하자. 그래야 유저가 인지하고 삭제하지」 · B14 — the old table had no place for the schema) |
| Instruction · skill · settings · preset files | daemon (the files are in the library folder) | one folder the user chose · default = the `<배포판>\library` made at first start (development · test = the `DataLayout` rule — G35) | R7 · G36 — outside content (e.g. `~/.claude/skills`) is not used in place but duplicated · copied into the root (G37 「외부에 두지말고 engram 안으로」) |
| Folder entries of a preset (ADR-0341) | the folder belongs to the user — Engram only reads it | any folder on the daemon PC, inside or outside the library · the path lives in the preset YAML (`folders` — §1-2) | not a library asset — no `Catalog` item · no reference index · no move · rename rewrite · no `.trash` · never written into (ADR-0341 decision 1) |
| Deleted item (Engram trash) | daemon (the files are in the library folder) | `<라이브러리 루트>\.trash\` — a **fixed place** decided by the system (G31 · user 2026-10-30 「시스템에서 알아서 보내는게 맞는것같은데 고정경로로하고 나중에 변경경로로」 — a rename within the same volume, so it is fast and there is no half-moved state · a changeable place later if the need arises) · it starts with `.` and is not scanned, so it does not show in the tree · list · search (§1-2) · layout inside · name-collision rules = §6-5 · if the root is a git working tree, `.trash/` is added to that repository's `.git/info/exclude` (local · not committed — M22) | Delete always moves here · a file pushed out by an overwrite under Explorer rules also goes here (P5 — it is restored even if the LLM overwrote it) · the OS trash is not used for delete · restore = to the original path (the LLM too — G11) · emptying · permanent delete = human only · handed to the OS trash and decided by the OS (what the OS refused is left in `.trash` — G12 · user 2026-10-31 G47 · M62) · never empties by itself (G11 · user 2026-10-20) (B8 · user 2026-10-19 「ㅇㅇ 우리 trash 폴더로해」 · 「휴지통으로 가되 휴지통 비우기를 llm으로 쏘지 못하게 막자는거지」 · survey = `docs/research/trash-unavailable-delete-practice-2026-10-19.md`) |
| Built-in assets (one preset 「기본」 and its instruction · settings — 2026-10-18) | daemon (asset-library) | one `builtin\` folder of the distribution — files of the same shape as the library (preset `.yaml` · instruction `.md` · settings `.yaml`) · the fake path `engram:/…` points to those files (§1-2) · the lookup location = the daemon passes a value it derived from the install location (M7 — the rules for development · test · `ENGRAM_DATA_DIR` = open item §2-4 · the step that loads it into the distribution zip = §5) · without it the app starts without priming (does not block — M7 · the same leniency as ADR-0092) | Δ1 · G1 (main session decision 2026-10-20 — report · the old 「코드 + 배포판 `prompts\agent-priming.md`」 split withdrawn) · M7 — the source of priming is this one place (it replaces the old `ENGRAM_PRIMING_FILE` external `.md` path = the fixed path of ADR-0092 decision 1 — an amendment, not a discard · user 2026-10-31 G46 · §7 35) |
| The preset attached to an agent · whether it is the default preset · working folder (`cwd` — chosen at creation · changed in the agent-slot 「작업 폴더」 field · locked after a conversation · while running) · backend · mode · passthrough arguments (`command` — as-is today · fixed at creation) · the instruction hash last applied to codex JSON (T6 · M16) | daemon (agent crate profile) | profile file — the new edition uses a new file name (the old `agents.json` is not read · §1-5 「옛 에이전트」 · G32) | Same lifetime as the agent · asset-library edits it only through the profile-edit port (M18 · §1-5) |
| Launch material (merged instructions · skill links · merged claude settings) | daemon (asset-library) | `daemon\run\launch\<id>-<epoch>\` — deleted at reclaim and swept at boot | The same lifetime rule as the existing mcp-config · what the codex terminal uses from here is the priming only (P4-FINAL — §1-5) |
| View state of numbers 1~4 — saved = last folder · field width · tree expansion (▾ opened folders · including skill folders) · 「기본 제공 보기」 on/off (G2 · 2026-10-20 — default off) · sort · filter · selection · last-touched time | shell — **authority** (the webview only draws) | the optional field `library_views` of `shell\state\state.json` | State of the viewing side (memo 58) · one copy even with several windows (⇐ origin) · the LLM reaches it directly by a shell command · selection saving is as in the old description (no conflict with D2 — it was not re-decided on 2026-10-17) |
| Back/forward history of numbers 1~4 | shell — **authority** · memory only | not saved (empty after restart — Explorer practice · PRD R10 · R14 · user 2026-10-17 — overturns the old 「뒤로/앞으로 기록도 번호마다 저장」) | Per-number separation stays |
| Preset edit draft of numbers 1~4 (per number · preset pair) — unsaved edits (four lists + `folders` + unsaved marker + draft number — M81) | shell side (client) — draft file (user 2026-10-31 G54 — replaces the shell memory of M82) · read and write = an invoke-only shell command (outside the shell-command table · not on the bus · the content is opaque to the shell · the shape is owned by the frontend — M93) | a **separate draft file** in the Engram data folder on the shell side (e.g. `shell\state\drafts\` — not the library folder · not `state.json` · name · shape · cleanup = client stage) · **key = (daemon identity `daemon_id` · library root · library number · preset path)** (main 2026-10-31 — M89-REVISED · M92) · the base `rev` it started from (M21 — from `preset.get`) is written along with it — only a 「원본이 그 사이 바뀜」 hint, and it is attached anyway (G53 · it is not tied to the original's OS file identity — M96) — erased on [Save] success (when the draft is still the sent number r — if more was edited after sending, the file remains and holds that edit · M88) · 「저장 안 함」 · [Revert] (G54 · G54-ADD) | Revives with ● even after library n is opened again, the app is restarted, or the app has died (VS Code hot exit shape — overturns the old 「다시 켜거나 죽으면 잃는다」 (M77)) · it remains also through moving a tab · attach · detach · the shell · bus · LLM close · switch · OS close · any other content change (it neither asks nor blocks — no `yes` gate · M90 · M95) · even if library n is switched from A to B (LLM — no asking), A's draft file remains and when that number returns to A it revives with ● · when library n is opened again, the view state shows the last-shown preset and the draft of that pair (after the app died, only when the screen restore was accepted · even if refused the draft is not erased and appears when its key is opened — M94) · opening the same preset in two numbers gives two drafts — the last save wins (G53 · M89-REVISED) · a delete answer received by the same window erases that draft file, and a rename · move answer moves the key · save target of an open draft to the new path (the old draft file erased · the view-state selection follows too — M91 · M96) · a draft file that is open in no window is not fixed by relink · move · rename (G53 the last save wins · M88) · a draft whose daemon · root does not match is not attached and is left as an orphan (how it is shown = client stage · M92) · a file that the LLM · another window · the outside deleted and created again at the same path is attached together with the hint — ghost prevention is only for a delete in the same window (a general guarantee needs a stable identity per file — G38 · T-74 + ADR-0325 amendment · not now · M96) · cleanup target = the draft of a preset that was deleted, renamed or moved (M91) · there is no draft in the daemon · bus (G52) · the shell does not depend on agent (ADR-0270 as is) · §1-10 「편집 초안」 |
| Fold/unfold (common to 1~4) — left field header (library — the favorites header is deferred · G34) · detail card reference group (preset · agent) · edit-area kind field header | shell — **authority** | the common optional field `library_collapse` of `state.json` (fixed element id → folded · the key name is an implementation decision) | One per device, shared by 1~4 (Unreal Details default 「종류마다 전역 공유」) · not remembered = the expansion of the preset rows in the edit area (always collapsed each time it is opened — PRD R27 · G6) · menu · dropdown · part-row press detail card (PRD R10) |
| Agent-slot field header fold/unfold (「기본」 · 「작업 폴더」 · 「프리셋」 — ▾/▸ · the same shape as the library edit area) | shell — **authority** | one common optional field of `state.json` (common to all agent slots · **separate** from `library_collapse` — the key name is an implementation decision) | User 2026-10-18 「머리접기는 그냥 머리접기해 안할 이유가 없음.」 · common memory · the split from the library 1~4 state is the session choice |
| (deferred 2026-10-31 · G34) favorites collection · target collection | — | none (this edition) | User 「그냥 즐겨찾기 일단 뺄까? 추후 구현하는걸로. 일단 좀 써보자」 · when it returns it is on the client side (G33 — 「멀티 UI로 붙을 수도 있어서」 · a fixed id issued by the daemon is rejected) · starting point = `docs/research/favorites-follow-rename-practice-2026-10-30.md` · `docs/research/change-feed-sync-practice-2026-10-30.md` · tracking T item (§5) |
| 「다시 묻지 않기」 (detach default preset) | shell settings | one existing `settings.json` key | The viewer's confirmation habit |
| Expansion · detail panel · popover · state during drag · width while dragging a field boundary | webview | none (only the finished value goes to the shell) | volatile |

**Rejected alternatives**
- **The shell scans the library** — client disk reading is forbidden (PRD invariant constraint), and it does not hold with a remote daemon (later).
- **Per-number view state in the daemon** — it is viewing-side state. With several clients there is no owner (the same reason as memo 58 「레이아웃을 데몬에 두지 않는다」).
- **View state in the webview `localStorage`** — it splits per window (webview), so the library slot of a pop-out window and the main window's edit area cannot share the ⇐ origin, and the LLM cannot reach it by a shell command. The edit draft is likewise not kept only in the webview — moving a tab to another window changes the webview, so it is written to the separate draft file on the shell side (user 2026-10-31 G54).
- **Edit draft only in shell memory** (M82 — main 2026-10-31) — it is lost on restart or crash, and the `CONFLICT` · `yes` gate had to be attached to every shell close command at each bus · LLM close · switch (re-review r10 NF1–NF5) — replaced with the draft file (user 2026-10-31 G54 — VS Code hot exit shape).
- **Edit draft only in the webview** (re-review r10 branch (B)) — lost when the tab is moved to another window or the LLM closes or switches it — the user did not choose it (G54).
- **The library as a module inside the daemon** (memo 96 — user 2026-10-14 「애매하긴 하지만 그렇게 해」) — changed to a separate crate (G16 · user 2026-10-20 「라이브러리를 별도 create로 확정하는건 맞는데」 · §1-4). The old rejection reason 「소비자가 하나다」 is a criterion that ADR-0151 decision 1 discarded on 2026-08-17 — the decision = `docs/research/crate-split-checklist-2026-10-20.md` (can it be separated: all five questions are 「예」 — after moving the backend check knowledge to the backend port (M2) · the cost is only fixed items (manifest · gate registration · public surface) · it is cheapest at creation, when no incoming edge exists).
- **Several library roots** (G25 · 2026-10-29~30 — references across roots · root-name prefix · copy only between roots · fixing references when a root name changes · confirmation when deleting a referenced root) — reverted by user 2026-10-31 「ㅇㅇ 루트를 하나로」 (G36) (R7 revived). To split, export the needed files by **path relative to the root** and import them at the same path (Unreal 「Migrate」 · Unity 「Export Package」 shape — main's knowledge · not surveyed · export · import itself is deferred — G37) · if only some are moved by hand, 「찾을 수 없음 · 다시 연결」. If increased later, a name prefix is attached only to the new roots, and a path without a prefix means the current root (G36). Survey = `docs/research/multi-root-asset-library-practice-2026-10-30.md` · `docs/research/cross-root-move-practice-2026-10-30.md` (kept as background).
- **Using an outside folder in place** (e.g. `~/.claude/skills`) — it is duplicated · copied into the root (G37 · G36).
  - Scope (ADR-0341 — amends ADR-0312 decision 3): this rejection is about library assets referenced item by item from outside, which break silently when the outside file moves. A preset **folder entry** that names a whole folder, used in place by the backend, is allowed (§1-2 · user 2026-11-04).

### 1-2. Shape on disk — asset kinds · file formats · references

**Kind decision (one place in the daemon scan)**

| Disk | Kind | List |
|---|---|---|
| A folder that directly contains `SKILL.md` | Skill — a **plain folder** (can be entered · its inside is scanned too · sorted together with folders · only the icon says skill — R12 · user 2026-10-17 — overturns the old 「항목이고 들어가지 않는다」 and the same day's 「스킬 폴더 안은 훑지 않는다」) · selecting that folder row or that `SKILL.md` row selects the skill · when added, it is the folder path · a skill inside a skill follows the same rule (a skill if `SKILL.md` is directly inside) · name = the front matter `name` of `SKILL.md` (leading and trailing spaces trimmed, inner spaces folded), else the folder name (M13 — collision key · name check = §1-3 5) · `SKILL.md` name lookup = the rules of that OS file system (Windows = `skill.md` also matches · on an OS that tells case apart = exactly `SKILL.md` — we do not force-ignore it, user 2026-10-17) | shown (skill icon) |
| The `SKILL.md` directly inside a skill folder | Stands in for that skill (not an instruction) · double-click = OS default editor (double-clicking a folder = enter) · file operations on that row (rename · delete · move) handle **only that file** — the whole skill goes through the folder row (P7 · Explorer shape) · if it is deleted, renamed or moved out of the folder, that folder becomes a plain folder — if the reference is kept, references that pointed at that skill become 「찾을 수 없음」 · if the reference is dropped too, that row is removed (if there are references a confirmation modal lets the user choose — §1-4 flow ④ · ADR-0339) | shown |
| A `.md` file — wherever it is (including inside a skill folder · excluding `SKILL.md` itself) | Instruction · free content (no format) · no description — preview on the card · an excerpt in the one-line area (R15) · `.txt` is not an instruction (user 2026-10-17) | shown |
| `.yaml` · `.yml` and `kind: preset` at the top | Preset | shown |
| `.yaml` · `.yml` and `kind: settings` at the top | Settings | shown |
| `.yaml` · `.yml` and `kind: preset` · `kind: settings` but broken · an unknown `kind` value (probably a typo) | 「오류 · <이유 · 줄>」 row | shown (R1) |
| `.yaml` · `.yml` but no `kind` · cannot be read at all | Not ours — if `library.refresh` read it again, it is notified in the answer as 「kind 없음」 (§1-7) · but **if a path a preset points at exists but cannot be read, that row is always 「오류」** (not 「찾을 수 없음」 — `kind` looks leniently · M14 · §1-3 7) | not shown (user 2026-10-17 — changes the old 「오류」 row) |
| Other files (images · `.json` · `.txt` · scripts etc. — also inside a skill folder) | Not ours | not shown (user 2026-10-17) |
| Folders · files starting with `.` | Not scanned — the root's Engram trash `.trash` (fixed place — G31) also falls here (dropped from the tree · list · search · B8) | not shown |
| The temporary name of a base atomic write `<이름>.tmp<pid>.<번호>` (e.g. `리뷰.yaml.tmp4120.0`) | Not scanned — the temporary file of an Engram atomic write (same folder · exactly the name shape of `base::file::write_atomic` — we do not make a second rule · main 2026-10-31 M56 · replaces the old `<파일>.engram-tmp`) · the decision = base's public function `base::file::is_temp_name` (tells by name only · the shape is not copied — main 2026-10-31 M65) · never deleted automatically (user 2026-10-31 G40 · the library does not call `base::file::sweep_temps`) · the name carries a pid · number, so the next write does not overwrite it and it stays as is (accepted — M56) · §1-4 `store.rs` · it is not in the problem list · the `library.refresh` answer either | not shown |

Settings · presets are told apart by an **in-file marker** (`kind`) (D7). If we leaned on the extension or the folder name, a move would change the kind.

**Preset file** (reading = serde-saphyr · Engram's rewriting = an editor that keeps hand-written comments · shape — the only place that knows both = `asset_library::format` · T2)

```yaml
# 손으로 단 주석은 Engram 이 고쳐도 대개 남는다(T2 — 편집기 = yaml-edit · 0b 가 고름 · 지키는 범위 = 그 라이브러리가 하는 만큼 — 사용자 2026-10-31 G48)
kind: preset
version: 1
description: PR 리뷰 전용
presets:
  - 공용/기본 규칙.yaml
instructions:
  - 지침/리뷰 규칙.md
  - path: 지침/codex 보고 형식.md   # 백엔드 꼬리표 — codex 에이전트에만 실린다(G29)
    backend: codex
skills:
  - 스킬/리뷰/review
settings:
  - 설정/claude opus.yaml
folders:                 # folder entries — absolute paths, used in place by the backend (ADR-0341 · see below)
  - D:\rules\company
  - D:\rules\personal
```

Field = four lists by kind (R22). The order of `instructions` · `settings` · `presets` is the order in which they are joined (D2). An item is a path string (the old `{path, relinked_from}` shape is withdrawn — there is no path that relinks automatically, so there is nothing to display · D10 · 2026-10-18).

**Backend tag — instruction · skill items only** (G29 · user 2026-10-30 「프리셋에 하는게 맞지 싶은데」 · survey = `docs/research/per-agent-instructions-practice-2026-10-30.md`) — item shape = a path string (common) or `{path, backend: claude|codex}` (M20). The tag is **in the preset** and not in the asset file — the received bundle's file is not edited · the same file is used differently per preset · the front matter is not stripped (G29). `presets` · `settings` items stay as plain path strings — for settings, the `claude:` · `codex:` fields inside the file already tell the backend. Loading order · collisions = §1-3 1 · 5.

**Folder entries — `folders`** (ADR-0341 decision 1 · user 2026-11-04) — a fifth list beside the four kind lists. It is not a kind list: R22's kind sections stay four and D2's order rule is unchanged. Each item is a plain string = an **absolute** folder path on the daemon PC (drive or UNC form · inside or outside the library · `~` and environment variables are not expanded); a relative path or a `LibPath` is refused by `preset.add` and, when found in a hand-edited file, shows as a broken line (block ②). There is no `{path, backend}` form — each backend reads the folder its own way (§1-5). Order = entry order. `format.rs` reads and writes the list like the other lists (comment-preserving edits — T2). Missing key = empty list. A folder entry is **not an `AssetRef`**: it never enters `Catalog`, `refs.rs` (reference counts · delete `CONFLICT` lists · relink · flow ④ rewrite) or `.trash` handling, so an in-app move or rename of a library folder that a folder entry points at leaves the entry on the old path (→ block ① — §1-3). Engram never writes into the folder. Identity for dedup = platform `fs::path_key` of the **normalized** path (§1-6). `path_key` folds case only — its doc (`platform/src/fs/path_key.rs`) leaves separators, `.` · `..`, a trailing separator and the `\\?\` prefix as spelled and makes the caller pick the spelling — so folder paths are normalized first: the verbatim prefix is stripped before the lexical steps (`\\?\D:\x` → `D:\x` · `\\?\UNC\srv\x` → `\\srv\x` — review round 2; Windows does not normalize a path behind that prefix, so the lexical steps would not mean the same thing with it on, and `D:\x` and `\\?\D:\x` must key alike) · absolute (a relative path is refused, not resolved) · `\` separators · no trailing separator except on a drive or UNC root · `.` and `..` collapsed lexically (no link resolution, no disk access). `preset.add` · `preset.save` store the normalized form; `resolve` normalizes again before keying, because a hand-edited file may hold any spelling (the file text is not rewritten). The normalizer is one function (place = implementation judgment — platform `fs` beside `path_key` fits). **cmd-unsafe paths** — a folder path that fails the `path_is_cli_safe` rule (`% & ^ | < >` or not valid UTF-8 — `platform::shell`, moved there from daemon `control/priming.rs` in step 5) cannot be passed to claude through `cmd.exe /c` (§1-5 translation row), so `preset.add` and the editor refuse it (§1-7 · §1-10); one that arrives through a hand-edited file or a `preset.save` from an LLM or the CLI blocks claude agents with ② (M8 — codex does not pass folder paths on its command line).

**Settings file**

```yaml
kind: settings
version: 1
description: 모델 고정 — claude opus · codex gpt-5.5
claude:                  # claude 원문 — 실행 때 --settings 로 넘길 settings 객체(Engram 은 해석하지 않는다)
  model: claude-opus-5-5 # 모델도 원문 안 — 있으면 CLI 가 그 모델로 시작(R35 · 선택 칸은 잠그지 않는다)
  env:
    SOME_VAR: "1"
codex:                   # codex 원문 — 실행 때 -c 로 하나씩 넘길 목록
  - model=gpt-5.5
  - model_reasoning_effort=high
```

There is no formal field — only `kind` · `version` · `description` and the per-backend raw fields (user 2026-10-18 — the old formal `backend` · `model` fields are withdrawn). The model · effort are also inside the raw field (D6 confirmed · R5). At launch **only the field with the same name as the agent's backend** is loaded and the other fields drop out silently. Engram does not interpret the raw text (it only checks it — §1-4 `validate.rs` through the backend settings-check port (§1-5) · it does not even read the model from the raw text) — even when two settings put a model in the same backend field it does not check, and what the CLI does happens (T3). The raw-field name is a backend word (the same spelling as the backend vocabulary of `agent.new` — case ignored).

**`description` (settings · preset — B12 · user 2026-10-19 「일단은 그렇게하자」)** — an optional field. If absent, the list · detail show a dim 「설명 없음」 and do not block. The 「새로 만들기 ▸ 설정 · 프리셋」 template (`library.newFile`) holds an empty `description: ` line. How to write it is carried by the priming (the Engram guide in 「기본」) and `engram help library` (§1-7 CLI) — the file format + an example that holds `description:` + one sentence of 「만들거나 고칠 때 한 줄 설명을 적는다」. Rejected = required field (it errors if absent — an agent can write the file directly).

**Reference shape `LibPath`** — one shape of `/` path from the top of the library (R3). With one root there is no prefix that tells roots apart (R7 · G36). Rules: UTF-8 · the separator is only `/` · no leading or trailing `/` · no empty field · no `.` · `..` · no `\` · `:` · control characters. The representation is our type (`asset_library::path::LibPath`) wrapping `RelativePathBuf` of the `relative-path` crate, and that one type handles validation · converting to a disk path (`to_path(root)`) · swapping the prefix on a move (`strip_prefix` + `join`). Comparing whether two are the same file uses the OS rule (Windows = case ignored) through the path-comparison helper of `platform` — the display keeps the spelling on disk as is.

**Built-in namespace `engram:/` (Δ1)** — a fixed fake path outside the library. `LibPath` does not accept `:`, so the two do not mix.

| Reference | Display name | Content |
|---|---|---|
| `engram:/default` | 기본 (Default) | Instruction = priming `engram:/priming` (`.md` inside the distribution's `builtin\` — the current priming text as is · G1 · with the backend tag on an instruction item a different priming can be loaded per backend — G29 · M20 · the split · the id is an implementation decision) + settings `engram:/settings/default` (`.yaml` inside the distribution's `builtin\` · claude raw text `{"env":{"DISABLE_AUTOUPDATER":"1"},"allowedMcpServers":[{"serverName":"engram"}]}` — turn automatic update off + the Engram message · control channel (server name = agent `MCP_SERVER_NAME`) · T10 · user 2026-10-18 「프리셋으로 해줘. 사실 감추는 설정이 있으면 안되지.」 — the old id `engram:/settings/no-autoupdate` was changed because the content grew) |

**The real thing = one `builtin\` folder of the distribution** (G1 · main session decision 2026-10-20 — report) — it keeps preset `.yaml` · instruction `.md` · settings `.yaml` in the same shape as the library, and `engram:/…` maps onto those files (the old 「코드 + 배포판 `prompts\agent-priming.md`」 split is withdrawn). The file names · the shape of the mapping are implementation decisions. **The source of priming is this one place** (M7 — replaces the fixed path `prompts/agent-priming.md` of ADR-0092 decision 1 · the external `.md` seam itself is as is · amendment — user 2026-10-31 G46) · the lookup location · when it is absent (it starts without priming — does not block) = that row of §1-1. Screen = a separate read-only root 「Engram 기본 제공」 under the library tree — default = hidden · shown only in a number where the tree header 「…」 menu ☐ 「기본 제공 보기」 is turned on (G2 · §1-10 `LibraryTree`).

The built-in preset is just one, 「기본」 — the old `engram:/backend/claude` · `engram:/backend/codex` (「claude 기본」 · 「codex 기본」 — content = one `backend` field) are withdrawn (user 2026-10-18 — backend = an agent value). The built-in preset is read-only and cannot be added to a library preset — if a library preset file points at `engram:/…`, that row is 「오류 · 기본 제공 프리셋은 추가할 수 없다」 (Δ1). It can be attached to an agent. **Copying** it into the library is allowed (P6 · edition 2 — copy from the 「Engram 기본 제공」 root · paste · the copy is an ordinary library file).

### 1-3. Resolution — 「실제로 받는 것」 (`asset_library::resolve`, pure function)

Input = an agent (backend + **mode** + whether default + the list of attached presets — what is loaded, which differs by mode, is given by the backend settings-check port · §1-5 「싣는 것」 · flow check G17) or a single preset + a lookup function that finds items (tested with a fake store). Output = `Resolution`.

1. **Expansion** — for an agent, [the default (if attached)] then [the attached presets in order]. Expanding one preset = first expand the `presets` inside it in order (D1 — the instructions of a preset contained inside come **before** its own instructions), then its own `instructions` · `settings` · `skills`. **Backend tag (G29 · M20 · user 2026-10-31 G51)** — instructions · skills are loaded **per preset**: for each preset in expansion order, join that preset's common items and right after them join that preset's items tagged with this agent's backend (appended — not overwritten). Example: if [A, B] are attached to an agent, A = [common a1 · codex a2] and B = [common b1 · codex b2], the order a codex agent receives = a1 → a2 → b1 → b2 · a claude agent = a1 → b1 (presets contained inside are the same — if A holds C, C's common · C's tagged → A's common · A's tagged). Items tagged for another backend are not loaded into that agent (dim notice — 6). **Use of the tag** (user 2026-10-31 G51-PURPOSE) = a supplement added for a backend that does not follow that preset well (e.g. adding a line for a weak model that Claude does not need) — since it adds what the preset's common items cannot cover, it is placed right after them · if the content differs greatly per backend, the presets are kept separate instead of using a tag. This overturns the old 「펼친 전체에서 공통 전부 → 그 백엔드 것 전부」 (M24-4 · M25 — global) and returns to the first reading of M20, 「프리셋 하나 안에서」 · rejected = global common first (M24-4) · backend ones first (Roo Code) — the same reason (G51). 「기본」 is the first preset in expansion order, so its priming comes before the items of other presets even when a backend tag is attached (§1-2 built-in) (under a global order the tagged priming would have come after all the common items).
2. **Duplicates** — if the same preset arrives by two paths, it appears once at its first place (R4). Instructions · settings · skills with the same path are also **once at the first place** in expansion order (the path is the identity — internal choice §3 · M14). Expansion order = attach order · depth first — e.g. when [A, B] are attached to an agent and A holds C inside, if C and B have the same instruction x, x is loaded once at C's place (the front of A · before B) and B's x drops out.
3. **Cycle** — if it appears again in the stack being expanded, that row is a 「순환」 error.
4. **Backend = an agent value** (user 2026-10-18 — the place where the old 「기본 제공 백엔드의 물러남(Δ1 · 3차 델타)」 was withdrawn) — the resolution's backend is read from the profile's `command` variant (fixed at creation — §1-5) and does not come from the settings. Settings carry into resolution only the raw field with the same name as that backend (joined in the order added) and other fields are not loaded (dim notice — 6 · P12). The tags of instructions · skills are also picked by this backend (1). When expanding a single preset (edit area — no agent) there is no backend, so raw fields · tags are not picked and everything is shown.
5. **Conflict** (red — cannot be launched as is) — **only one**: two or more skills with the same name (different paths) (Engram lays skills down in per-name folders, so they collide on our side regardless of the CLI — user 2026-10-18 · the old 「백엔드 정하는 설정 둘 이상」 · 「`model` 칸 둘 이상(T3)」 withdrawn — no model-conflict check). **Only items actually loaded into that agent are counted** (M20 — common + that backend's tagged ones · codex JSON also puts skills inside contained presets into the loaded set and the conflict check — M13 · the codex terminal loads no skills, so there is nothing to count — P4-FINAL). **Name · collision key (M13)** — name = the front matter `name` (trimmed and folded), else the folder name (§1-2) · collision key = lowercased with `[^a-z0-9_-]` turned into `-` (a Windows junction folder does not tell case apart and claude turns those characters into `-` — survey = `docs/research/skill-md-recognition-claude-codex-2026-10-20.md` · review #14) · if the name is not 1–64 characters of `[a-z0-9-]`, that row is 「오류」 (7 · block ②) — **only on a backend that rejects it** (currently = codex JSON · the decision is the backend settings-check port's — M24-3 · the collision-key normalization is the same for all backends · it is intended that the collision key leaves `_` — M25). Whether skills of the same name under tags of different backends in a single preset (no agent) count as a conflict = open (§2-4). **One conflict = one row** = what conflict + **all** the items involved, with the path each item came by (the preset chain from the expansion start — for an agent, the attached preset — to that item · the screen joins it with `›` = contained in that preset). It is produced as code + arguments (R49 · §3). The red row in the edit area and the launch block reason `Conflict` (8 · E4) are the **same row** (user 2026-10-18).
6. **Warnings — none · non-blocking notices are separate** (user 2026-10-18 — PRD R6 · R22 · R45). The yellow category is withdrawn. The old warnings (another backend's raw field present but unused · raised the codex size limit (old R38 — withdrawn · M24-6) · context share ratio · automatically relinked · the 「다음 실행은 새 대화」 of an agent whose backend changes — Δ4) are told neither in the edit area nor at launch. The reason (user) = rules such as which edits call for a new conversation can change, so the screen does not compute by following that rule · the old conversation is not lost. **Notice list `notices`** (M19 · G28) — rows that state a fact and do not block launch: ① the instruction files · skills that the codex terminal did not load (P4-FINAL — including those in contained presets · codex-tagged ones · the exception of 「경고 범주 없음」 the user made · wording = facts only · it does not recommend a fix — the user's worry that bad wording could make the LLM read that file and put it in by itself · final wording (user 2026-11-09 at step 6 — `trd-codex-translation.md` §4-9, in `launch.rs`) = 「알림: codex 터미널 모드는 프리셋 지시서와 스킬을 지원하지 않습니다. 적용되지 않은 항목: 지시서 <이름>, 스킬 <이름>」 — it replaces the draft 「알림: codex 터미널 모드는 지시서·스킬을 싣지 않습니다(의도된 동작). …」 and no longer says it is intended behavior — G28) ② rows not loaded into this agent's backend = dim (settings without that backend's field — P12 · instructions · skills of another backend's tag — M20) ③ ~~schema dim 「스키마 검사 안 됨」 · 「이 판 스키마로는 확인 못 함」 (P10 · P10-ADD — §1-4 `validate.rs`)~~ — dropped by ADR-0359. Notices go only to the caller (CLI output · screen) and nothing is sent to the agent that was started (G28). The number is left empty because other places point to 7 · 8.
7. **State** — not found (no file — row = 「찾을 수 없음 · [다시 연결] ×」) · error (cannot be read · ~~settings schema error~~ (dropped — ADR-0359; a `-c` line the command line cannot carry stays an error) · the skill name in 5 above · **a path that exists but cannot be read is always an error** — not 「찾을 수 없음」 · M14) · cycle — attached only to the row it points at (R6). There is neither the old 「다시 연결됨(자동 적용 · 확인 전)」 state nor the old 「찾을 수 없음 — 후보 <경로> [연결]」 (relink confirmation pending — the shape of D10 on 2026-10-18) — a row is either 「찾을 수 없음」 or sound (B11 · user 2026-10-19 「ㅇㅇ 그게 더 자연스러운것같네」). **Folder exception (ADR-0341 decision 6)** — 「있는데 못 읽는 경로는 늘 오류」 covers library paths only: a folder entry that exists but cannot be read (access denied · stat timeout) is ① `Missing` with 「폴더를 찾을 수 없음」, not 「오류」 (「Folder entries in resolution」 below · PRD R1 addendum).
8. **Block reasons (right before launch) — four, one category** (user 2026-10-18 「막는게 좋을듯」 — practice: a part attached explicitly is required, so it blocks if absent · `docs/research/launch-missing-component-practice-2026-10-18.md`). It blocks for the same reasons **whoever launches** (double-click · CLI · LLM). ① not found (the attached file is absent — relink only when the user · LLM picks it · §1-4 flow ②) ② error (YAML syntax · cycle · ~~type · range error of the settings schema~~ (dropped — ADR-0359) — an error in the merged claude settings is attached to the later asset row that brought it in (M14) · a skill a codex JSON agent cannot load (the file name is not exactly `SKILL.md` · no `---` front matter · broken YAML (even after codex's front-matter repair — §1-5 「codex 스킬 형식」 · 0a 2026-10-31) · `description` empty · BOM · `name` over 64 characters — P7AC · not applicable to a claude agent · no new category is made) · a codex `-c` row that hits the command-line guard · command-line length exceeded (both modes — M8 · not silently dropped) — §1-4 `validate.rs` · §1-5 backend settings-check port) ③ conflict (skills with the same name — the conflict row of 5 is carried into the reason as is · E4) ④ the library folder (one root — G36) is unset/absent while a library preset is attached. Withdrawn = the old ⑤ 「codex 파일 사전 검사 실패」 (P4-FINAL — the codex terminal writes nothing to the working folder · user 2026-10-20) · the old 「다시 연결 확인 대기」 (only a candidate suggestion — the candidate suggestion itself was withdrawn · B11 · user 2026-10-19) · the old 「백엔드 정하는 설정 없음(R33)」 (the backend is always an agent value) · 「codex 파일 대답 없음」 · 「설정 실행 직전 검사 실패」 (CLI check withdrawn) · the 「화면이 낸 실행에서만 막힘」 branch (user 2026-10-18). How it is shown = the structured refusal of the launch reply (one request — G15 · M19 · §1-5 (a) 1) — if a human issued the launch, the screen raises the block window with that reply (§1-10 · G13). (The no-workspace block = next topic §8)

The same function produces the conflict row in the edit area and the launch block reason (the same row), the **problem list** above the edit area, 「에이전트 N개에서 참조 중」, the three sets of launch material, and the whole notice list — so the screen and launch do not diverge by different rules. `Resolution` has no warning field (6).

**Problem list (B3 · user 2026-10-19 「위에 쭉 나열해 유저가 한눈에 볼 수 있도록」)** — `Resolution` produces one row per problem in the expanded result: not found · error (including cycle) · conflict — **including those inside contained presets (attached presets · presets inside them)**, with for each row the path it came by (「<프리셋> › …」 — the same chain as the path in 5) and the place that points at that row (field · expansion chain — the material with which the screen jumps to that row when pressed). In an agent slot, the launch check (`LaunchKitSource::check` — §1-5) adds the agent-level block (④ no library folder — it looks at the root state, not at the expansion) to the same list — so the agent's problem list = the same rows as the four block reasons. The wording is the same code + arguments as the row display (§3). Survey = `docs/research/editor-problem-summary-practice-2026-10-18.md`. The problem list · the impact row below · the tree ⛔ are computed from the in-memory `Catalog`, so the commands that carry them (`preset.get` · `agent.presets` · tree ⛔ read) answer after starting or waiting for the first scan if it has not happened yet (the same entrance as `library.list` — §1-4 refresh rule 「첫 훑기」 · so that after a restart not every row becomes 「찾을 수 없음」 from an empty `Catalog` · review 2026-10-31).
- **Impact row for a shared preset** (P8 · user 2026-10-20 「싸면 그냥 그때그때 계산하던지」) — one preset's problem list carries an **error** row 「에이전트 N개에서 충돌 — <이름>」 if an agent that attached it would now be blocked (not a warning — R6 as is · pressing goes to that agent). Computation = re-resolve the agents that reference that preset (read straight from `ProfileRegistry` — M5) with the in-memory catalog.
- **Tree ⛔ = 「지금 실행하면 막힘」** (P8) — computed on the spot with the in-memory catalog (pure expansion · check — the settings check is cached by the content tag · P8) · **refresh = the same rule as the library list** (user 2026-10-31 G43): a change that went through Engram (screen · LLM · CLI) goes straight into the in-memory `Catalog` (flow ① 「Engram 안의 변경」) and the screen receives it again on the same trigger as the library list and shows it · an outside change appears only by refresh (F5 · `library.refresh`) or by a block at launch · no new broadcast (「방송 · 구독 · 감시 없음」 as is) — the shape it is carried in · when it is called = the §1-7 「트리 ⛔ 읽기」 row. **Storage · computation split** (G43) — the parse · schema error of the file itself is computed when that file is read and kept with the item · 「찾을 수 없음」 (what is pointed at is not in `Catalog`) is not kept and is computed from `Catalog` when viewed (survey = `docs/research/broken-reference-check-timing-practice-2026-10-31.md` · `docs/research/derived-vs-stored-broken-marker-practice-2026-10-31.md` — a common shape is to hold the relation table and look up the mark beside an item when viewed). Death · failure marks while running are separate, as now. The cost was judged small — not measured (it is measured at implementation time).

**Folder entries in resolution (ADR-0341)** — `resolve` collects folder entries with the same expansion as instructions (1): per preset in expansion order, nested presets first (D1), entry order inside a preset; a folder reached twice (same `path_key` of the normalized path — §1-2) stays at its first place (2). Folder entries carry no backend tag and are not counted by the conflict check (5) — collisions among a folder's own items are claude's to resolve, and codex skill roots are passed as roots, not as named skills (§1-5). **Existence** — a folder entry is checked on disk each time the launch check, a problem list or tree ⛔ is computed; it is not cached in `Catalog` (it is not a library item), so an outside rename of the folder shows at the next computation without `library.refresh`. **`resolve` stays pure** — it does not touch the disk; it receives the stat through an injected folder-stat seam, the same way it receives items through the lookup function (the seam §4 「Folder entries」 fakes · real implementation = `folder.rs` `stat_folder`, called outside the library lock). **The stat is bounded** — a stat on a network path (UNC or a mapped drive) can block for the SMB timeout, so the real seam runs it with a timeout (**ADR-0360**: a launch waits 60 s required + 2 s advisory, a read outside a launch 5 s — child TRD §3-8; measured — §6-2) and a timeout counts as unreadable → ①. **Stat mechanics** (review round 2): (i) **no stat under the library lock** — the computation collects the folder paths it needs under the library read lock, releases the lock, stats them, then attaches the ① lines (the same rule as 「잠금」 ① in §1-4 flow ① — disk work stays outside the library lock), so a slow share never holds up library writers or other readers; (ii) **at most one in-flight stat per normalized path** — a blocking Windows `metadata` call cannot be cancelled, so a timeout abandons the waiting, not the call; a later caller that needs the same path while that call is still running reuses its pending result instead of starting another (the abandoned threads stay bounded by the number of distinct unreachable folders); (iii) **each distinct folder is stat'ed once per computation** — a folder reached through several presets or agents (e.g. tree ⛔ over many agents) is stat'ed once and its result reused for every line. (iv) **the folder listing follows the same rules** (review round 3) — `list_folder` for the backend port's folder load set (the codex instruction pick · codex skill roots · codex terminal notices · the `agent.presets` dim line) also runs outside the library lock and is time-bounded like the stat; a share that drops between the stat and the walk (the walk fails or times out) is treated as unreadable → ①. Missing or not a directory → block ① `Missing` with reason code `folder_not_found` (placeholder) and args {path, via} (`item` = the `folder:` form — §1-5 「Plan and artifacts」); exists but cannot be read (access denied · stat timeout) → the same ① line with the OS error or 「timeout」 as diagnostic (ADR-0341 decision 6 「cannot find folder」 — no new block category, the four reasons stay four · E4). It applies to every backend and mode, codex terminal included even though codex terminal carries nothing from the folder — intended (user 2026-11-04 「폴더 없으면 막아」 with no exception · main ruling 2026-11-04), the same as a missing library item. The line offers no relink (flow ② is for library assets); fixing = edit the path or remove the entry. A folder path that fails `path_is_cli_safe` (only from a hand-edited file or a `preset.save` from an LLM or the CLI — §1-2) is a ② `Broken` line on claude agents (the backend settings-check port's command-line guard — M8), not a ① line. **Notices** — for codex terminal the resolution adds the folder's omitted instruction file and skills to notice ① (6): one notice per folder that holds codex-loadable content (args = folder path · omitted instruction file · skill names — from the backend port's folder load set · §1-5); a folder holding nothing codex would load (no `AGENTS.override.md` · `AGENTS.md`, no skill under `.agents/skills` · `.codex/skills`) produces no notice. Likewise on every backend and mode, a folder holding nothing that backend loads gets no notice (user 2026-11-05). The problem list and `agent.presets` carry folder lines with their chain (「<preset> › folder <path>」).

### 1-4. asset-library — the new crate `crates/engram-dashboard-asset-library/` (Rust `engram_dashboard_asset_library`)

**Crate placement (G16 · G24 · M1 · M17)** — the library is not a daemon module but a separate thin crate (user 2026-10-20 「라이브러리를 별도 create로 확정하는건 맞는데」 — replaces the old inside-the-daemon module (memo 96) · rejected alternative = §1-1). Name = what it holds is the asset loaded into an agent (instruction · skill · settings · preset), hence asset — aligned with this document's vocabulary `AssetRef` (user 2026-10-29 「asset 으로해」 · replaces the memo's example `engram-dashboard-library`). The bus command name `library.*` and the screen name 「라이브러리」 stay as they are.
- **Dependencies = exactly agent · platform · command · base** — the daemon depends on this crate and does **wiring only** (construct · plug ports · pass values). There is no edge going the other way — agent does not know this crate and only defines the ports (there is no type to move — decision table C6 = 0).
- **It holds no network client** — fetching the schema is done through the fetch port the daemon plugs in (port table below · `validate.rs`). agent also stays without a network · tests use a fake fetch (M17). **ADR-0359: no schema fetch — the fetch port is not built.**
- **It has no test feature (`test-support`)** — daemon-side tests use the real `FsStore` + a temporary folder (M17 · decision table C5 = 0).
- **Gates** (M17 · the stage where they go in = §5) — ① test line `cargo test -p engram-dashboard-asset-library` ② dependency ceiling = the workspace name **set** {agent · asset-library (itself) · base · command · platform} — not a line count (the same reason as the cli ceiling line — by line count one cannot see even if the daemon swaps in at one place) ③ add `asset_library` to the name alphabet of the messaging-isolation regex ④ `use tauri` 0 lines (it is linked into the daemon — the base · platform precedent). The sync-keeping (async import) gate is decided when the schema fetching comes in (§2-4).
- **The 「백엔드 모듈 맵」 item of CLAUDE.md is not edited now** — it is added at implementation time after getting the user's confirmation (M1 · M17 · §5).
- **What the daemon passes as values** — the place `DataLayout` of the daemon yields (the `daemon\state\` folder — the `library.json` name inside it is owned by asset-library and attached inside the received folder (a file name used by only one store belongs to that store — ADR-0264 decision 4 · review 2026-10-31) · ~~`daemon\cache\schemas\`~~ (ADR-0359) · `daemon\run\launch\` · the default root place · the `builtin\` at the install location · ~~one copy of the distribution schema~~ (ADR-0359)) · the daemon identity `daemon_id` (only carried in the `library.status` answer — the owner of the identity is the daemon · §1-1 · §3). The crate does not call daemon internals (`connection_core` · `command_delivery` · `control`) — if it came to call them, the crate decision would be overturned (decision table §3 「뒤집는 것」).
- **Assembly order (flow check G17 — two ports point at each other)** — the manager holds `LaunchKitSource` (= `LibraryService`), and `LibraryService` holds the profile-edit port (= manager). The daemon assembly cuts it like this: ① `ProfileRegistry` (the `Arc` that even now stands before the manager — the `build_daemon_wiring_with_store` of daemon `lib.rs`) ② `LibraryService::new` (registry `Arc` · the values above · **the profile-edit port is an empty cell filled late** — `LateRosterBroadcast` precedent `control/commands.rs:341` · after it is filled it is held as a weak reference so that no `Arc` cycle is made) — inside this, the first-start default root (G35) and the `launch\` boot sweep are done ③ manager (`LaunchKitSource` = ②) ④ fill the empty cell of ② with the manager ⑤ ~~the daemon starts the schema refresh once on one thread~~ (dropped — ADR-0359) (an asset-library method · outside the lock · the same shape as the usage-scheduler thread) ⑥ put the cell filling of ④ **before** `command_table_slot.set` (daemon `lib.rs:666`) (review 2026-10-31 — the old 「그다음에 수락 루프 · 제어 라우트를 연다」 disagreed with the code: the control route (MCP server) comes up before `build_daemon_wiring` · `lib.rs:609` → `:651`). What guards the empty cell is that table slot — a route call before the table stands is answered with 503 (`lib.rs:660`) and the accept loop is opened after it (`lib.rs:772`). So no command · launch ever sees a service with an empty cell (touching an empty cell is answered with an error · it is not silently folded into a Noop).

| `validate.rs` | **Rescoped by ADR-0359 (2026-11-11): no schema check — what stays is the hand-over checks: YAML syntax, the shape Engram reads, the codex skill format with its repair rules, the command-line guard and length bound (child TRD `trd-asset-library.md` §3-6). The text below is history.** Settings check — **light · inside the process · the CLI is not run** (user 2026-10-18 「가볍게하는걸로하고 나중에 문제되면 차후에 고려하자」 — the old pre-launch CLI check of 2026-10-17 is withdrawn · measured = `docs/research/settings-asset-validation-spike-2026-10-17.md`) · **mechanical** — what differs per backend (schema source · reading the CLI edition · raw text → JSON · claude merge · unknown-key handling · codex skill format · command-line guard) is produced by the backend settings-check port and this place only runs with it (M2 · M18 · review #2 · #3) | **What** — ① syntax (YAML) = when `format.rs` reads → ⛔ error 「N째 줄」 ② schema — check the raw-text JSON produced by the port (including the codex `-c` line assembly) against that backend's schema (claude = SchemaStore `claude-code-settings.json` — a community copy that the official docs point to · later than claude — `docs/research/claude-settings-schema-source-2026-10-18.md` · codex = that edition's release asset `rust-v<ver>/config-schema.json` · it is the port that knows the source). A **type · range error** of a key the schema knows = ⛔ error — blocks launch (user 「막어」 — claude throws away the whole `--settings` with exit code 0 and no log even if only one value has the wrong type (measured) · being one merged `--settings`, the share of 「기본」 is thrown away together) · **the merged claude settings are also checked** — an error is attached to the later asset row that brought it in (M14). **Unknown key** — claude = not told (at any depth — SchemaStore misses many new keys · violations at nested places the schema closes with `additionalProperties:false` are also filtered out) · codex = ⚠ only (the schema is exact · does not block — the 2026-10-17 decision as is) — but **if the schema held is older than the installed CLI**, an unknown key is a dim 「이 판 스키마로는 확인 못 함」 (notice — §1-3 6 · P10-ADD) · if there is no schema at all, syntax only + a dim 「스키마 검사 안 됨」 (P10). ③ command line — if a codex raw-text line hits the command-line guard or the command line exceeds the length budget, ⛔ error (both modes — M8 · decided by the port). ④ codex skill format — only in the check · problem list of a codex JSON agent (P7AC · decided by the port — the rules that ported every codex front-matter separation · repair and their golden tests · §1-5 · 0a 2026-10-31 · the YAML interpretation is done by passing the parsing function of `format.rs` to the port as a closure — agent has no YAML dependency · main 2026-10-31 M63). **When** — every time Engram reads that content (the practice for cheap checks — VS Code · JetBrains · `docs/research/validation-status-after-edit-practice-2026-10-18.md`): the first scan (all) · `library.refresh` (files whose tag changed) · an edit inside Engram (immediately) · launch (the settings attached to that agent — launch already reads them). When a new schema comes in, all settings are re-checked immediately — ⛔ is recomputed on the spot (P8 · P10-ADD) · the re-check is a `Catalog` change that draws a ticket — it stands in the same line as scans · changes and raises the change generation inside the write lock (if there is a scan that is walking or queued, it stands behind it and fixes that result, and the late merge of a launch check that checked with the old schema is discarded — flow ① 「잠금」 ② · ④ · review 2026-10-31 R3). There is no separate 「검사」 · 「모두 검사」 menu or `library.validate` — refresh (F5 · right-click 「새로 고침」 · `library.refresh`) covers it · there is no 「검사 필요」 state either (it runs at every read, always up to date). An outside edit shows on refresh or launch (no re-read on editor focus — user 「너무 복잡하게 할 필요는 없는것같아」). **Schema — one copy in the distribution + background refresh** (P10 · user 2026-10-20 「일단 지금 하나 넣어두고 알아서 버전비교해서 업뎃하라고 하면 되지 않음? … 실패하면 기존걸로 하고」) — the distribution carries one schema of the current edition per backend (it always stands offline). The daemon reads the installed CLI edition once at start (the port's edition read) and, if the edition has changed, fetches the refreshed copy **in the background** — outside the library lock · nobody waits (launch also does not wait for the fetch and checks with the schema it holds — P10-ADD): codex = if the installed edition ≠ the cached edition, that edition's exact file · claude = the SchemaStore copy does not differ per CLI edition, so if the claude edition changed since the last check, the latest is fetched again with a conditional request. The fetch goes through the fetch port (the daemon = a download that does not know the content) · cache = `daemon\cache\schemas\` (§1-1 — if it has been deleted it is fetched again at the next edition check · B14) · on failure what is held (the cache or the distribution's one copy) is used as it is · there is no blocking because the schema is missing. The old 「처음 필요할 때 받는다 · 데몬 시작 때가 아니다」 (user 2026-10-17 「가장 늦은 타이밍」) is replaced by P10. **How** — the checker = `jsonschema` 0.60.2 (0a 2026-10-31 — the old 0.59.0 · format validation on/off and the handling of unknown formats are stated explicitly in code — the default differs per draft · §1-12) `default-features = false, features = ["resolve-file","idna"]` (no reqwest · rustls · tokio · compiles offline because there is no remote `$ref`) · schemars `uint*` formats are ignored, so integer ranges come only from the schema's `minimum`/`maximum` (§6-3) · the result is attached to the same tag as the parse cache (cached by content — P8 · the re-check of a new schema also changes the result on the cache side — so that a later scan does not rewrite an old-schema result onto a file whose tag is unchanged · R3) |

The later export · import (dependency closure — deferred · G37 「참조쪽은 나중에 구현하자 … 있어야되는기능은 맞으니 연결해서 구현하면 될듯」) only adds bundling · collision handling |

| `builtin.rs` | Built-in (「기본」 and its instructions · settings) | `BuiltinId` · `engram:/…` → mapping to files inside `builtin\` (the same shape as the library — G1) · the lookup location is a value the daemon passes (M7) · if absent, without 「기본」's priming (does not block — M7) |
| `launch.rs` | Launch material | `impl LaunchKitSource for LibraryService` — `check` · `prepare` · `release` (§1-5) · boot sweep (`launch\`) · `release` does not take the library lock (it deletes only the volatile folder — so the reaper does not stand behind a long write lock) · it may be called twice (an idempotent contract — the call sites are the spawn-failure guard · the reaper, one each · §5-2 4 (c2) ② · ④) · the boot sweep also detaches junctions first with platform `unlink_dir` (the same rule as `release` — flow check G17) |
| `folder.rs` (name placeholder · ADR-0341) | Folder entries — read only | `stat_folder(path)` → ok · missing · unreadable (§1-3 「Folder entries in resolution」 — bounded by a timeout (ADR-0360: 60 s required + 2 s advisory per launch, 5 s outside a launch); a timeout = unreadable · it is the real implementation of `resolve`'s injected folder-stat seam · called with the library lock released · at most one in-flight stat per normalized path, a later caller reusing the pending result · each distinct folder once per computation — §1-3 「Stat mechanics」) · `list_folder(path)` = a bounded, read-only walk of the folder (relative paths · no link following · limits = implementation judgment, but under each codex skill root (`.agents/skills` · `.codex/skills`) the walk is at least as deep as codex's skill-root walk — ≤ 6 levels — so the port can apply codex's rules — §1-5 「folder load set」) handed to the backend settings-check port's folder load set (§1-5) together with `format.rs`'s front-matter parse as a closure (claude `paths:` rules — the M63 pattern) · used by `launch.rs` (stat before launch · codex instruction file text · codex skill roots · codex terminal notices) and by the preview command (§1-7). Never writes — no `LibraryStore` write takes a folder-entry path |
| `config.rs` | Root | `LibraryConfig` (one root · first-start default root — G35 · G36) · the value of the root generation (M14) is held not here but by the scan generation `Mutex` (written in one go with the change generation · the swap-in count — flow ① 「잠금」 ② · review 2026-10-31 R3) |

**Refresh rule (user decision 2026-10-16 · 2026-10-17)** — a full scan only when the data is first needed (「첫 훑기」 below) · when the library folder is changed. Other list requests (slot open · focus · folder move) are answered from the in-memory list the daemon holds, and changes inside Engram (move · name · copy · delete · create · edit) are reflected into that list immediately. Outside changes are brought in by tree right-click 「새로 고침」 (that folder — including descendants · root header row = everything) · F5 = based on the focused field — tree = the selected folder (everything if none) · list = the folder now being viewed, both including descendants (since we do not re-read when entering a folder — user 2026-10-17) or `library.refresh {folder}` (omitted = everything) — an agent is advised to call this command after changing files outside Engram (priming · not enforced — PRD R41 · user 2026-10-17 · so the answer is the result of the scan that started walking after the call — it does not attach to a scan that was already walking · flow ① 「잠금」 ⑤ · review 2026-10-31 R3) · the problems of the re-read files are carried in the answer (§1-7). Safety net = if a command points at a path not in the list, only that one path is checked and put in · the attached files are checked again right before launch. No watching · subscription · broadcast · periodic request. **First scan = everything once (user 2026-10-17 · S1–S3)** — it writes the tag of every file and reads the settings · preset `.yaml` to do even the syntax · schema check (there is no cache, so all 「바뀜」 · the schema is the one held at that time — `validate.rs`). 「첫 훑기가 끝났다」 = means that this full scan has been raised, and it does not change with a folder-scoped scan — so a folder `library.refresh` before the first scan also starts or waits for this full scan (flow ① 「잠금」 ⑤ · review 2026-10-31 R4). Start = when the data is first needed — **when a `LibraryCommandHost` method that reads `Catalog` is first called** (not only opening a library slot · `library.*` — `preset.get` · `agent.presets` · tree ⛔ read · an edit command that carries the problem list · reference count in its answer is the same entrance, so after a restart it does not answer with an empty `Catalog` · the launch check (`LaunchKitSource`) is not this entrance · review 2026-10-31 · the old third trigger 「실행 중 다시 연결 후보가 필요할 때」 is gone since the candidate suggestion was withdrawn — 2026-10-19 · the daemon does not scan ahead when it starts — user 2026-10-17 「데이터가 필요할때 훓어야지」) · launch does not wait for the full scan and reads only its own preset and the files it points at, immediately (including the syntax check). It runs on a daemon worker thread — neither the daemon nor the screen stops. A `Catalog` read that came before the first scan finished (list request · the commands above) waits for that scan (merge rule — flow ① 「잠금」 ⑤ · review 2026-10-31), and the frontend shows a loading indicator in the meantime and, when it finishes, shows the list · icons · reference counts at once (no filling in pieces — S3 · if it is slow, look again after the §6-2 measurement). The cache is only in memory, so when the daemon is restarted the full scan runs once more (S1). Implementation = flow ① below.

**Flow ① list request and scan (R19 · request style)** — the two are kept apart. The list does not read the disk, and only the scan reads the disk.
- **List request** — bus `library.list {folder}` (synchronous port · `blocking_handler` thread) → if the first scan has not happened yet, it starts that scan or waits for the one in progress (the request merging of 「잠금」 ⑤ below · other `LibraryCommandHost` methods that read `Catalog` — `preset.get` · `agent.presets` · tree ⛔ read · the problem list · reference count in an edit answer · `library.*` edits whose plan or validation reads `Catalog` (rename · move · copy · delete · relink — an edit does this at the method entrance · 「잠금」 ② · R2) — also pass through this entrance · refresh rule 「첫 훑기」 · review 2026-10-31) → read lock → from the in-memory `Catalog` it yields the direct items of that folder + subfolder rows (including skill folders · skill display · files that are not our kind drop out — §1-2) · reference counts (`refs.rs`) · state. If a command points at a path that is not in `Catalog`, only that one path is checked on disk and put in (the safety net of the refresh rule · before the disk check, the change generation · root generation · swap-in count are written down, and the put-in is done after releasing the read lock in a separate short write lock, but if any one of the three moved in between it is discarded without putting in (it is putting in a path that was not there, so it is discarded even if a swap-in intervened) — 「잠금」 ③ · ④ below · review 2026-10-31 · R2 · R3).
- **Scan** — trigger = first need (「첫 훑기」 of the refresh rule) · `library.refresh {folder?}` · `library.setRoot`. On a daemon worker thread `FsStore::scan(root, under)` (walkdir · `under = None` = the whole root · `library.refresh {folder}` = `Some(그 폴더)` — only its descendants are scanned again and items outside stay as the previous scan left them) → only changed files are re-parsed (tag = path · modified time · size · file identity compared for equality + racy rule — the `catalog.rs` row · re-read settings are checked by `validate.rs` · the first scan has no cache, so all) → `Catalog` rebuilt (preset-side references — direct + indirect · Δ3 · a reference whose pointed path is not in `Catalog` = 「찾을 수 없음」 — the old 「→ 다시 연결 후보(②)」 step is withdrawn · B11). The scan and Engram changes (file operations · edits · relink) are mutually exclusive, and the order of entry is one line (so that a scan result and an Engram change do not cross · neither overtakes the other — 「잠금」 ② below). **Locks (flow check G17 · review 2026-10-31 · review 2026-10-31 R2 · R3 — ① – ⑤ are one rule)** — ① **walking · parsing is outside the library lock** — the write lock is taken only at the moment of swapping in `Catalog` — if the write lock were held while walking, the launch check would wait for the full scan (a violation of refresh rule 「실행은 전체 훑기를 기다리지 않고」). ② **scan ↔ Engram change = scan generation `Mutex` + `Condvar`** (not the library lock) — that `Mutex` holds the scan in progress · the number of changes in progress · the change-generation number · the root-generation number · the swap-in count · the ticket line (the three numbers — change generation · root generation · swap-in count — are put in one `Mutex` because ④ writes the three down at once · the place of the root generation is this one place · review 2026-10-31 R3). **Turn** (review 2026-10-31 R2) — a scan and a change draw one ticket before entering and enter in the drawn order (FIFO): a scan when the earlier numbers have all finished (no scan · change in progress) · a change when the earlier numbers have all entered and no scan is in progress (changes drawn in succession enter together and are counted as in progress). So a scan that comes after a waiting change cannot overtake that change, and a change that comes after a waiting scan cannot overtake that scan — a scan that newly arrives does not overtake waiting changes, and overlapping changes do not postpone the first scan endlessly. **Raising the generation** — a change raises the change generation **while holding the write lock · before releasing it** (R2 — by the time a finished change is visible to others the generation has already been raised · ④ leans on this) · `library.setRoot` also raises the root generation in the same way inside the write lock that publishes the new root and its `Catalog`. ~~**A re-check of settings under a new schema is also a change**~~ (dropped — ADR-0359) (R3 · the `validate.rs` row) — it draws a ticket, counts as in progress, and raises the change generation inside that write lock. Unlike an edit, it does not do the first-scan join at the entrance (start or wait) and stands only by the ticket's turn — if there is a walking or queued scan before it, FIFO puts it behind that and it fixes that result with the new schema, and if no first scan was ever started it is an empty `Catalog` and there is nothing to do (it does not trigger a first scan). The new schema is plugged into the checker **before** the ticket is drawn (a scan drawn after that checks with the new schema). At the swap-in, inside the write lock, the change generation is compared with its value at scan start, and if a change intervened in between the result is discarded and the scan is done again (the second wall — so that a scan started in the middle of a rename is not inserted later and does not carry the old path again) · when the result is published, the swap-in count is raised in the same write lock before releasing it (R3 — ④ leans on it). That `Mutex` is a leaf — it takes no other lock while holding it, and the library lock is not held while waiting for that `Condvar` (taking it briefly in the write lock to raise or read the generation is fine). **The first-scan join of an edit is at the method entrance** (R2 · refresh rule 「첫 훑기」 · §1-7 「첫 훑기」) — before the ticket is drawn · before counting as a change in progress · before taking the library lock, it starts or waits for the first scan, and only after that finishes does it draw a ticket. The other way round it would block itself — the change counted as in progress (or the earlier-drawn ticket) blocks the start of that scan, and the scan that already ran waits at the swap-in for the write lock that change holds. ③ **do not take the write lock while holding the read lock** — `std::sync::RwLock` has no upgrade and so blocks itself. The launch check waits for neither the scan nor a ticket and reads the attached files straight from disk **outside** the lock, and `resolve` · `validate` run under the read lock (with the newly read bytes put in front) and release it — if new bytes were read, that item is fixed by the late merge of ④ in a separate short write-lock section (M11 — so the problem list · tree ⛔ afterwards see the same bytes). The safety net of a list request (checking one path not in the list and putting it in) is the same — after releasing the read lock it is put in by ④. ④ **late merge = conditional** (R2 · R3) — the writing sides are two: the new bytes of the launch check and putting in one path in the list safety net. Both write down the change generation · root generation · swap-in count together **before** the disk read outside the lock (taking the `Mutex` of ② briefly once), and compare again inside the short write lock where they merge. **If the change generation or the root generation moved, the merge is discarded entirely** — it is only a cache update, and a change that finished in between (rename · move · delete · preset edit · a re-check of settings under a new schema — R3) · a root change has already published a newer state (the next read fills it again). If it were not discarded, a merge that entered after a finished change would revive the check result of the old path · old bytes · old schema (a ghost item · the M11 breakage — with only the change-generation comparison of ②, only the scan was blocked). **If only the swap-in count moved, only a merge that puts in a path not in `Catalog` is discarded** (R3) — the swap-in in between may have removed that path (the ghost: launch reads P → P is deleted from outside → the swap-in of a scan that covers P removes P → a launch merge that does not move the generation puts P back). The update of an existing item is exactly the 「늦게 읽은 쪽」 rule below, and putting in by the list safety net is always a path that was not there, so it is discarded if a swap-in intervened. If the two generations are unchanged, a merge (apart from the putting-in above) goes straight into the published `Catalog` even while a scan is running (the old 「훑기 중이면 고치지 않는다 — 바꿔 끼우기가 들인다」 is withdrawn: the swap-in of a scan of folder A put an item of folder B, which the launch had fixed in between, back to its state at the start of the A scan, so the problem list · ⛔ went stale). The swap-in changes only items **inside** the scan range and leaves outside the range as published (including what the launch merged) · if the scan and the launch check both read the same item inside the range, the one that read later remains (reading order = a monotonic number inside the daemon · drawn **before opening** the file · its shape is an implementation decision) · an item the scan could not find inside its range is treated as that scan having read 「없음」 (order = the number drawn before listing that folder — if the launch read and merged an item first and a later listing could not find it, the swap-in removes it · R3). ⑤ **request merging** (M9 · review #10 · R3) — a request that comes later waits for and uses that result without a ticket **only when the scan's range covers its own request** (a full scan covers every folder request · a scan of folder A covers only requests for A and its descendants · a root change is a different root, so no scan covers it).
