Which scan one may join splits in two (R3): **the side waiting for the first scan** (`Catalog` reads — including the edit entrance — need not a new one, only that `Catalog` *exists*) also joins a scan that is already walking · **`library.refresh`** joins only a covering generation that is queued and has not yet started walking, and if the covering scan is already walking it draws its own ticket — the result after walking has started may have missed bytes the caller changed outside before that (the 「밖에서 바꾼 뒤 refresh 를 부른다」 contract — PRD R41). Refreshes that crowd in are still folded into one (a later one joins the first generation that is before walking). **A refresh before the first scan** (review 2026-10-31 R4) — if the first scan has not yet been raised, `library.refresh` passes through the first-scan entrance even when given a folder: if there is no first scan it starts the full first scan and joins it (it joins even if it is only queued and not yet walking — it is new because it was before walking when joined), and if the first scan is already walking it waits until it ends and then draws its own ticket — but on waking it looks at 「첫 훑기가 끝났다」 again, and if that scan could not be raised (failure etc.) and it is still not, it passes through the first-scan entrance again instead of drawing a ticket (it starts the full first scan or joins it — if it just scanned only the folder by ticket, a partial `Catalog` would be raised · if the first scan joined there also fails, that failure is the answer · review 2026-10-31 R5). The answer is that folder's share either way. If only the folder were scanned and raised, the rest would be an empty `Catalog`, so a reference pointing outside the folder would be carried in the answer as a false 「찾을 수 없음」, and an LLM that sees it could 「고칠」 the user's data. 「첫 훑기가 끝났다」 = means **one full** scan has been raised (swapped in) — it does not change with a folder-scoped scan being raised. If there is nothing to join, it queues with its own generation (the scan-generation number — that queue is a place inside the ticket line of ②, so it does not overtake a change drawn earlier · R2). The answer of `library.refresh` = the number of changed items + the problems of the re-read files (§1-7).
- **Changes inside Engram** — flow ③ · ④ · relink apply (②) do not scan and fix `Catalog` (items · preset-side reference reverse index) directly under the write lock · order = first-scan join at the method entrance → the turn of the scan-generation ticket → count as a change in progress and be exclusive with scans → raise the change generation before releasing the write lock (the merge of a late-arriving launch check · list safety net sees that generation and discards — 「잠금」 ② · ④ of 「훑기」 above · review 2026-10-31 · R2).
- No broadcast · subscription · watching. Tree ⛔ is the same — a change inside Engram goes straight into `Catalog` as above and the screen receives it again on the same trigger as the library list · an outside change shows only on refresh · at a block when launching (§1-3 「트리 ⛔」 · §1-7 「트리 ⛔ 읽기」 · user 2026-10-31 G43).

**Flow ② relink (Δ2 · D10 — B11 · user 2026-10-19 「ㅇㅇ 그게 더 자연스러운것같네」 · edition 1 — P1)** — it runs **only after the user · LLM picks one**. There is neither a path that applies automatically looking only at the name nor a path that suggests candidates at scan time (practice — peers make the user find the first one by hand and only link the rest by name · no peer links by name with a mere press · `docs/research/relink-and-error-popup-practice-2026-10-18.md` · ★this overturns memo 68 「저절로」★ · the 「훑기 때 후보 하나면 제안 + 막힘 ④ 다시 연결 확인 대기」 of the old 2026-10-18 D10 is withdrawn).
- **Not found** — if the reference path is not in `Catalog` now (the lookup = the path key `fs::path_key` — if only the case differs, it exists · §1-6 · review 2026-10-31), that row is 「찾을 수 없음 · [다시 연결] ×」 (R19 · block reason ①). It is not compared with the previous scan, so there is no index · delete record (§1-1 withdrawn row). Until one is picked, the reference stays **at the old path as is**.
- **Picking** — the screen's [Relink] = 「+ 추가」 opens a search popover filtered to **that kind only** (kind = the field that holds that row) with **the old name pre-filled** (the pre-fill is our convenience — no peer basis). Before picking, nothing is linked. **It exists from edition 1** (P1 · user's delegation 「알아서 해 순서적인 부분은」 — the edition 1 priming carries 「Engram 밖에서 파일을 옮겼으면 사용자가 [다시 연결] 로 잇는다」).
- **Apply — one `library.relink {from, to}`** (a file picked in the 「다시 연결」 popover · shared with the LLM — code + arguments). Under the write lock, by name without asking one by one: (a) fix **every** reference that points at that old path (preset file rows · agent attachments — whatever preset or agent) to the new path (b) also fix **other** 「찾을 수 없음」 items whose old folder was the same, if a file with the same name · **same kind** exists in the new file's folder (M14). The reference fixing is the same function as the reference fixing of flow ④ (found with `refs.rs` · fixing preset files one at a time atomically · then agents through the profile-edit port — G38 · M18). Answer = the pairs that were fixed + 「N개 더 찾아 연결함」 (practice — Premiere Link Media · After Effects 「X additional missing items have been found」 · Resolve · Blender). After fixing, that row is an ordinary row (the old 「다시 연결됨 · 이전 경로」 marker · `relinked_from` · confirmation button · `library.ackRelink` are withdrawn). Moving · renaming inside the app is followed exactly as now (flow ④ — this flow is the share for outside changes · cleaning up after an in-app operation that was interrupted midway is also this).

**Flow ③ edit (Δ8 · user 2026-10-31 G52 · G53)** — **the screen's preset edit area = draft + [Save]**: edits made in the area (add · remove · order · tag · the check in the 「+ 추가」 popover) are held only in the draft and carry an unsaved marker (the draft is kept in the separate draft file on the shell side — user 2026-10-31 G54 · §1-10 「편집 초안」) · [Save] = one `preset.save` (§1-7) — it overwrites that preset's lists wholesale with the draft's four lists · **no edition check — whatever the disk has become in between (another slot · the LLM · a hand edit), the last save wins** (the Notepad rule — the draft operations are not re-applied to the new file and merged · G53 · the screen does not carry `baseRev` — optional `baseRev` is the LLM · CLI's share, and if it were carried it would be `CONFLICT` when different · main 2026-10-31 — M84) · the write computes the edit going to the list given from the file now on disk and does it with `format.rs` + `yaml-edit` (hand-written comments as far as that library keeps them · the pre-write gate as is — G48) · fields outside the lists (`description` etc.) are not touched by the save · the edit area's own check (conflict rows · problem list · popover disabling) runs on the draft (the computation = the daemon read `preset.check` — main 2026-10-31 — M80) · tree ⛔ · launch block · other slots see only the saved file (main 2026-10-31 — M75) · the save check is all or nothing (if even one item is off, the whole save is refused · a nonexistent path is not off — main 2026-10-31 — M78 · M78-REVISED · §1-7). **The `preset.add/remove/reorder/setBackend` of the LLM · CLI has no draft** — it applies straight to the latest content of the file (one command = one save · no undo record — a wrong edit is edited again · git if the library is a git repository — user 2026-10-31 G54-ADD · the rules below as is · the screen edit area does not call these operations — G52 overturns the old 「화면 편집도 연산으로 최신 파일에 적용」 (memo 41)). Every read answer carries `rev` — **a stable hash of the file bytes** (the same value even when the daemon is restarted or the edition changes — M21: the old 「프로세스 안에서만 뜻이 있는 해시」 made the first reorder after a restart a false `CONFLICT`). `add` · `remove` are applied idempotently even on a stale edition, and `reorder` gives `CONFLICT` if `base_rev` differs (the caller reads again — the old 「the screen receives it again and shows the one line 「다른 곳에서 바뀌어 새로 불러왔습니다」」 is withdrawn since the screen became draft + save · G52). **An operation that read new bytes fixes that catalog item** (even if it ends with `CONFLICT` — so the same `CONFLICT` does not repeat after an outside edit until refresh · M11 · review #12). **Agent-side edition** — the `baseRev` of `agent.reorderPresets` = the same stable hash of that agent's attached preset list (in the order of `default_preset` and `presets`) · carried in the `agent.presets` answer (M11 — a main-session definition · a value read straight from the registry). **Stale edit** (M14) — if the preceding row (`after`) given as the place to insert is absent, it is appended at the end of that field · an edit that carries an old root generation after the root changed is refused (code + arguments — 「루트 · 기본 경로」). **Backend tag** (M20) — `preset.add` takes an optional `backend` · there is one command that changes a tag (name = §1-7). An outside editor's modification is reflected through the same path. Answer = that preset's new state (fields by kind · row states · conflict rows · problem list · `rev` · 「에이전트 N개에서 참조 중」). The 「적용 뒤 참조하는 에이전트를 다시 해석해 **백엔드가 바뀌는 에이전트**를 답에 싣는다(Δ4)」 of the old edition is withdrawn — there is no warning row that used it (§1-3 6 · user 2026-10-18). Adding several is partially applied `{added, skipped[{path, reason}]}` (Δ7).

**Flow ④ file operations (R17 · R18 · Δ3 · edition 2)** — **skeleton = plan → execute → reference fixing** (G38 · user 2026-10-31 「일단 이건 이대로 두고 나중에 생각하자」 · survey = `docs/research/multi-file-rename-crash-safety-practice-2026-10-31.md` — no peer found that protects a multi-file rename with a record that writes down the intent · Obsidian shape). Reference fixing applies to move · rename · relink (flow ②) · delete that drops references · `SKILL.md` rename that drops references · moving out of the folder (the row is dropped instead of being changed — ADR-0339) and does not apply to copy · delete that keeps the references. Under the write lock:
- (a) **Plan** — the old → new mapping table · the preset files to fix (`refs.rs`) · the handling of each item according to 「탐색기 규칙」 below. The agent side is not put into the plan as a list — the only place that reads `ProfileRegistry` here is the `yes` decision (M5 · a list drawn in advance misses a clone made in between that went only through the manager — (c) · review 2026-10-31). The paths to drop for an operation that drops references are also not snapshotted in the plan — every path that the execution deleted is counted in (c) (if only those referenced at plan time were snapshotted, a row attached in between would be missed — the same reason · ADR-0335 decision 6 · main 2026-11-01 M105 · ADR-0339). Since the plan reads `Catalog`, if it is before the first scan, at the method entrance it first starts or waits for that — before the ticket · the in-progress count · the library lock (§1-7 「첫 훑기」 · flow ① 「잠금」 ② · review 2026-10-31 R2).
- (b) **Execute** — disk operations (`rename` · copy · delete = `rename` into that bundle of the root `.trash` — within the same root, so no OS trash · no copy). The stage that moves into `.trash` (delete · a file pushed out by an overwrite) is done inside the write lock while taking the trash `Mutex` (it queues with emptying — it raises the waiting count before taking and lowers it when done (all moves of one operation are one stage — raised before the first move and lowered after the last · holding may be released per item · one bundle per operation — a move that pushes out two by overwriting is also one bundle · R6), and emptying waits until that count reaches 0 before snapshotting the list and before taking each bundle, handing the turn over, so if it is in the middle of emptying it enters after the one bundle now in hand finishes · §3 · review 2026-10-31 R3 · R4 · R5 · R6).
- (c) **Reference fixing** — preset files are written **one at a time**, fixing the row and writing atomically (`base::file::write_atomic` — a temporary `<이름>.tmp<pid>.<번호>` in the same folder + swap-in · main 2026-10-31 M56) · then the agent attachments are fixed through the profile-edit port (M18). Only the mapping table (old → new), not an agent list, is passed to the port, and the manager rewrites the matching attachments in **the whole current registry** under one registry lock (a mutate that holds the entire map — the shape of `profile.rs:471`) and returns the agents it fixed. Reading the original for a clone (`agent.duplicate` — §1-5) + putting in the new profile is also inside the same single registry lock, so the two queue — if the clone is first, the copy is also caught by the rewrite, and if the rewrite is first, the copy carries the fixed original (review 2026-10-31). Answer = the mapping table + the number fixed + the result per item. An unsaved edit draft open inside the window that issued the command has its rows changed by the screen with this answer's mapping table (`library.move` · `rename` · `relink` answer — main 2026-10-31 — M76-REVISED-2 · §1-10 「편집 초안」) · other drafts are under G53 the last save wins (known). **Every list a path rewrite touched keeps only the first place of the same path** — move · rename · overwrite move · relink (flow ② · ADR-0330) all · agent attachments and the lists of preset files and the unsaved edit draft rows of that window (after applying the mapping table — §1-10) (if a duplicate remains in a preset list, 「목록 안 같은 항목 중복 = 어긋남」 (M78 · M85) is violated · `same_path` · main 2026-11-01 M102 · M103 · ADR-0339). **Delete that drops references · `SKILL.md` rename · moving out of the folder** (ADR-0339) also take this path — the order is the same: execution first (into `.trash` · rename · move) → preset files one at a time, dropping that row and writing atomically → agents (the file → preset → agent of ADR-0335 decision 1 · main 2026-11-01 M100). **The paths to drop** (main 2026-11-01 M105) = every path the execution deleted — the item itself and, for a folder, all descendants inside it · in the `SKILL.md` case that skill folder · counted at execution time ((a)) · a preset that went to `.trash` in the same operation is not a target of dropping rows. What is dropped with all these paths is rows of preset files — an agent attaches only presets (`AgentProfile.presets` · `default_preset`), so to the agent drop only the preset file paths among them are passed (if there are none it is not called), and in the `SKILL.md` case there is no agent stage — an agent changes only through that preset. Answer = the paths dropped + the presets · agents that changed + the result per item — an unsaved edit draft open inside the window that issued the command has the rows of those paths dropped by the screen (other edits in the draft stay · main 2026-11-01 M103 · §1-10 「편집 초안」). Agents that attached a deleted preset file are dropped by the profile-edit port's whole-registry drop (`remove_preset_paths(paths)` — name placeholder · §1-5) — only the preset paths to drop are passed, not an agent list (the reason = the same as the rewrite above — queuing with clone · ADR-0335 decision 6 · main 2026-11-01 M101).
- **If interrupted midway** (the daemon goes down etc.) — it is not finished afterwards. Rows not yet fixed stay pointing at the old path and become 「찾을 수 없음」, and one relink fixes every preset · agent that points at that old path (R19 · flow ② (a)). Leftover temporary files (`<이름>.tmp<pid>.<번호>`) are also not cleaned up — the scan skips them and the next write does not overwrite them, so they remain until a person deletes them (`store.rs` · user 2026-10-31 G40 — the old 「부팅 때 쓸어 낸다」 is withdrawn · name shape · no overwrite = main 2026-10-31 M56). If an operation that drops references (ADR-0339) is interrupted after finishing the move into `.trash` · rename · move, the rows not yet dropped are the same as keeping — they stay as 「찾을 수 없음」, and if put back to the original place (restore · renaming back) they attach again, and one drops them with × or after putting back to the original place redoes it with drop references (main 2026-11-01 M104). The path that uses an identity to recover a file moved outside (OS file identity (object ID) · hash — an id inside the file is not a candidate · main 2026-11-01 M109 · a sidecar file (`.meta`) is also not a candidate · user 2026-11-01 「메타파일은 하지말고」 · G61 · ADR-0339 (E)) as a relink candidate will be looked at again after trying it (G38 · tracking T-74 — opening it reopens the 「후보 제안 없음」 of R19).
- **Delete chooses the reference handling** (ADR-0339 · user 2026-11-01 G59 · G60 — replaces the old 「삭제는 참조를 고치지 않는다」 (P3 · M4 · review #5)) — human = if there are references, always a modal [Cancel] [Drop references too and move] [Move to trash] · LLM · CLI = an optional argument (§1-7 `library.delete`) · if omitted, the default = keep. **Keep** — the references stay at the original path and are 「찾을 수 없음」 until restored. If restored to the original place they resolve by themselves, and if restored to 「(2)」 the references point as is at the original place (the file the user put there in the meantime) — place = identity (R3 · user 2026-10-20 「따로 처리를 해야됨?」 — no special handling · P3 · M4). **Drop references too** — plan → execute (`.trash` first) → reference fixing (drops the row by the path of (c) — preset → agent · main 2026-11-01) · it does not attach again even if restored. Deleting just `SKILL.md` makes that skill disappear, so the target is the row of the preset file that points at that skill folder (an agent does not attach skills, so it changes only through that preset · the `remove_preset_paths` is not called — (c)) — what a reference holds is the folder path · whether a skill exists is told by `SKILL.md` (the skill-recognition rule as is — R12 · user 2026-11-01 · ADR-0339). **Renaming the `SKILL.md` of a referenced skill is the same choosing** (that skill disappears — user 2026-11-01 「이것도 링크옵션으로 되야하지 않음? 사실 이런경우 자체가 이상하긴한데」 · ADR-0339) — human = modal [Cancel] [Drop references too and rename] [Rename] · LLM · CLI = `yes` of `library.rename` + optional `detachRefs` (placeholder · default keep · §1-7) · keeping gives 「찾을 수 없음」 · dropping = rename first → drop that skill's row from the preset files (no agent stage — (c)). **Moving the `SKILL.md` of a referenced skill out of that folder (drag-and-move · cut and paste) is the same choosing** (main 2026-11-01 M106 — from the principle of user G62 · G63 · ADR-0339) — human = modal [Cancel] [Drop references too and move] [Move] · LLM · CLI = `yes` of `library.move` + optional `detachRefs` (§1-7) · keeping gives 「찾을 수 없음」 · dropping = move first → drop that skill's row from the preset files (no agent stage — (c)) · 「옮기기」 = `SKILL.md` leaving directly under that skill folder (also into a subfolder of the same skill folder — M110 · §1-7). In all three, if interrupted midway it is the same as keeping (M104). Renaming · moving the skill folder itself is an ordinary move — the references are fixed (not a fourth case).
- **Explorer rules** (P5 · user 2026-10-20 「윈도우 폴더 기준으로 맞추면 되는거 아님?」 · 「llm이 덮을수도 있으니 그렇게 하던지」 · the main session wrote it from memory — to be confirmed on a real Windows at implementation time) — when moving, the same name = [Overwrite] [Skip] [Decide per file → keep both = 「(2)」] · folders of the same name are merged · moving a folder into its own descendant = refused · if parent and child are selected together, only the parent · a name that already exists by F2 = refused + notice · a per-item failure (open · permission) = [Retry] [Skip] [Cancel] — what has finished stays finished. **Only one thing differs** — an overwritten file is not deleted immediately but goes to `.trash` (Explorer deletes it immediately · so that it can be restored even if the LLM overwrote it — R18). **Overwrite = delete + rename** (ADR-0339 · user 2026-11-01 G58) — references are not chosen and follow the place, pointing to the new file that came into that place (a row that pointed only at the overwritten old file also receives the new content · an overwrite has no reference choice) · after a path rewrite the same path within one list (`same_path` — §1-6 · ADR-0325) keeps only the first place (a clean-up — not a separate concept · every list the rewrite touched — agent attachments · lists of preset files · that window's draft rows · (c) · main 2026-11-01 M102 · M103) · even if overwritten with a file of a different kind, references follow the place, and if the kind does not match that row is 「틀린 종류」 (M78 — accepted · main 2026-11-01 M107). The `library.move` of the LLM · CLI takes the same choice as an argument and returns a result per item (shape = §1-7). The partial failure of restoring several · emptying the trash follows the same rule. A trash name collision does not occur — each delete makes a new bundle folder (§6-5 1).
- **Copy** (P6 · edition 2 · user 「복사 간단하면 하나 있는게 좋지 않음?」) — the original stays as it is, so references are not fixed · the same-name rule = above · pasting into the same folder gives 「- 복사본」 (Explorer) · copying from the read-only 「Engram 기본 제공」 root into the library is allowed.
- **Several at once (B7)** — `library.move` · `library.delete` · copy take several paths as one operation (Ctrl · Shift · box selection · drag-and-drop all take this one path). Delete, if **even one** of the chosen has a reference (preset + agent), is a single `CONFLICT` without `yes` (Δ9) — it carries all the items involved and their references (the screen shows one modal — §1-10) · **for a folder, the references of everything inside it are combined** (P7 — CLI · LLM `--yes` is the same) · an operation on a `SKILL.md` row handles only that file (P7 · §1-2).
- **Delete = `.trash` (B8)** — delete always moves to the root's `.trash` (the LLM · CLI too — so that it can be restored). The root itself cannot be deleted (it cannot even be pointed at by a path — there is no empty `LibPath`). `library.*` does not take a path under `.trash` (it is a hidden item, so it is not in `Catalog`, and the 「목록에 없는 경로 하나만 확인해 넣는」 safety net also does not put in a node that starts with `.` — it shuts the back door of deleting inside the trash over the bus · this refusal is the main session's proposal) — exception = the view-trash · restore commands (`library.restore` — an item in the trash to its original path · the LLM too · G11 · §1-7). **Emptying · permanent delete are not in the agent command table · priming — they are shell-only commands that only a human calls** (not set up as an exception to the principle — G21 · M24-7 · §7 28) · transport = a shell-only bus command (one that the daemon received through the control route is refused — M6 · the `store.rs` row) · both go to the OS trash and the OS decides — whether it goes into the trash or is deleted outright · what the OS refused (open file · permission etc.) is not deleted and is left in `.trash` with a notice (G12 · user 2026-10-31 G47 · main 2026-10-31 M62 · §6-5 2 · the old reason distinction G45 · M59 is withdrawn). An agent bypassing `.trash` with its own tools (`rm` etc.) is not prevented — accepted (P13 · G21) · the priming carries only one general line 「라이브러리를 다룰 땐 engram library.* 명령을 쓴다」 (P13).

**Flow ⑤ launch material** — §1-5.

**Flow ⑥** — (withdrawn 2026-10-31 · G34 — favorites are deferred, so there is no place that uses it · the number is left empty).

**Root · default path (one root — R7 · G36)** — `library.status` gives `{daemon_id, root, state: unset|ok|missing, suggested_new}` and the root generation (the value an edit carries — M14). **First-start default root** (G35 · user 2026-10-31 「최초 디폴트 등록해둬」) — on a first start where no root has ever been written, the daemon creates an empty default root folder and writes it as the root. The default root place is given by a **method of the data-folder layout (`DataLayout`)**. `DataLayout` also holds **where the root came from** (`ENGRAM_DATA_DIR` specified · debug default · release default) — release default (root = `<배포판>\data`) = the `library` of its parent (= `<배포판>\library`), otherwise = `library` **inside** the root. It is not guessed from a directory name. When the distribution is rebuilt (`scripts/build-release.ps1` — it deletes the distribution folder wholesale), `<배포판>\library` is also deleted along with it — intended (the same as `data\` · the library actually used is placed outside the distribution by changing the folder (R9) · scripts are not fixed — user 2026-10-31 G39 · §6-3). So a test isolated by `ENGRAM_DATA_DIR` does not touch the real folder. If it cannot be created it stays as `unset` and the screen guidance (R8) receives it. `suggested_new` is the same place, and [Create new] (when the root is absent · gone) only pre-fills it and puts in no content (Δ1 — the first-root flow in the default case is replaced by G35). When the root is changed (`library.setRoot` — R9 as is · G37-NOTE), agent attachments are left as they are and **the same path is looked up in the new root** — if absent, 「찾을 수 없음」 (Δf — there is no relink across roots · `relink` takes only paths inside the same root) · the root generation rises and an edit carrying the old generation is refused (M14). `.trash` is a fixed place inside that root (G31) — the old root's trash stays in the old folder. The root cannot be deleted (only changed — B8). Block ④ `LibraryUnavailable` = that one root is unset · absent (G36).

### 1-5. agent — profile · launch materials port · settings-check port · profile-edit port · manager · backend translation

**What changes in the profile (`profile.rs`)**

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `presets: Vec<AttachedPreset { path }>` | The attached presets (ordered) — empty at creation (the old `relinked_from` is withdrawn — D10 = one confirmation) |
| `default_preset: bool` | 「기본」 field — `true` at creation (R32) |
| Hash of the instructions last applied for codex JSON (name = implementation judgment) | The value that decides whether the instructions changed on resume (T6 — only when they differ, `additionalContext` on the first turn) · author = the launch (runtime) · M16 · review #18 (the old document had no place to store it) · **it is recorded when the first turn carrying those instructions has reached the other side** (the spot where the first-submit latch commits — codex JSON = the echo of the first user message · new conversation = `developerInstructions` · resume = `additionalContext`) · **the way it is recorded = one mutate of the agent's own share of the registry** (incarnation-marker guard · review 2026-10-31) — it runs separately, whether or not the session-id compare-and-replace wrote anything at the latch's first-turn event: the resume compare-and-replace is (expected = stored value · new value = stored value), so it writes nothing (`profile.rs:853-880` `commit_session_id` — `replace_session_id` rejects the same value), and riding on that write would leave the hash unchanged forever on resume · the manager takes the hash from `LaunchKit` and carries it through that closure (now at the `manager.rs:588` `session_id_sink` spot — shape = implementation judgment) · the session-id persistence point itself is left alone (CLAUDE.md 「세션 id 영속 = 첫 제출 래치 한 지점」) — it is not recorded at spawn (a resume after an incarnation whose first turn never went out would skip the changed instructions · flow review G17) |
| notice fingerprint (name = implementation judgment · ADR-0341 decision 5) | the omission identity of the launch notices last delivered to a caller — a canonical identity string, no hash (`None` = nothing recorded · `Some("")` = an empty set was shown) — the manager compares it in `prelaunch` and records it only when a success reply carrying the notices goes to a caller (§1-5 「Notices once per agent」) · author = the launch (runtime) · kept by `merge_preserving_live` · not copied by `agent.duplicate` |
| (withdrawn 2026-10-20) the old `generated_paths` | It was the list of files · junctions Engram made in the working folder — the codex terminal no longer writes anything to the working folder (P4-FINAL), so there is nothing to withdraw |
| (withdrawn 2026-10-18) the old `preset_schema: u32` | It was a migration marker — there is no migration (D4 · old data is discarded — G32) |

**Working folder `cwd` (작업 폴더 cwd)** — the field stays as it is now (required · optional at creation · disk key · wire shape unchanged — empty value · creation without a path = next topic §8). What is added is **a way to change it, and a lock** (user 2026-10-18 「에이전트 프리셋 셋팅하고 하는부분 있잖아. 거기에 항목이 하나 더 있는거지」 · 「대화 뒤 잠금은 있어야지」).
- **Lock** — it cannot be changed if the agent has a persisted backend session id (written by the first-submit latch — ADR-0226 · user 「ssi 박히잖아. 그게 있으면 못바꾸게」) or is **running**. The screen is read-only plus a short reason according to why it is locked — running = 「실행 중 — 바꾸려면 멈추기」 · has conversed = 「대화한 에이전트 — 바꾸려면 새 에이전트」 (P2-REVISED — only the wording splits in two). It changes only before any conversation, so there is no session to release — no new-conversation or session-release logic is put on a working-folder change (this lock replaces the `launched_workspace` · `release_session_id` path of the old design moved to §8).
- **`agent.setWorkspace {agent, cwd}`** (body = manager — no library needed) — the path must exist and be a folder (otherwise an error code + args) · if locked, `CONFLICT` (code + args = the reason it is locked — has conversed · running · R49). The lock decision and the save are done **while holding the agent's `SpawnReservation`** (review 2026-10-31) — take the reservation (if it is already held, a launch is preparing = `CONFLICT`) → look at running · the persisted session id → write `cwd` with one `ProfileRegistry` mutate (if `display_name` is empty, fill it with the current name first — see 「`display_name` 고정」 below) → release the reservation (RAII). The registry is not written while holding the `spawning` lock itself — that Mutex is a leaf lock (while held, it takes neither sessions nor profiles · `manager.rs:670-677` · ADR-0006). The reservation takes that lock briefly to insert the id and releases it, so the leaf rule holds. **A launch that arrives in between loses at the reservation — the answer is `CONFLICT`** (reason = changing the working folder · try again — **but if the losing side is a create verb's after-create activation, §1-5 (가) 1 ② applies**: `create_and_start` (`agent.spawn --cwd`) registers and emits the registry notification before calling `activate_profile` (`commands.rs:1217-1218`), so a `setWorkspace` that arrives in that gap can take the reservation first; the answer is then `INTERNAL` (request number held) + the created agent id · reason in the args field — with `CONFLICT` the number is released and a retry with the same number would create again · review 2026-10-31 R2) · it is not the current `Moot(None)` (「이미 뜨는 중」 — does not start silently · `manager.rs:1339-1346`) — the reservation table tells apart who holds it (a launch · a working-folder change) (shape = implementation judgment). The input of the screen's 「작업 폴더」 field · 「찾아보기」 also calls this command (the same path as the LLM — CLAUDE.md 「LLM-우선 제어」).

**Other profile rules**
- **`command` (Claude/Codex/Shell + `extra_args` + `output_format`)** stays an **agent value** as now — variant = backend · `output_format` = mode (D5) · both are decided at creation and cannot be changed (user 2026-10-18 「그냥 고정해. 백엔드는.」). Resolution (§1-3 4) reads the backend from this variant — the variant → backend word mapping uses what exists now (the backend vocabulary of `agent.new`). The passthrough args also stay a single `command.extra_args` as now. The old edition's 「`command` 는 해석 결과에서 파생」 · `backend::command_for` · per-backend args `backend_args` were for the case where the backend becomes a setting, so they are withdrawn (there is no such case). A Shell agent takes no preset (it is not a resolution target).
- **`display_name` pinned (display_name 고정)** — if `None` at creation, the display name at that time is written in. The old path where the name was derived from the `cwd` basename silently changes the mail address · tree name when the working folder changes — this edition adds a way to change the working folder (`agent.setWorkspace` · 「작업 폴더」 field), so it is pinned at this step. **`agent.setWorkspace` is pinned too** (review 2026-10-31) — even if written in at creation, clearing the name (wire `RenameProfile{name:null}` — `protocol/src/messages.rs:183-188`) turns `display_name` back into `None`, and from then on the name is derived from the `cwd` basename (`profile.rs:282-297` `canonical_name_when_live`) → so **before** changing `cwd`, `setWorkspace` fills in the currently derived name if `display_name` is `None` (inside the same reservation — no silent rename after the change).
- **One new-profile constructor** — `AgentProfile::new` (the constructor that `SpawnByCwd` · the smoke bin · `CreateProfile` already pass through) attaches the defaults: `default_preset = true` · `presets = []` (`command` as received — the dispatch `backend::builtin_backend_preset` that attached the built-in backend preset is withdrawn · user 2026-10-18). `agent.new` · wire `CreateProfile` · `SpawnByCwd` · the smoke bin all get the same defaults through this one path. **The creation flow stays as it is now** (menu → OS folder picker → create → launch with only 「기본」 attached — P2-REVISED · user 2026-10-20 「에이전트 트리쪽은 그냥 러프하게 되게 구현하고 나중에 정리하자고」 — the fact that there is no user preset on the first launch · settings at creation · creation without a path = next topic §8).
- **Added to the `merge_preserving_live` preserved fields** (「런타임이 저자인 칸」) — `presets` · `default_preset` · the last-applied instructions hash · the notice fingerprint (ADR-0341 · author = the launch) · `cwd` (after creation the only author = `agent.setWorkspace` — there is currently no path that writes `cwd` after creation · confirmed). The authors of these fields are the preset commands (profile-edit port) · the launch, not an old copy from when spawn started.
- **Clone (복제) `agent.duplicate {agent}` (B13 · user 2026-10-19 · 1st edition)** — the same command as the agent-tree right-click 「복제」. It creates a profile with a new id; **what it carries over** = the attached presets (`default_preset` + `presets`) · backend · mode · passthrough args (all of `command` — including `extra_args`) · working folder (`cwd`) · environment variables. **What it does not carry over** = the backend session id · the conversation (it starts a new conversation — so the 「작업 폴더」 field is not locked) · running state · the last-applied instructions hash · the notice fingerprint (ADR-0341 — the new agent starts with no notice shown). Name = the app's existing name-issuing rule 「<이름>(2)」 · (3) … (no space — ADR-0115 · ADR-0120 · ADR-0123 · `format!("{desired}({n})")` of the issuer `manager.rs:1215` · M12 · review #13: with a space, the second clone becomes 「bob (2)(1)」) · pinned as `display_name`. Body = manager (no library needed — the same place as `agent.setWorkspace` · the main session's choice). **Reading the original + inserting the new profile = inside one registry lock** (the name is decided before that, under the name-issuing gate, as creation does now — `manager.rs:1162` · `:987`) — it queues with the library-path rewrite (profile-edit port — below) on the registry lock, so a copy holding an old preset path cannot slip past the rewrite (flow ④ (c) · review 2026-10-31). Answer = the new agent id · name.

**Old agents — not read** (D4 · G32 · user 2026-10-18 「기존거 필요없어」 · 2026-10-30 「옛것은 물어보지마 현재 기준으로 그냥 다 날리면됨」) — the new edition's profile store is written under a **new file name** (the name is implementation judgment), and the old `agents.json` is left neither read nor deleted (the same path as `presets.json` — ADR-0264 「옛 데이터는 버린다」 · the main session's internal choice — G32 left the simplest discard path to the main session). So there is no edition marker to tell old-edition files apart, no setting aside, and no separate copy. An old agent's session · working folder · backend do not carry into the new edition — to use one, create it anew. **The new name is also guarded by `.gitignore`** (review 2026-10-31) — a profile carries plain-text `env`, and the current rule `**/data/daemon/state/agents.json*` cannot cover the new name → so the step that brings that file in (§5-2 3) adds a new-name line together with the tail `*` (including `.corrupt-*` copies — ADR-0136 decision 3 · ADR-0264 decision 7).

**Compatibility · downgrade** — the two editions use different files, so neither sees the other's agents — if you downgrade to the old build, the old build reads the old `agents.json`; when you upgrade again, the new edition reads its own file as it was (the list is not emptied). Not supporting downgrade means accepting that agents created in the new edition are invisible to the old build. The `cwd` key keeps its current spelling in the new file too.

**Launch materials port (`launch.rs` newly added)** — the daemon plugs it in (the real one = asset-library's `LibraryService` directly — §1-4). The default implementation `NoopLaunchKits` produces 「지금과 같은 결과」 (backend = the command variant, instructions = the priming file the control channel emits, as is) and **the argv is byte-for-byte the same** (`--system-prompt-snapshot off` is not attached either — see the translation table below).

- `trait LaunchKitSource { fn check(&self, profile) -> Result<LaunchPlan, LaunchBlocked>; fn prepare(&self, plan, id, epoch) -> Result<LaunchKit, PrepareError>; fn release(&self, id, epoch); }` · `enum PrepareError { Stale, Failed }` — `Stale` = the plan's original is gone or has changed ⇒ `LaunchRaced` · `Failed` ⇒ `SpawnFailed` (§5-2 4 「P4b · 8 단계가 지킬 것」 (c)). `check` does not edit the profile. **There is no launch-source argument and no profile-change field in this edition** (M16 · M18 · review #16 — the block is the same whoever launches, and there is no unattended default to fill in, so there is nowhere to use them · the next topic (asking at launch for an agent with no path — §8) adds them then). `prepare` takes no priming argument — the priming is in `builtin\`, so asset-library finds it by itself (M7), and if it is missing, the agent starts without priming (it does not block · M18).
- `LaunchPlan` = the agent backend word · the list of instruction sources (common + those with that backend's tag — §1-3 1) · skills (name · absolute folder) · the list of that backend's raw texts (model · effort are inside this too — there is no separate model field) · whether priming is included · **the notice list `notices`** (§1-3 6 — carried as is in the launch success answer · M19) · `real: bool` (Noop = false). **`real` is kept** (M18 said to decide after reading review #16 — the main session's judgment) — the purpose is to protect the regression of `NoopLaunchKits` (an argv byte-for-byte the same as today), and the two branches that, only for real materials, attach snapshot off and drop the control-channel fragment look at this (see the translation table below). There is no warning field (§1-3 6 · user 2026-10-18).
- `LaunchKit` = `LaunchPlan` + the volatile folder · the merged instruction file path · the skill list (absolute paths).
- `LaunchBlocked { reasons: Vec<BlockReason> }` — four kinds of reasons (in the order of §1-3 8 — `Missing` · `Broken` · `Conflict` · `LibraryUnavailable`). Skills codex cannot load · the command-line guard · command-line length overflow fall under `Broken` (P7AC · M8 — no new reason is created). Withdrawn = the old codex working-folder pre-check reason (P4-FINAL) · the old `NeedsCodexFilesAnswer` and `RelinkUnconfirmed` (waiting for reconnection confirmation — B11 · 2026-10-19). Each reason carries the item involved (library path · the presets that reference that item) and the cause (code + args · line) — the material for the block window (problem list) · the LLM answer (§1-10 · R49). `Conflict` carries the conflict lines of §1-3 5 one by one (what conflict · all items involved · the path it came by) — at launch, all the screen shows is this conflict content (and the other block reasons) (user 2026-10-18). `LibraryUnavailable` carries no value that tells roots apart (one root — G36).

**Backend settings-check port (name placeholder — M2 · M18 · review #2 · #3 · #7 · #8)** — the settings knowledge that differs per backend is gathered in **one trait of the agent `backend`** (CLAUDE.md 「백엔드 확장」 — if that knowledge leaks into asset-library, it is a violation). Implementations = claude · codex backend · caller = asset-library (`validate.rs` · `launch.rs`) · tests = a fake implementation. What it holds:
- ~~**Schema source**~~ — removed by ADR-0359 (child TRD §3-9a). Was: claude = a SchemaStore copy · codex = `rust-v<ver>/config-schema.json` per edition · when to fetch again (claude = conditionally the latest when the edition changes · codex = that edition's file — P10). It does not do the download itself — it only gives the address · edition, and asset-library does the fetching through the daemon fetch port (M17).
- ~~**Reading the CLI edition** (`--version` — tests use a fake).~~ Removed by ADR-0359 (child TRD §3-9a — the CLI version probe had no other caller).
- ~~**Raw text → JSON** — claude = the raw-text object as is · codex = assemble `key=value` into an object by the `-c` rules (dotted key = nested table · value = TOML, and if it does not read, the literal text).~~ Removed by ADR-0359 (child TRD §3-9a · B32 — its only use was the schema check; the codex `-c` line shape stays refused by `command_line_args`).
- **claude merge** — in attached order · objects recursively · lists concatenated with the same value kept once · otherwise the later one (§3 · T10). Since ADR-0359 · B32 it is **not a port member**: the rule stays as the claude backend's own function that `materialize` uses for the one `--settings` file (child TRD §3-9a).
- ~~**Unknown-key handling** — claude = not reported · codex = ⚠ (§1-4 `validate.rs`).~~ Removed by ADR-0359 (unknown keys are the CLI's).
- **codex skill format** — the file name is exactly `SKILL.md` · `---` front matter · YAML · `description` not empty · no BOM · `name` ≤ 64 (P7AC · research = `docs/research/skill-md-recognition-claude-codex-2026-10-20.md`) · skill name · conflict-key rule (§1-3 5 — M13). **This port does not read YAML** (main 2026-10-31 M63 — agent has no YAML dependency): the caller (asset-library `validate.rs`) passes the parsing function of `format.rs` in as a closure (front-matter text → a value deserialized into **a serde type that agent defines** (name · `description` · `metadata.short-description` — the same fields · types codex deserializes in typed form with `SkillFrontmatter`) or an error with a line — a value of the wrong type is also a parse failure, so it calls the ② repair exactly as codex does (if it were received as a general YAML tree, the repair would not be called and a skill codex loads would be blocked with ⛔) · the type lives in agent and `format.rs` only runs serde-saphyr — main 2026-10-31 M72 · replaces the old 「꼴은 구현 판단」), and the port (codex backend) does ① separation · ② repair (text-level transform — it is codex knowledge, so it lives in the codex backend) · ③ check below, calling that function wherever YAML interpretation is needed. **The decision must be the same as the rule by which codex reads skills** (0a 2026-10-31 — if they diverge, a skill codex loads is blocked or one it cannot load is let through · basis = codex `codex-rs/skills/src/parser.rs` @ rust-v0.156.1 — the same as 0.166.0 · research = `docs/research/markdown-frontmatter-hash-sync-crates-2026-10-31.md` §2): ① front-matter separation = codex `extract_frontmatter` copied by hand as is (`lines()` · first line `trim() == "---"` · up to the next `---` line · missing or 0 lines = no front matter · a whitespace-only block goes on to the YAML step · the BOM is not stripped — codex's `trim()` does not strip U+FEFF either and rejects it) ② **the repair is ported in full** — if the parsing function fails, run codex `repair_frontmatter_scalar_fields` once at the text level and call the same parsing function again on the repaired text (M63): among all top-level unquoted scalars, the values that contain a colon + space · the values that start with `[` `{` `@` `` ` `` and would be misread as flow syntax (unknown fields included — e.g. `tags: [next,@supabase/ssr]`) are wrapped in single quotes (an inner `'` is doubled · a trailing `#` comment is preserved · block scalars · already-quoted values are left as is) · if it still fails after the repair, the **original** error ③ the name · `description` check comes after deserialization, so it does not call the repair (name = whitespace collapsed, and if empty, the folder name · an error if `chars().count() > 64` · `description` is an error if empty · no length cap · a whitespace-only `metadata.short-description` counts as absent) · **golden tests** = upstream `codex-rs/skills/src/parser_tests.rs` L56–85 + the existing research's pinned tests + repair cases — the expected values are drawn from codex's behavior (they run on the asset-library side together with the real parsing function — it depends on agent, so it calls the codex port implementation as is · the backend-side tests are separated with a fake parsing function and measure only the order of separation · repair · retry — M63 · the edge-case differences between serde-saphyr and codex's serde_yaml 0.9.34 are untested — §6-2 · test shape = implementation judgment).
- **Command-line guard** — is the codex `-c` line safe to pass through `cmd.exe /c` (two modes — review #8) · command-line length budget (8191 characters) — if it is hit, setting 「오류」 (block ② · nothing is dropped silently (말없이 빼지 않는다) — M8). The new path args (merged instructions · plugin folder · merged settings) also pass the existing `path_is_cli_safe`-shaped guard (M8 · that function is `platform::shell::path_is_cli_safe` since step 5 — moved out of daemon `control/priming.rs`, which now calls it; the claude `materialize` and the claude port's `folder_entry_blocks` call it too). **Decision time (flow review G17)** — the final argv is only built at launch step 7 (the incarnation marker · control-channel args · volatile paths come to exist after `check`). So `check` decides by counting the varying pieces at their **upper-bound length** (so that it lines up with the same line as the problem list · tree ⛔) · the codex `materialize` (launch step 6) counts once more with the actual pieces (still with the upper bounds for what only the spawn knows — the resume id, the control URL) and, if the line overflows there, fails the spawn: `Err` → `PtyError::SpawnFailed`, not `Broken` (step 6 decision #7 — such an overflow is possible only when an input changed between `check` and `materialize`; no manager hook turns it into `LaunchBlocked`; amends this line's earlier "cuts it off before spawn with the same `Broken`"). The `Broken` comes only from `check`. The count includes the codex terminal's priming `-c developer_instructions=` too — on real materials a priming that does not fit or that the line cannot carry blocks the launch (U1 (a) of step 6); the Noop path keeps warning and dropping it (`backend/codex/mod.rs` `developer_instructions_override`) until real kits replace it (M8 「말없이 빼지 않는다」). The count is an upper bound for both quoting rules (each `"` and `\` counted twice, a fixed 262-unit program token, a 300-unit margin for the npm `codex.cmd` shim, whose expanded line cmd limits to 8191 again — `trd-codex-translation.md` §4-3).
- **What it loads** (flow review G17) — whether it loads instructions · skills for each backend · mode (now = the codex terminal loads only the priming of 「기본」 · everything else loads all — P4-FINAL). The resolution's notice ① (§1-3 6) · the conflict count (§1-3 5) · the targets of the codex skill-format check look at this — if that knowledge gets embedded in `resolve.rs`, it is a 「백엔드 확장」 violation.

**Profile-edit port (name placeholder — M18 · M5 · review #6)** — agent defines it and `AgentManager` implements it. The ways asset-library edits an agent's attachments (attach · detach · order — agent preset commands / path rewrite — move · rename · reconnect / path removal — the preset files deleted by a reference-removing delete · ADR-0339 — an agent attaches only presets (`AgentProfile.presets` · `default_preset`), so a reference-removing `SKILL.md` delete · rename · move only removes the skill lines of the preset files and does not call this port) are **this one port** — it does not mutate `ProfileRegistry` directly. **The path rewrite does not take an agent list** — it takes only the correspondence table (old → new), and the manager rewrites the whole current registry under one registry lock and returns the edited agents (it queues on that lock with the clone's original-read + insert — 「복제」 above · §1-4 flow ④ (c) · review 2026-10-31) · the rewritten list keeps only the first place of the same path (the reconnect rewrite too — main 2026-11-01 M102 · ADR-0339). **Path removal has the same shape** (`remove_preset_paths(paths)` — name placeholder · main 2026-11-01 M101 · ADR-0339) — it takes only the paths to remove (= among the paths that operation deleted, the preset files — for a folder, the presets among the deleted descendants · counted when the operation runs — §1-4 flow ④ (c) · M105), removes all attachments that are `same_path`-equal to one of the given paths from the whole current registry under one registry lock, and returns the changed agents in id order · the reason it does not take an agent list is the same as for the rewrite (ADR-0335 decision 6). It is added together with the port at step 4 (it is not a file-operation step). So registry changes and launch reservations share **one lock order** inside the manager (an attachment changed in between is caught by the launch preprocessing's compare-and-replace — (가) 2 below · only the working-folder change is not a compare-and-replace but takes that agent's reservation so as not to cross with a launch — `agent.setWorkspace` above · review 2026-10-31). Reads (reference · resolution) read `ProfileRegistry` directly (M5). Lock order = §3. **One read goes through this port** (flow review G17) — 「작업 폴더」 the lock reason (running · launch preparing · has conversed) does not stand on the registry alone (running · preparing are in the manager's session table · reservation table and not in `ProfileRegistry`). `agent.presets` receives it through this port's lock read, and the decision is one manager function the same as `agent.setWorkspace` (so the two places do not decide differently).

**Manager launch flow** — split into two chunks.

*(가) Launch preprocessing (`prelaunch`) — **right after** the `SpawnReservation` reservation and the 「이미 떠 있음」 check.* Both read only `profile.id`, so putting the preprocessing after them makes it **queue one per agent** (two launches of the same agent do not run together). It comes **before** `epoch_for_spawn` · working-folder canonicalize · session-id derivation. The early return of 「활성화 진행 중」 in `activate_profile` also stays in front as is.

0. **Insert an id that is not in the registry first** — wire `SpawnByCwd` (`connection_core.rs:1556-1596`) · the smoke bin (e.g. `bin/priming_smoke.rs:168-179`) call `spawn_agent` with a profile that is not in the registry. So the **「없을 때 넣는」 part** of `register_for_spawn` (capacity check · name assignment (`display_name` pinned) · upsert) is split out as `ensure_registered` and moved **before** the preprocessing. The 「있을 때 갱신하는」 part and the rest of the work stay where they are. So the preprocessing always edits an **item that is in the registry**. **Before** inserting, the check of 1 is run once with the caller's copy, and if it blocks, nothing is inserted (1 ② · main 2026-11-01).

1. `kits.check(명부에서 읽은 프로필)` — check with **the value re-read from the registry after the reservation**, not the copy the caller passed (call it after the registry lock is read and released — §3 lock order). **Even if it blocks, that answer is not given at once — it is the answer only if the comparison in 2 matches** (a block produced from an input that changed during the check is stale — an edited attachment would be cut off by a stale block · S25 step 4 P4a review 2026-11-01). **If it blocks, it does not spawn and returns a structured refusal to the caller as is — a launch is one request** (G15 · M3 · M19 · user 「패킷을 왜 두번보냄? 한번 보내고 실패하면 팝업 띄우는거고 아니면 실행하는거 아님?」 — the old screen pre-check command is withdrawn from the launch step, so there is no race between the pre-check and the real check): bus `agent.spawn` = an error answer (for the CLI, a non-zero exit code) with a reason code + args — add an args field to the command crate's `CommandError` · the wire activation also widens that error answer (shape = §1-7 · §1-8) · if a person made the launch, the webview asks the shell for the block window with that answer (§1-10 · G13) · the LLM · CLI receive the same reason as code + args. The success answer carries the notice list (M19 — the screen shows a notice popup · the CLI prints it as is · G28). **The carrying path (flow review G17)** — the manager's activation result carries the block and the notices upward: `PtyError` gets a block variant (`LaunchBlocked` as is) · the success side gets `notices` along (`AgentCommandHost::activate_profile` · `StartedAgent` · up to the place that builds wire `Spawned` — name · shape = implementation judgment) · the block is taken out of 「모든 실패 → `INTERNAL`」's folding of `wake_existing` (`create_and_start` is in ② below) · the wire uses the widened error answer instead of `e.to_string()`. **The outer error code — split by whether the verb creates the agent (review 2026-10-31)**: ① **a verb that launches an existing agent** (`wake_existing`) = `CONFLICT` (the four reasons go in the args field) — with `INTERNAL`, `command_delivery::retains_the_id` holds the request number, so a launch re-sent with the same number after the fix would be swallowed as `ALREADY_APPLIED` (if a new code is created, add it to that function's 「놓는 실패」). ② **a verb that creates the agent** (`create_and_start` = `agent.spawn --cwd` · `agent.new {presets}`) = **before creating** the agent, first runs the same check on the profile to be created (a copy with the defaults attached — outside the registry) (`agent.new` is the attachment validation — a preset that does not fit is not blocked but skipped · partial application — user 2026-10-31 G41) — if it blocks there, nothing is created and `CONFLICT` (releasing the number is correct). **An on-the-fly creation that arrives with an id not in the registry (wire `SpawnByCwd` · smoke bin — 0) is also checked before creating**: `ensure_registered` runs the same check with the caller's copy **before** inserting (without holding any manager lock · since it is a copy outside the registry, if the check asks for that id's `workspace_lock` it is `NotFound` — it is not read as a block reason · in the recheck of 1 after inserting, its own spawn reservation shows as `Preparing` — it is not an outer lock), and if it blocks, nothing is inserted — that answer carries no created id, so if it blocked after inserting, nobody could target that item (main 2026-11-01). If the recheck of 1 after inserting blocks or 2 cuts it off, what was inserted stays (partial success). A block that arises **after** creating (this recheck of 1 · the compare-and-replace failure of 2 · the step 7 recheck · a race in between — including losing the reservation to `agent.setWorkspace` · review 2026-10-31 R2) follows the current `INTERNAL` path that holds the number — if it were released, a retry with the same number would create again and leave a duplicate that cannot be deleted (ADR-0122 · `commands.rs:1241-1251`). The answer carries **the created agent id + the block reason** in its args field so that recovery targets that agent (not creating anew but fixing that agent and launching again — the same meaning as the current wording 「그 이름으로 다시 띄우라」). *Step 16 as landed (2026-11-05)* — ① `wake_existing`: block → `CONFLICT` + `args.reasons` · `LaunchRaced` → `CONFLICT` (retry). ② the pre-create check for `agent.spawn --cwd` is deferred to step 10a (TODO in `commands.rs` `create_and_start`); until then every failure after the create (block · race · store · spawn) answers `INTERNAL` + `args.agent_id` (+ `reasons` for a block). *Step 20 as landed (2026-11-09, `0badd007` — the deferral was absorbed into step 20, not 10a)* — ② holds for both create entrances through the one creation body (`commands.rs` `create_and_start`, ADR-0348): a block before the add = bus `CONFLICT` + `args.reasons` with nothing created (wire `SpawnByCwd`: `AgentEvent::Error` with `launch_blocked` and no created id; shell bus `agent.spawnInto`: `CONFLICT` + `args.reasons`); a failure after the add = `INTERNAL` + `args.agent_id` (+ `reasons` for a block) on bus `agent.spawn` and `agent.spawnInto` (wire: `AgentEvent::Error.created_agent_id`). The agent tree's ⛔ comes from real-time calculation (§1-3 · P8) — whether a blocked launch is also left on the old failure-display path (`failed_reason`) is undecided (§2-4).
   - **The settings check runs together when the launch reads the attached settings** (§1-4 `validate.rs` — in process · it does not run the CLI · it uses the schema it has without waiting for a download — P10-ADD) — a type · range error is block ②. **ADR-0359: no schema — a type · range error is the CLI's to handle at start (claude discards a bad `--settings` object silently); only the hand-over checks block.** The old pre-launch CLI check (`claude --settings … doctor` · `codex debug models`) and its pass record · cache are withdrawn (user 2026-10-18 — if problems arise, look again then). A runtime error (exit code + stderr) is not shown on the settings row — it stays only with the agent (the current failure-display path · raw exit code + stderr · user 2026-10-18 — withdraws 「그 설정 행 ⛔」 of 2026-10-17).
2. **Matching — compare-and-replace** — gives the answer of 1 (pass or block) **only if** `presets` · `default_preset` · `command` · `env` **have not changed** since the read in 1 (compared together under one registry lock). If they changed (a person's or an LLM's profile-edit-port edit cut in between), it re-reads and does 1 **once** more, and if it changed again, cuts it off with 「실행 준비 중 실행 입력(프리셋 · 명령 · 환경)이 계속 바뀌었다 — 다시 시도하라」 (outer code `CONFLICT` — the request number is released for the same reason as the block · flow review G17 · but if it comes after a create verb has created, it is `INTERNAL` + the created agent id as in 1 ②, review 2026-10-31). There is no field used at this spot, like an unattended default, in this edition (the next topic adds it — M16) — it only compares. The reason `command` · `env` are compared together = the spawn merge (`register_for_spawn`) is the one that carries those two fields from the caller's copy into the registry — so even if the comparison matches, if those two in the **copy** differ from the checked registry value, it is cut off with the same race (it does not overwrite-merge — today there is no operational path that changes those two, so if they differed the copy is stale, and trying again with a new copy works · implemented 2026-11-01). The working folder is not compared (the change takes that agent's launch reservation · and for the stale `cwd` in the copy, the merge keeps the registry value). An id just inserted is not merged (the inserted value is the copy — the on-the-fly creation's save is the insert alone). The old edition's 「해석된 백엔드로 변형 맞추기 · 백엔드가 저장된 변형과 다르면 `release_session_id`(옛 R33)」 is withdrawn — the backend is an agent value and does not change (user 2026-10-18). (working-folder matching · creating the Engram folder = §8)
3. **Re-read from the registry** — read **after** `epoch_for_spawn` and derive the later steps (canonicalize · session id · mode) from the registry's current value. Do not insert it between registration and the marker — `register_for_spawn` → `epoch_for_spawn` stays attached (finding 19 · in between, the previous incarnation's late record would sit in the emptied field — ADR-0226 · implemented 2026-11-01). In this edition there is no occasion for the preprocessing to release a session (the old backend change is withdrawn · the working folder is locked after conversation, so a change does not release the session — 「작업 폴더 `cwd`」 above). If such an occasion arises, a launch that released the session goes to `Fresh` even if the caller asked for `Resume` (the outcome is `Started` — the same outcome path as the current `resume_no_fallback`) — so that the first-submit latch (ADR-0226) only sees an id that does not diverge from the matched profile.

*(나) Startup — three are inserted into the current order as it is.*

4. `kits.prepare(plan, id, epoch)` — **right after** `epoch_for_spawn` · the registry re-read (3) · **before** `Fresh` session-id release · `provision`. asset-library writes the merged instructions into the volatile folder (if 「기본」 is attached, the priming of `builtin\` + the preset instructions in the order common · that backend's tag — §1-3 1) and emits the skill list. **The RAII guard dedicated to launch materials (`LaunchKitGuard`)** is armed **right before** `prepare` — it is independent of `ProvisionGuard` and calls `kits.release(id, epoch)` wherever it fails before the spawn is done. The reason for this spot = for the contract of `LaunchRaced` (it has never opened a process · control channel and has not touched the session id) to hold, `Stale` must come out before those things (main 2026-11-01 · S25 step 4 P4b-1 review).
5. Control channel `provision` (as it is now — MCP settings · token). With real materials the priming does not come from here (4 above — M7) · only `NoopLaunchKits` uses the `priming_file` this path emits (when to withdraw the old path = §2-4).
6. `backend::materialize(command, kit, cwd, fs)` (new dispatch) — does the on-disk layout that only the backend knows **only inside the volatile folder** (claude plugin-folder junction · codex JSON's `extraRoots` junction folder · the codex terminal has nothing to lay out — P4-FINAL). Writing is done only through the **layout port `LaunchFs`** (`write_file` · `link_dir` · `exists` · `is_link`) — operation = std + `platform::fs`, tests = an in-memory fake (measures the backend layout without a disk). The backend receives that port only as a port confined to the volatile folder — it blocks writes outside the volatile folder · names ending in a dot · space or containing `:` · past a link that already exists on disk (including case spelling · 8.3 spelling — measured: blocked, P4b-2 re-review 2026-11-01) (canonical = the `place_in_volatile` doc of `launch.rs` · P4b-2 review 2026-11-01). **Nothing is written to the working folder** (P4-FINAL). `unlink_dir` · `remove` are not put in `LaunchFs` (M18 — 「휘발 폴더 정리가 쓸 때만 둔다」) — deleting the volatile folder is the job of `kits.release` (asset-library), and so as not to follow the junction inside it and delete a library skill, it first detaches with platform `unlink_dir`. The result = `LaunchArtifacts` (the path · arg pieces that backend will use).
7. `build_command_spec(…, control, &artifacts)` · `open_spawn(…, &artifacts)` — both functions receive one more launch-materials descriptor (the same way as `ControlEndpoint`).
8. Reclaim = one reaper (kill goes through the reaper — §5-2 4 (c2) ④) · after the list notification · best effort — `kits.release(id, epoch)` deletes the volatile folder. At daemon boot it sweeps away the leftover `launch\`.

**(Withdrawn 2026-10-20) the old 「생성물 소유(codex 터미널)」** — the working-folder `AGENTS.override.md` · `.agents\skills\engram-*` junction · ownership marker · withdrawal rule · block on the existing-file pre-check were all withdrawn (P4-FINAL · user 2026-10-20 「프리셋은 베이스때문에 무조건 들어가야겠네. 그럼 안에 스킬이나 그런 지시서가 있을때 경고 띄우면 될듯」 — races between codex agents in the same folder · the copy block · no cleanup on delete (review #1) disappear along with them). The research for 「나중에 codex 가 지원하면」 = `docs/research/codex-instruction-channels-2026-10-20.md` (the per-agent profile `-p` path).

**Backend translation** (`backend/claude/launch.rs` · `backend/codex/launch.rs` newly added — the flag knowledge lives only in these two)

| Material | claude (both modes) | codex JSON (app-server) | codex terminal |
|---|---|---|---|
| Instructions | `--append-system-prompt-file <합친 파일>` (replaces today's priming spot) + **only with real materials (`real`)** `--system-prompt-snapshot off` (new text on resume too — measured certain · with the same text, no cache loss measured) | New conversation = `thread/start.developerInstructions` (measured certain). Resume = `thread/resume.developerInstructions` with the current full text on every resume (codex ≥ 0.155.0 — held for the next compaction in that process, M8 · step 6) and, only when the instructions hash differs from the last-applied value (profile field), `additionalContext` on the first turn (experimental API — T6); the `experimentalApi` opt-in is sent only on a spawn that carries `additionalContext` (step 6) | **Only the priming of 「기본」** — the current path as is (`-c developer_instructions=` — P4-FINAL) · the preset instructions (all common · tagged ones) are not loaded and the notice carries the names (§1-3 6 · G28) |
| Skills | the volatile folder `engram\skills\<이름>` = a directory junction to the library skill folder → `--plugin-dir <휘발>\engram` → name `engram:<스킬>` (T7 · loading a plugin folder is measured certain · following the junction is measured certain with a fake API — research 2026-10-20, no real model turn; the live turn = step 8 real CLI lane — §6-2 · step 5 D2) | the same junction folder with `skills/extraRoots/set` (per process, so it is sent on every process including a resume · junction followed — measured certain on codex 0.163.0, step 6 M1 · M4 · M9) — including the skills of the presets nested inside (M13) · if the format is one codex cannot load, block ② (P7AC) | not loaded — notice (P4-FINAL) |
| Raw settings | merge the claude raw texts of the attached presets (including 「기본」) in **attached order** into one volatile-folder `settings.json` → one `--settings` (§3 merge rule — values take the later one · lists merge · no Engram-priority rule · the merging is done by the backend settings-check port). **There is no hidden settings fragment** — the mail-server permission `allowedMcpServers` is visible in 「기본」's claude raw text (T10 · replaces ADR-0109 decision 9 · user 2026-10-18). With real materials (`real`), the control channel's fragment (`ControlEndpoint.settings_file`) is not loaded as `--settings` · with `NoopLaunchKits` it is loaded as it is now (argv byte-for-byte the same). The Engram MCP server registration arg `--mcp-config` is out of scope — it stays a launch arg (not a preset) | the preset's codex raw-text lines one by one with `-c` · after the passthrough · before our overrides · if the command-line guard · length budget is hit, block ② (M8) | the same — loaded (P4-FINAL · it is a CLI arg, so it works in the terminal too) · if hit, block ② (M8 — withdraws the old 「빼고 경고」) |
| Model | not loaded separately — the `model` (settings key) of the raw claude field goes into the merged `--settings` above · if absent, the CLI default (R35 · user 2026-10-18 — withdraws the old `--model <m>` translation) | not loaded separately — the `model=` line of the raw codex field goes into `-c` above (withdraws the old `-c model=<m>` translation) | the same |
| Size limit | — | — | (withdrawn — P4-FINAL: the terminal loads no instructions · skills, so there is no limit to raise · the old R38 (raising the size limit with `-c`) is also withdrawn from the PRD — M24-6) |
| Folder entries (ADR-0341) | each folder entry → `--add-dir <folder>` in plan order (`--add-dir` is variadic in `claude --help` 2.1.284 — its place is pinned by the step 5 argv golden so it never swallows a following value) + env `CLAUDE_CODE_ADDITIONAL_DIRECTORIES_CLAUDE_MD=1` when ≥ 1 folder entry (through `LaunchArtifacts` env · applied after the profile env, so it **overrides** a profile value — the stated exception to profile-wins, §1-5 「Plan and artifacts」) · nothing composed · the folder's settings not applied · the paths pass the `path_is_cli_safe` guard (M8 — a failing path = block ② · `preset.add` and the editor refuse such a path, so only a hand-edited file or a `preset.save` from an LLM or the CLI reaches this — §1-2) · no claude command-line length budget is claimed here (the code's 8191 limit is the codex backend's `CMD_LINE_LIMIT_UTF16` — `backend/codex/mod.rs`) | instructions = the folder's `AGENTS.override.md`, else `AGENTS.md` (the pick = backend settings-check port · the text is read by asset-library) joins the merged instructions in plan order → `developerInstructions` · resume `additionalContext` (the T6 hash covers it) · skills = the folder's `.agents/skills` and `.codex/skills` that exist, appended as extra roots to the same `skills/extraRoots/set` call (codex prunes dot-folders under a root, so the subfolders are passed, not the folder · no junction · nothing written) · `.codex/config.toml` not applied | nothing from the folder (no `--add-dir` — codex `--add-dir` is sandbox write only) · omitted instruction file and skills → notice ① (§1-3 6) |

**Folder entries and once-per-agent notices — agent side (ADR-0341)**
- **Plan and artifacts** — `LaunchPlan` gains the folder list (absolute paths · expansion order · deduplicated — §1-3) and, for codex JSON, a list of extra skill roots (field names = implementation judgment). The codex folder instruction file joins `instructions` as one more `SourceRef` at its place (right after that preset's own instruction lines — §1-3 1); the extra skill roots stay apart from `skills` — they are roots, not named skills, so they enter neither the conflict check nor the junction folder. `LaunchArtifacts` gains backend-produced env pairs (claude's `CLAUDE_CODE_ADDITIONAL_DIRECTORIES_CLAUDE_MD`), which `build_spec` applies after the profile env. **Exception to the local env convention** — in `backend/claude/mod.rs` the profile wins: `NO_AUTOUPDATE_ENV` and `MAX_THINKING_TOKENS` are skipped when the profile already sets the key. `CLAUDE_CODE_ADDITIONAL_DIRECTORIES_CLAUDE_MD=1` is the stated exception: it overrides a profile value (e.g. a profile `0`) because ADR-0341 decision 2 requires the variable whenever a folder entry is attached — without it claude silently drops the folder's `CLAUDE.md` and rules. The manager and the transport stay backend-agnostic — only `backend/claude` and `backend/codex` read these fields (ADR-0004 · ADR-0341 impact).
- **Folder form of the string contracts** (review round 1) — `SourceRef.path` and `BlockReason::Missing.item` are documented as library paths (`launch.rs` — 「라이브러리 참조(루트부터의 `/` 경로 또는 기본 제공 `engram:/…`)」 · 「`item` = 걸린 항목의 라이브러리 경로」). A folder entry uses them in an explicit third form = **`folder:` + the normalized absolute folder path** (§1-2 — e.g. `folder:D:\rules\company`); for the codex folder instruction `SourceRef`, `path` = that `folder:` string and `file` = the chosen `AGENTS.override.md` / `AGENTS.md`. Step 16 extends both type docs with that form. What it means elsewhere: **R4 same-path dedup** compares `folder:` strings by the `path_key` of the folder part (§1-3 2) and never matches a `folder:` string against a library path — a library instruction that is also a folder entry's `AGENTS.md` (a folder entry pointing inside the library) loads twice for codex JSON (known overlap — §6-3) · **prepare's `Stale` obligation** (§5-2 4 (c)) covers the folder instruction file — if the file `check` chose (or the folder) is gone when `prepare` reads it, `prepare` returns `Stale` (⇒ `LaunchRaced`), never `Failed` · **R28 reveal** never receives a `folder:` item — a folder line's double-click reveals the preset that holds the entry (from `via` · PRD R31 addendum), and folder lines have no magnifier (R22).
- **Backend settings-check port — folder load set** (method name placeholder) — given a folder listing from asset-library `folder.rs` (§1-4) and the front-matter parse closure, the backend returns what it loads from that folder in a mode: claude = the predicted native set (`CLAUDE.md` · `.claude/CLAUDE.md` · `CLAUDE.local.md` · `.claude/rules/**/*.md` without `paths:` · `.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md` without `paths:` · `.claude/agents/**/*.md` whose front matter has a non-empty string `name` and `description` (claude also drops a `name` starting with `-` or holding `:` after NFKC) · `.claude/commands/**/*.md` — agents and commands are walked recursively · the fixed folder and file names compare by the OS path rule · the `.md` suffix of walked files compares case-sensitively, as claude's walker does — claude 2.1.284 as landed in step 5). **Only clear cases are predicted** (main 2026-11-09) — a file whose answer depends on claude's finer rules is returned as `unsure` with a reason instead of being predicted: an unclear `paths` value (anything but a plain list of globs) or any `paths` on a skill · a hook key in an agent's front matter · `---` inside a front matter · a blank or comment-only body · a command folder holding a `skill.md` (claude keeps only that file) · an unentered link where claude loads files · an unreadable file or a front matter the caller's parser refuses. The front-matter types stay in the neutral port file (`backend/settings_check.rs` — user 2026-11-09) · codex JSON = the instruction file (`AGENTS.override.md` > `AGENTS.md`) + skill roots (`.agents/skills` · `.codex/skills`) + the skills under those roots, found by **codex's `skills/extraRoots/set` walk rules** (research table 「Findings — codex」, settled from the codex source at `rust-v0.163.0` in step 6: walked from the root itself, a `SKILL.md` directly in the root included · six folder levels below the root, one more not · dot-folders pruned below the root · exactly `SKILL.md`; links — codex **follows** a link under a root (step 6 M1, measured), while the listing does not enter links (§1-4), so a link under a root — or a root, `.agents` or `.codex` that is a link — is returned `unsure` (`Link`) rather than predicted), each skill run through the existing **codex skill-format check** (P7AC · M63 — the same front-matter closure) and returned with a **mark**, not a block (Engram passes the root, not the skill, so a malformed folder skill is codex's to skip) · codex terminal = nothing (the codex JSON set becomes the omission notice). **Shapes as landed (step 6)** — `FolderLoadKind` gains `SkillRoot` (a root that holds at least one listed entry); `FolderLoadSet` gains `malformed` (a skill that fails the format check — a mark, distinct from `unsure`), `skill_names` (the name codex gives each loaded skill — front-matter `name`, else the folder name — one row per `Skill` row, in order; the input of the same-name notice) and `omitted` (codex terminal: the rows the codex JSON set loads, for the omission notice); the claude half leaves the three lists empty. The codex launch and the preview (§1-7) call the same method: the instruction file the preview names is the one carried, and the skill list is a prediction of codex's own root walk under those rules (codex walks the roots itself at launch); for claude only the preview uses it — claude reads the folder itself. **Loading conditions — the preview is default-only** (review round 2) — the claude set assumes claude's default loading conditions. Engram's own argv passes no `--setting-sources` · `--bare` (grep 0 in `crates/`), but the agent's passthrough `command.extra_args` (§1-5 `command` · appended to the claude argv in `backend/claude/mod.rs`) can carry `--setting-sources user`, `--bare` or `--safe-mode`, and each makes claude load less from an added folder (setting sources gate skills and `CLAUDE.local.md`; `--bare` skips agents and commands; `--safe-mode` turns off folder skills — research). The preview does **not** read the agent's `extra_args` — it is also called for a folder with no agent (`preset.previewFolder {folder, backend?, mode?}`) — so with such a passthrough flag claude can load less than the preview shows (risk row — §6-3).
- **Notices once per agent** (ADR-0341 decision 5 · amends ADR-0337 decision 3) — the profile gains a notice fingerprint (name = implementation judgment · `None` = nothing recorded) = a canonical identity string over the **omission identity** of the launch notices only (no hash — a std hash is not stable across Rust releases; stored as is, compared for equality — step 16 as landed). `Notice` = `Reason { code, args, line }` (`launch.rs`); keying on the whole `Reason` would re-show the notice after an unrelated edit (a `line` shift, a diagnostic or wording arg), so the identity per notice = `code` + the names and paths it lists, with `args` serialized in a canonical key order (object keys sorted · name and path lists sorted) and `line` left out; the set = those identities sorted, deduplicated and joined by a newline (`launch::notice_fingerprint`) — the empty set is the empty string, so a stored `Some("")` = an empty set was shown. To keep the comparison opaque, notice `args` carry only what identifies the notice (the omitted names and paths) — wording comes from the code, and a diagnostic goes to the log, not into `args` (fixed with the notice codes by the steps that produce them — 6 · 7; step 16 ships no producer and a generic wording — §2-4 8). In `prelaunch`, once the check passes, the manager logs every notice as today (`manager/prelaunch.rs`) and compares the plan's fingerprint with the stored one: equal → the success reply carries an empty notice list · different → the full list. **When it records** (review round 1 · main ruling 2026-11-04) — only when a success reply carrying the notices is delivered to a caller (screen · LLM · CLI). As landed in step 16 (2026-11-05 — this replaces the review-round-1/2 wording keyed on `SpawnOutcome::Started` / `RestoreOutcome::Started | Resumed`, which a moot activation also reports): recording is opt-in per caller (`NoticeDelivery::Delivered` — today only the bus `agent.spawn`; `spawn_agent_reporting` returns notices but never records) and happens with the manager's own registry mutate only when that caller's activation actually started a process — a fresh spawn or a caller resume — as a compare-and-set against the baseline `prelaunch` compared (carried out of the launch in `LaunchNotices`; it lands only while that incarnation is current). WS activations do not record yet — the webview does not show notices (step 11 switches them). A moot activation (already running) ran no `prelaunch`, carries no notices and records nothing. **Boot restore records nothing** — `restore_all` → `restore_one` → `spawn_fresh_settled` / `resume_no_fallback` with `Entry::Internal` → … → `prelaunch` delivers no reply to anyone, so the first caller launch after it still shows the set. **A failed launch records nothing** — a block, `LaunchRaced`, a spawn error, and a post-start failure (`EarlyVerdict::LinkFailed` → `PtyError::SpawnFailed`, or death before the verdict) all answer with an error that carries no notices, so the next successful launch shows them. The reply leaving the manager is the delivery point; a reply lost in transit afterwards is not detected (accepted). An empty set is recorded too (X → none → X shows X again — the set changed). The comparison is opaque — the manager does not interpret notices. `merge_preserving_live` keeps the field (author = the launch) · `agent.duplicate` does not copy it. `agent.presets` notices (the slot's dim line — R31) are a view and are not filtered.
