**The JSON-mode model · effort picker (T8 — handed over to the next topic (the agent system) 2026-10-20 · G10 — that topic inherits this section's spec · reverses the old 「마지막 단계 · 2판 · 범위 안」 · it is not built in S25)** — the list is **only that agent backend's models** (the backend is fixed per agent — a claude agent = the claude list · a codex agent = the codex list · the two backends are not mixed in one list · B16). It does not block and **does not lock** (3rd delta · user 2026-10-18 「모델선택칸 잠금은 하지마. 그냥 유저가 알아서 하라고 그러지.」 — withdraws the old 「설정이 모델을 고정하면 잠김 · 설정이 고정」): the value the CLI started with (CLI 가 시작한 값) (if the raw field has a model, the CLI starts with it · if not, the CLI default — Engram does not read the model from the raw field and uses the value the CLI reports) is pre-selected, and the picker changes it in that session (matching it with the raw field is up to the user). Wherever the first message comes from (screen · mail · bus), it goes out with the value selected at that time. The selected value belongs to that session and is not saved (R35). **Both model and effort reach the session by a backend request before the first user message** — the path is decided by the backend.
- claude: change the model · effort with a control request of stream-json (the same request shape as the SDK's change). The list is the `initialize` response (memo 27 measured).
- codex: the model · effort override field of the first `turn/start`. The list is `model/list`.
- **Acceptance gate** (the first commit of that topic — old §5 step 14): confirm with the real CLI, on **each** JSON backend (claude · codex), that both model and effort change before the first message (the next turn shows it). Also see whether the CLI reports the 「CLI 가 시작한 값」 to pre-select (including the case where a model is put in the raw field) (unmeasured).
- **Stop condition**: if that request does not work on either backend, **stop** the implementation of that part and **go back to the user** (fix R35 or find another supported path). Silently turning the field off is not an acceptable substitute. The ownership of the pending-input registry · the first-submit latch is left untouched.

`DISABLE_AUTOUPDATER` and `allowedMcpServers:[{serverName:"engram"}]` are carried as the claude raw text (settings `env` · top-level keys) of the built-in preset 「기본」 (R32). `DISABLE_AUTOUPDATER` is **also hard-coded** in the claude backend since `3036e3d8` (2026-10-30 — `backend/claude/mod.rs` `NO_AUTOUPDATE_ENV` — on every claude spawn, skipped when the profile sets the key, and on the version probe); it **stays hard-coded on both paths (Noop and real) until step 8 measures whether settings `env` reaches the updater** (§6-1 · step 5 D1), so a real launch carries the value twice until then. `allowedMcpServers` is moved here from the fragment file the control channel now writes for every spawn (`control/mcp_config.rs` · ADR-0109 decision 9) (T10). So if 「기본」 is detached, the Engram message · control channel may also drop out (when the user's global settings block it with `allowedMcpServers: []`) — 「Engram 메시지 · 제어 통로」 is added to the detach confirmation wording (`DetachDefaultConfirm` · R32).

**Place and ownership of the command vocabulary (ADR-0155 decision 1 · ADR-0251 precedent)** — `library.*` · `preset.*` · the agent preset commands are **declared in the agent crate's `declare_commands!` block** (catalog 8 → 9). The owner of the vocabulary (name · args · answer row type) is the agent crate, and asset-library only **implements** that vocabulary — through the port `LibraryCommandHost` (a typed method per command — M18 · the real one = `LibraryService`) · the daemon plugs it in with `blocking_handler`. The `agent.*` preset commands go through the same port (the attachment validation needs the library · the edit goes through the profile-edit port). **All operations are bus commands** (M21 — the CLI and the screen use the same command) · exceptions = emptying the trash · permanent delete (shell only — M6 · §1-4 `store.rs`) · local OS helpers (folder picker · open · reveal — screen only · not commands). The daemon-side library features (commands · CLI) are always on — even if the screen package is turned off, the LLM uses them as is (G22-RESOLVED · user 2026-10-29 「데몬은 디폴트로 제공하는거고 UI는 그냥 패키지? 형식으로 온오프」). Rejected = the daemon's own block + table synthesis (ADR-0251 rejected it in `usage.*` for the same reason). **Targeting an agent (flow review G17)** — the `agent` argument is resolved by the table assembly part (`make_table`) with the same resolver as `agent.spawn` (`commands.rs`'s `resolve`) and passed on as an id — the `LibraryCommandHost` method takes only an id (if asset-library read the registry and resolved the name separately, the two rules would diverge).

### 1-6. platform — five OS-layer operations

| Function (placeholder) | Windows | Elsewhere | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|
| `fs::link_dir(target, link)` · `fs::unlink_dir(link)` | Directory junction (no administrator rights needed) · creation = `junction::create` (even on failure, the leftover empty folder is not deleted (it could delete a folder someone else made — it is cleared along with the volatile folder) — implemented 2026-11-01 · S25 step 2 review) · `unlink_dir` refuses anything that is not a junction — it reads the reparse tag from one handle opened on the reparse point itself (delete access · sharing read-only) (std does not give out the tag) to confirm it is a junction, and deletes with that handle (the POSIX-style delete flag · if 1 · 50 · 87, it falls back to the old form — only the link is deleted and the target stays · `std::fs::remove_dir`, which finds the path again and deletes, could delete a spot swapped for something else between the check and the delete, so it is not used — implemented 2026-11-01 · `junction::delete` leaves an empty folder and `junction::exists` gives `false` on a broken junction, so it cannot be used as a guard — 0a 2026-10-31) · where used = the claude plugin folder inside the volatile folder · the codex JSON `extraRoots` junction (backend `materialize`) · detaching before deleting the volatile folder (asset-library `release` — §1-5 6) · the old codex terminal working-folder junction is withdrawn (P4-FINAL) | Symbolic link (std) | `junction` 2.1.0 — it uses `windows-sys` `>=0.59, <=0.61`, and those editions (0.59.0 · 0.60.2 · 0.61.2) are already in `Cargo.lock`, so new copies do not grow (likely — the edition the resolver picks · `scopeguard` is confirmed with `cargo tree` at step 2) · unlinking is done directly by platform with `windows`, not by this crate nor std — it confirms and deletes with one handle (research = `docs/research/junction-recycle-bin-atomic-write-2026-10-31.md` · implemented 2026-11-01) |
| `fs::same_path(a, b)` | Case-insensitive comparison — two `path_key` are equal (line rule below · main 2026-10-31 M60) | Compared as is (case-sensitive — macOS's case folding · NFC/NFD is a known gap · `unicode-normalization` is not in the lock · 0a 2026-10-31) | std · OS upper-case mapping (line below) |
| `fs::path_key(path)` (review 2026-10-31 — this line grew, so the header is five · the old 「셋」 was not fixed when the OS trash line was revived on 2026-10-20) | A case-folded key — the same rule as `same_path` (`same_path(a, b)` = the two keys are equal) · **the folding rule** (main 2026-10-31 M60 — accepts the BLOCK of the 0a adversarial review) = per UTF-16 code unit · upper-case only BMP units (surrogates are not touched) · OS upper-case mapping (`RtlUpcaseUnicodeChar` or `LCMapStringEx` invariant upper-case — which one is chosen in the step 2 pilot and measured against NTFS · §6-2) — Rust `char::to_uppercase` is not used (it merges supplementary-plane names that NTFS tells apart — e.g. U+10428 · U+10400) · folders with per-folder case sensitivity (WSL `fsutil setCaseSensitiveInfo`) turned on are out of support (§6-3) · where used = tables that use a path as the key (the `items` · `used_by` of `Catalog` — §1-4 `catalog.rs`) · `same_path` is pairwise comparison so it cannot be used for a path-key table (using the literal text as the key makes references differing only in case look like 「찾을 수 없음」 on Windows) · places that compare a reference by path (the agent-side reverse lookup of `refs.rs` · the matching attachments in the path rewrite) compare with `same_path` | As is | OS upper-case mapping (the chosen API's feature goes into platform's `windows` union — step 2) · std |
| `fs::file_identity(path)` | **128-bit `FileIdInfo` first** (`GetFileInformationByHandleEx` — volume serial number + 128-bit file id) · on a volume where that does not work, it falls back to the volume serial number + 64-bit file index (`GetFileInformationByHandle`) — a 64-bit one may not be unique on ReFS (main 2026-10-31 M60 · MS `BY_HANDLE_FILE_INFORMATION` docs) — the file identity of the scan tag (§1-4 `catalog.rs`) | (device number, inode) (std `MetadataExt`) | platform calls it directly (one dependency of the `windows` 0.58 union — both functions are behind the feature `Win32_Storage_FileSystem`, so that is added · M16 · review #17 · the `windows` list of CLAUDE.md 「의존성」 is fixed at implementation) or file-id 0.2.3 (both 128 and 64 — a new crate) — chosen in the step 2 pilot (0a 2026-10-31 · `winapi-util` 0.1.11 is already locked but is 64-bit only, so it fits only the fallback path — in that case see whether to add it to the hand list of gate 4g · research = `docs/research/library-scan-file-identity-path-key-crates-2026-10-31.md`) · std (elsewhere) |
| Sending to the OS trash (name is a placeholder — G12 · 2nd edition) | **Hand over to the trash of the daemon PC and let the OS decide** (user 2026-10-31 G47 「그냥 휴지통으로 보내고 삭제되던지 말던지는 다 os에서 알아서 하라고그래」 — whether it goes into the trash or cannot go in and is deleted as is, Windows behaves as it does · returns to the original rule of user 2026-10-20 G12) — the means of calling = **a direct `IFileOperation` call is the default** (re-measured against the `trash` crate at implementation — basis column · G47 · a new STA thread per call — 「스레드」 below · main 2026-10-31 M64 · M69 · each item gets its own `PerformOperations` — 「항목마다」 below · M66 · whether the flags `FOF_NO_UI` · `FOFX_RECYCLEONDELETE` · `FOFX_EARLYFAILURE` · `FOF_ALLOWUNDO` are needed is the 9b pilot · main 2026-10-31 M57 · `FOF_WANTNUKEWARNING` is not used — per the MS docs it can override `FOF_NOCONFIRMATION` in some cases and show a confirmation window, and if a window appears 9b fails · M70 · G47). **There is no guard · no no-trash decision · no separate permanent-delete step** (G47 — withdraws the `TSF_DELETE_RECYCLE_IF_POSSIBLE` guard (main 2026-10-31 M59) of the old progress sink `PreDeleteItem` · the 「휴지통 없음 확정」 code table · the platform permanent-delete function called after `no_bin_confirmed` (M70) · we do not decide whether to delete). **Result = one portable result per item + the original native code (for diagnostics only)** (main 2026-10-31 M62 — the interpretation is kept only inside platform · the result fields were folded by user 2026-10-31 G47 · replaces the old `recycled` · `no_bin_confirmed` · `removed_unconfirmed` · `kept(reason)`) — `removed` (that item is gone after the call — it does not say whether it went into the trash or the OS deleted it as is) · `kept` (that item is as it was after the call — the OS refused: an open file · permissions · anything · original code = that item's `PerformOperations` HRESULT or the `hrDelete` of the progress sink `PostDeleteItem` — the sink is used not as a guard but only to collect the diagnostic code) · `not_found` (a path that was already absent before the call — if absent after the call, `removed` · M71 · M85) (`not_attempted` is withdrawn — each item has its own `PerformOperations`, so there is no item for `FOFX_EARLYFAILURE` to skip · main 2026-10-31 — M85) — the split = the 「결과 가름」 table below. The caller (asset-library's emptying · permanent delete) sees only this result — `removed` = emptied · `kept` = left in `.trash` with the item result `{code, args}` · `not_found` = the item result `NOT_FOUND` · resend a whole-bundle failure item by item (it does not know the HRESULT — M62). If sending the bundle folder whole fails, the caller resends the items remaining in that bundle one by one and gets the result per item — one open file blocks only its own item · review 2026-10-31 R6 · `FOFX_EARLYFAILURE` does not roll back items it has already finished | macOS = trash (later — 「플랫폼 중립」 · not researched) | **`IFileOperation` directly — the default · re-measured against the `trash` crate at implementation** (user 2026-10-31 G47 — 0a 2026-10-31 · of the reasons main M57 rejected it, 「항목별 HRESULT 를 돌려주지 않아 G45 의 가름이 서지 않는다」 drops out since the split is gone, while the need for a way to report failure per item remains (the `trash` crate has no per-item result) · and the worries that it does not use `FOFX_RECYCLEONDELETE` and pulls in a third `windows` copy (0.62) are unchanged — §1-12 · research = `docs/research/junction-recycle-bin-atomic-write-2026-10-31.md` · precedent = the Files app · Go `mono0x/trash`) · add `Win32_UI_Shell` and the COM implementation feature to the platform `windows` 0.58 union's one dependency (the exact list = the 9b pilot) |

- **Gate** — add `junction` to the hand list of the OS crate dependency gate (`/qa` 4g · CI `platform gate 5`). New per-OS code · tests are put inside platform, and if a test file **outside** platform needs to use OS `cfg`, the list of the OS cfg file list gate (`/qa` 4f · CI `platform gate 4`) is also fixed in the same commit (preferably call a platform helper and use it without `cfg`).
- **History of the OS trash function (withdrawn 2026-10-19 → revived as the emptying job 2026-10-20 → how it is built = `IFileOperation` directly 2026-10-31)** — on 2026-10-19 the old first line (the `fs::move_to_trash` for deletion — `IFileOperation` · `FOFX_RECYCLEONDELETE` · `FOF_ALLOWUNDO` · `FOF_NO_UI` of a dedicated STA worker thread) was withdrawn: deletion is a same-root rename into `.trash` inside the library root, so no OS-layer operation is needed (B8 · user 2026-10-19 「ㅇㅇ 우리 trash 폴더로해」 · reason for rejection = §7 20) — **the deletion side is still the same now**. On 2026-10-20 one OS trash function was revived for a person's emptying · permanent-delete job (G12 · the last line of the table above — user 「휴지통 비우면 윈도우 휴지통으로 보내고 안되면 그냥 삭제되게」 · the rejection reason for the deletion side (silent permanent delete) does not apply here — even if it falls through to a permanent delete, that is the same as deleting at once). On 2026-10-31 the 0a research chose a direct `IFileOperation` call as how to build that function (main M57 · user 2026-10-19 「구현전에 외부 라이브러리 쓸수있는거 다 조사하고 들어가」) — the `trash` crate cannot return per-item results, pulls in a third `windows` copy, and the per-thread STA `CoInitializeEx` panics in `RPC_E_CHANGED_MODE` (0a confirmed the old 「미측」 remark from the source · §1-12). So it reverses the old 「`windows` feature 도 더하지 않는다」 and adds `Win32_UI_Shell` (and the COM implementation feature) at 9b. On the afternoon of 2026-10-31 user G47 withdrew the reason split · guard (hand over and let the OS decide), so the first reason of M57 (the G45 split) dropped out — the default is still a direct `IFileOperation` · re-measured against the `trash` crate at implementation (the per-item failure report · the third `windows` copy worry are unchanged).
- **Thread (스레드) (main 2026-10-31 M64 · M69)** — the OS trash function starts **one new thread per call** with `std::thread::Builder::spawn` (a spawn failure arrives as `Result`, not a panic — `std::thread::spawn` panics if the OS cannot create a thread · M69), and inside it does `CoInitializeEx` (STA) → that call's items → `CoUninitialize`, while the caller waits with a join — there is no resident worker thread · its lifetime · cleanup at shutdown (replaces the resident shape of the old 「전용 STA 스레드」). The caller = asset-library's emptying, while holding the trash `Mutex` per bundle (§3 「잠금」), so there is only one call at a time, and that thread knows and takes none of asset-library's locks. A thread start failure · a COM initialization failure (independent of the calling thread's COM mode — the new thread opens its own STA · the `RPC_E_CHANGED_MODE` panic of the `trash` crate is not here) · an HRESULT failure of a COM call before the engine is opened are folded **as a value** — all items of that call (those that existed before the call) get the single value `kept` (diagnostic = the original code · M71 · the reason field was withdrawn by G47 · an item that did not exist before the call is still `not_found` — M86) · a failure of a single item follows the 「결과 가름」 table. No `unwrap` · `expect` is placed inside the thread closure or the `extern "system"` callback of the progress sink (a panic raised in a callback cannot cross the FFI boundary and aborts on the spot). **A panic that happens anyway follows ADR-0292** — log the reason and the daemon exits (it is not contained · the global hook ends it on the spot, so a join never receives `Err`). No new containment spot is created — containment is only the three agent pumps and the input writer (ADR-0293), and adding one needs a user decision that amends ADR-0293 (main 2026-10-31 M69 · replaces the old 「스레드 안 panic(join 이 `Err`) 도 `kept(unknown)` — 데몬으로 panic 을 올리지 않는다」). The risk that a third-party shell copy hook · an unresponsive network volume hangs that thread inside the process or kills the process = §6-3 · measured in the 9b pilot (§6-2) — if it shows up, move to a separate helper process with a time limit (it can be killed · timing out = those items `kept`) · it is not brought in from the start.
- **Per item (항목마다) (main 2026-10-31 M66 · M71 · M85)** — a call that sends several items (`items` emptying · resending item by item after a whole-bundle failure) calls **its own `PerformOperations` per item** — `FOFX_EARLYFAILURE` skips the rest of the same `PerformOperations` if one item fails, so they are not bundled into one call. The function must return exactly one result per received item (it does not drop any) — it checks whether each received item exists **before** the thread · COM initialization (main 2026-10-31 — M86) · a path that was already absent before the call = that item's `not_found` (asset-library's item result `NOT_FOUND` · even if initialization fails — M86) · on an initialization failure (thread start · COM — before the engine is opened), all the remaining (existing) items get the single value `kept` · if absent after the call (including one that vanished midway), `removed` (main 2026-10-31 — M85 · fixes the old 「호출 앞 · 도중에 사라진 경로 = `not_found`」 (M71)) · there is no `not_attempted` — each item has its own `PerformOperations`, so `FOFX_EARLYFAILURE` has no item to skip (M85 — withdraws the 「멈춘 뒤 엔진에 닿지 못한 항목」 field of the old M66 · M71).
- **Result split (결과 가름) (main 2026-10-31 M62 · M71 · user 2026-10-31 G47 — withdraws the old reason split · guard cancellation · 「휴지통 없음 확정」 line (M59 · M70) · the user's open U1 · U2 vanish with G47 — §6-5 8 · 9)** — we do not decide whether to delete (the OS decides) · an item that is gone after sending is not called left behind (`kept`), and an item that is left is not called emptied (the decision = if absent before the call, `not_found` · if absent after the call, `removed` · if present, `kept` — M71 · M85). The only thing the policy field sees is the platform result (the original code is a diagnostic):

| What the engine saw | platform result | asset-library emptying · permanent delete |
|---|---|---|
| That item is gone after the call (whether it went into the trash or the OS deleted it as is — G47 · including one that vanished during the call — M85 · merges the old `recycled` · `no_bin_confirmed` · `removed_unconfirmed` into one) | `removed` | Emptied |
| That item is as it was after the call — the OS refused (an in-use `0x80070020` kind · an access-denied `0x80070005` kind · anything else — not split by code) · initialization failure (thread start · COM — all items that existed before that call, one value · M69 · M71 · M86) | `kept` + original code | Left in `.trash` + item result (user 2026-10-31 G47) |
| That path was already absent before the call (it is looked at before the call — before the thread · COM initialization, so `not_found` even if initialization fails · main 2026-10-31 — M86 · one that disappeared after the call is `removed` above · main 2026-10-31 — M85 · the old `not_attempted` line is withdrawn — each item has its own call, so there is no such item) | `not_found` (main 2026-10-31 M71) | That item result `NOT_FOUND` (not `kept`) |

### 1-7. Command surface — the screen buttons and the LLM call the same thing

**All operations are bus commands** (M21 — the CLI and the screen use the same command) · exceptions = emptying the trash · permanent delete (shell only — the last row of the daemon table below · M6) · local OS helpers (folder picker · open · reveal — screen only · not commands · §1-9). The server step verifies this table with `engram` CLI (G20 — it stands without a screen).

**Webview → daemon round trip (a prerequisite of the client step — G20)** — the webview already sends wire commands with `forward_daemon_command` and matches the answer by request_id. The answer `CommandReply` of the bus envelope (`Command{envelope}`) has its correlation key inside the envelope, so the webview `handleEvent` cannot yet match it (that gap in the shell `daemon_client/inbound.rs` header). Add that branch and open `agentClient.callCommand(name, args)`. The daemon answers the names in its own table on the spot (`locals.claim` of `command_delivery::deliver`).

**Daemon commands (declared in the agent block · body = `LibraryService` · edition = §5)**

| Name | What it does | Answer / remarks |
|---|---|---|
| `library.status` | Root (one — G36) · state `unset` · `ok` · `missing` · **root generation** (the value edit commands carry — M14 · 「공통 인자 · 답 꼴」 below) · daemon identity · the suggested path for creating new | R8 · R9 · shape = §1-4 「루트 · 기본 경로」 |
| `library.setRoot {path, create?}` | Change the root (`create` = create if missing) · the root generation goes up (M14) · agent attachments · preset lines find the same path in the new root (Δf) | R9 as is (G37-NOTE) — screen (menu 「라이브러리 폴더 바꾸기…」 next to the path 「…」 — G2) · the LLM the same command (there is no settings window in the first edition — T5). **There are only two root commands: `library.status` · `library.setRoot`** (G36 · R9 — the root add · remove · rename commands from the multi-root days are withdrawn) |
| `library.folders` | The whole folder tree — folders + skill folders (skill display — a skill icon in the tree · user 2026-10-17) | Tree area |
| `library.refresh {folder?}` | Rescan that folder (including below) · omitted = all (before the first scan, even if a folder is given it starts the full first scan or waits for it, and the answer is that folder's share — flow ① 「잠금」 ⑤ · review 2026-10-31 R4) — tree right-click 「새로 고침」 · recommends that the agent call it (2026-10-16 · recommendation — 2026-10-17) · it checks the settings it re-read (there is no separate `library.validate` · 「모두 검사」 — 2026-10-18) | The number of changed items + the problems of the files re-read this time — 「오류 — <경로> · <줄> · <이유>」 (broken kind files · unknown `kind` · ~~type · range errors of the settings schema~~ (dropped — ADR-0359) — ⛔) · 「경고 — <경로> · <키> · <이유>」 (codex unknown key — ⚠ · does not block) · 「kind 없음 — 목록에 안 보임 — <경로>」 (the re-read `.yaml` has no `kind` — hidden silently from the usual list but reported to the caller) · 「찾을 수 없음 — <프리셋 경로> → <없는 경로>」 — as code + args (R49 · user 2026-10-17) |

The agent slot's `+` search turns on `builtins` (Δa — independent of 「기본 제공 보기」 on/off) · the library search (`query`) carries the built-in items along with the 「기본 제공」 mark only when 「기본 제공 보기」 of that number is on (G2 · main session decision 2026-10-20 — matched with 「숨김」 · the arg shape is implementation judgment) · `kinds` = only that kind · omitted · empty list = all (the same meaning as the screen filter — only the chosen kinds are shown · if none is chosen, all · Unreal Content Browser style · B1) |

| `library.move {paths, toFolder, onConflict?, yes?, detachRefs?}` · `library.rename {path, newName, yes?, detachRefs?}` (`yes` · `detachRefs` = when it makes `SKILL.md` of a referenced skill no longer a skill — `detachRefs` = name placeholder · the same as the `library.delete` line · ADR-0339) | Move · rename + fix references (2nd edition · flow ④ — no progress record · G38) — several `paths` (B7) · all paths of drag and drop (§1-10 `LibraryDnd`) are also this one command. **Explorer rules** (P5 · flow ④) — on the same name, choose with `onConflict` (overwrite — the displaced file goes to `.trash` · skip · keep both = 「(2)」 · value spelling is implementation judgment) · a same-name folder is merged · a folder into its own descendant = refused · if parent and child are given together, only the parent · `rename` to an existing name = refused (code + args) | The correspondence table (old → new — the screen also rewrites the unsaved edit draft lines inside the window that issued the command with this, keeping only the first place of the same path · main 2026-10-31 — M76-REVISED-2 · main 2026-11-01 M103) · the number fixed (Δ3) · if references were removed, the removed paths (「공통 인자 · 답 꼴」 below) · **per-item result** (「공통 인자 · 답 꼴」 below). If it meets the same name without `onConflict`, only that item fails with the 「충돌」 item result and nothing is overwritten (the caller chooses and calls again — M26). If `rename` makes `SKILL.md` of a referenced skill folder no longer a skill (that folder becomes an ordinary folder and the reference to that skill becomes 「찾을 수 없음」 — P7), without `yes` it is `CONFLICT` + the references involved + two forms (`--yes` = keep · `--yes --detach-refs` = also remove the reference) (M24-5 — a mirror of the screen confirmation modal [Cancel] [Remove references too and rename] [Rename] · `detachRefs` = after the rename, remove the line of the preset file that points to that skill folder — an agent attaches only presets, so `remove_preset_paths` is not called · user 2026-11-01 G63 · ADR-0339). If `move` moves `SKILL.md` of a referenced skill so that it leaves from directly under that skill folder (including into a subfolder of the same skill folder — M110), the same pair — without `yes` it is `CONFLICT` + the references involved + two forms (the same shape as delete) · `detachRefs` = after the move, remove the line that points to that skill folder (a mirror of the screen modal [Cancel] [Remove references too and move] [Move] · main 2026-11-01 M106 · ADR-0339) · moving or renaming a whole skill folder is an ordinary move (reference fix) |

Emptying · permanent delete is not this command — it is the shell-only `library.emptyTrash` (the last row of the table) |

| `preset.add {path, items, after?}` · `preset.remove {path, items}` · `preset.reorder {path, section, order, baseRev}` | Add · remove · order — applied to the latest file at once (flow ③ · the LLM · CLI's job — the screen edit field does not call these operations and uses a draft + `preset.save` · user 2026-10-31 G52) · the instructions · skill items of `add` take an optional `backend` (`claude` · `codex` · omitted = common — G29 · M20) · if the place to insert `after` is absent, it is appended to the end of that area (M14) | `add` · `remove` are idempotent even on a stale edition — a 「빼기」 sent after seeing a stale edition also removes that path from the current file (known behavior · accepted · `add` · `remove` have no edition check — M24-8) · `reorder` is `CONFLICT` if `baseRev` differs · adding several is `{added, skipped}` (Δ7) · answer = the new state of that preset (the same shape as `preset.get` — an edit does not change the agent backend, so it does not carry the list of affected agents either · user 2026-10-18) |

| `preset.save {path, instructions, skills, settings, presets, folders?, baseRev?}` (main 2026-10-31 — interface · user G52 · G53 · §3 · `baseRev` = M84) | The [Save] of the screen preset edit field — **replaces wholesale** that preset's four lists with the given lists (instructions · skill items = path strings (common) or `{path, backend}` — M20 · `presets` · `settings` items = path strings · refused if a tag is given — §1-2) · **no edition check — it overwrites whatever changed in the meantime (another slot · an LLM · a hand edit)** (the last save wins — the notepad rule · G53) · **optional `baseRev`** (main 2026-10-31 — M84) — the screen does not carry it (G53 notepad rule) · if carried (an LLM · CLI after reading), `CONFLICT` when it differs · the help guides the LLM to carry it or to use `add` · `remove` · `reorder` · the write is `format.rs` + `yaml-edit` as is — it computes the edits that go from the file now on disk to the given lists and applies them, so hand comments remain as far as `yaml-edit` preserves them · the pre-write gate as is (refusal = a stable code + an anchor arg — G48) · fields outside the lists (`description` etc.) are not touched · if in the meantime that preset has been moved, renamed or deleted, `NOT_FOUND` — it does not recreate the file at the old path (it checks first that the parent · target exist · so that `write_atomic` does not set it up again — the same as M65 · main 2026-10-31 — M76-REVISED-2) · root generation = the same optional argument as the other edit commands (M14 · M26) · **check = all or nothing** (main 2026-10-31 — M78 · M78-REVISED) — if even one item is off (a cycle · a wrong kind · a tag on a `presets` · `settings` item · a wrongly shaped reference (`AssetRef` — a library path `LibPath` or `engram:/` · main 2026-10-31 — M85) · a duplicate of the same item in a list (M85)) the whole save is refused and nothing is written · **a missing path is not a mismatch** — a 「찾을 수 없음」 line is legitimate content (P3 · M4 — a delete that keeps references leaves that line until restored or reconnected (a reference-removing delete removes the line — ADR-0339) · if it were blocked, such a preset could not be saved · main 2026-10-31 — M78-REVISED) · refusal answer = a stable code + an arg that lists every item that is off · the reason it differs from the partial application (Δ7 · G41) of `preset.add` = a save is a wholesale replace, so partial application would silently drop lines of the user's draft · the screen already shows it through the check on the draft (M75), so normally what is off does not reach the save | Answer = the same shape as `preset.get` · the screen edit field's save calls only this one command · the LLM · CLI call it by the same handle (CLAUDE.md 「LLM-우선 제어」) · the apply-at-once operations (`preset.add` · `remove` · `reorder` · `setBackend`) remain as they are |
| `preset.check {path, instructions, skills, settings, presets, folders?}` (main 2026-10-31 — M80) | The screen edit field's draft check (M75) — puts the four given lists into that preset's list places and runs them through the same resolution function as launch (§1-3) to produce the conflict lines · the problem list (including cycles) · **it does not write** (the file · `Catalog` · `rev` as is) · the client does not rewrite the resolution (the end of §1-3 「같은 함수」) | Answer = the same conflict · problem shape as `preset.get` · it is a read, so the LLM · CLI call it too (CLAUDE.md 「LLM-우선 제어」) · before the first scan, it starts or waits, then answers (「공통 인자 · 답 꼴」 below) |
| `preset.add {path, folders, after?}` · `preset.remove {path, folders}` · `preset.reorder {path, section: "folders", order, baseRev}` (ADR-0341 — the `folders` argument and section are this revision's proposal · final names follow `docs/reference/llm-command-conventions.md`) | Add · remove · reorder **folder entries** — applied to the latest file like the other `preset.*` edits (flow ③ · comment-preserving — T2) · `add` takes absolute paths of **existing, readable folders** (otherwise refused with code + args — typo guard · an existing folder that cannot be read — access denied or a stat timeout — is refused too: it would block launch with ① at once · review round 2) and refuses a relative path and a path failing `path_is_cli_safe` (§1-2) · each path is normalized before the check and stored normalized (§1-2) · **several folders in one `add` = all or nothing** (review round 1) — one missing, unreadable, relative or cmd-unsafe path refuses the whole call and the refusal names each bad path; nothing is written (unlike Δ7's partial application of library items — a typo in one of several hand-typed paths should not leave the others half-added) · `items` and `folders` are not mixed in one call · a folder already in the list (same `path_key` of the normalized path) is skipped like Δ7 — a skip, not a refusal · **matching in `remove` · `reorder` · `add after?`** (review round 3) — a folder entry is matched by the `path_key` of the normalized path, never by spelling: `remove` drops every spelling of that folder in the file (a hand-edited `D:\Rules\` and `d:/rules` both go) · `reorder`'s `order` is the deduplicated list as `preset.get` returns it, and the write stores the normalized form with spelling duplicates merged, as a save does · `after` names its anchor folder in any spelling | answer = `preset.get` shape · folder edits never touch `Catalog` references (a folder entry is not an asset — §1-2) |
| `preset.previewFolder {folder, backend?, mode?}` (name placeholder · ADR-0341 decision 7) | Folder scan preview — reads the folder (read only · bounded — §1-4 `folder.rs`) and returns, per backend and mode (or the one asked), what will load: claude = the predicted native set (marked as a prediction) · codex JSON = the instruction file + the skills under the skill roots, found by codex's root-walk rules with malformed skills marked (§1-5 folder load set) · codex terminal = nothing, with what the notice would list (§1-5 folder load set) · the answer also carries the normalized path (§1-2) and **whether `preset.add` would accept this path** (field names = implementation judgment — the same checks and the same code + args as an `add` refusal: relative · cmd-unsafe · not an existing folder · existing but unreadable (access denied · stat timeout)) | read only · LLM · CLI · screen (the folder line's detail card — R29 · and the editor's typo guard — §1-10 「Folder section」) call it · a missing or unreadable folder answers with the same code + args as block ① (the stat is the bounded one — §1-3) |
| `agent.presets {agent}` | The agent's 「기본」 · 「작업 폴더」 (`cwd` · whether it is locked and why — running · has conversed · P2-REVISED) · preset fields · backend · mode (agent values — profile `command`) · conflict lines (§1-3 5) · problem list (= the same lines as the four launch block reasons — those inside the attached presets come with the path they came by · including one per agent (no library folder) · B3) · **notice list `notices`** (M19 — instructions · skills the codex terminal does not load (P4-FINAL) · dim lines not loaded on this backend (P12 · M20) · ~~schema dimming (P10)~~ (dropped — ADR-0359) — §1-3 6) · `baseRev` (a stable hash of the attached preset list — in the order of `default_preset` and `presets` · M11 · M25) — no warnings (user 2026-10-18) | R31 · the list inside the screen's block window is also this answer (§1-10) · it resolves the problem list · conflict lines with the in-memory `Catalog`, so before the first scan it starts or waits, then answers (「공통 인자 · 답 꼴」 below · review 2026-10-31) |
| Tree ⛔ read (the name belongs to the client step — placeholder · §1-11a finding 18) | Per agent 「지금 실행하면 막힘」 (P8 · §1-3 「트리 ⛔」) — calculated on the spot with the in-memory `Catalog` (pure expansion · check — the settings check is cached by content tag · 「찾을 수 없음」 is calculated when viewed · the parse of the file itself · schema errors are what is placed on the item — G43) | Answer = per agent whether ⛔ and the block reason (the same code + args as the problem list — shape is the client step) · before the first scan, it starts or waits, then answers (「첫 훑기」 below) · **when it is called** (user 2026-10-31 G43) = the same occasions as the library list (tree slot open · focus · refresh (F5 · `library.refresh`) — the query invalidation of §1-10 「데이터」) + after the agent list changes (the client that received `agent-list-updated` calls it) · **it does not ride on a broadcast** — no new broadcast (the profile-edit port runs inside the library write lock, so broadcast → ⛔ calculation → a read lock on the same thread = deadlock · finding 18) · so what an LLM · CLI changed shows on the next occasion |
| `agent.setWorkspace {agent, cwd}` | Change the working folder — body = manager (§1-5 「작업 폴더 `cwd`」) | If the path is missing or not a folder, an error · if it has conversed (a persisted session id) · or is running, `CONFLICT` — code + args (R49 · user 2026-10-18) |
| `agent.attachPreset {agent, presets, after?}` · `agent.detachPreset {agent, presets, yes?}` · `agent.reorderPresets {agent, order, baseRev}` | Attach · detach · order — after validation, to the profile-edit port (M18) | It takes only presets (Δ7) · a partial-application one-line result + that agent's new state (the same shape as `agent.presets` — the same rule as the `preset.*` answer · so the screen does not call `agent.presets` again after every edit · flow review G17) · attaching and detaching leave the backend as it is (an agent value — user 2026-10-18 · withdraws the old 「기본 제공 백엔드는 사용할 수 없음 상태로 둔다(3차 델타)」). Detaching the default preset (기본) is `CONFLICT` without `yes` (Δ9) · the `baseRev` of `reorderPresets` = the value `agent.presets` gave — if it differs, `CONFLICT` (M11) |
| `agent.new` (changed) | `backend` + mode (both agent values · fixed at creation — no command to change them · user 2026-10-18) + `cwd` + `presets?` (the old `files?` is withdrawn — the codex terminal writes nothing to the working folder · P4-FINAL) | The old `preset` (working-folder bookmark id) field is withdrawn — so the current 「`cwd` · `preset` 중 정확히 하나」 narrows to 「`cwd` 필수」 — it is kept as an arg so the LLM creates and launches in one go (user 2026-10-18 「llm은 그냥 한방에 실행할수 있게 기존처럼 인자 두는게 맞고」 · creation without `cwd` = next topic §8) · the validation · attachment of `presets` = the same body as `agent.attachPreset` (`LibraryCommandHost` → profile-edit port) called inside the same handler (one round trip — today `verb_new` is called only by the manager · flow review G17) · **validation runs on the profile to be created (outside the registry) in front of `create_agent`** — **partial application** (user 2026-10-31 G41 · Δ7 — the same rule as `agent.attachPreset`): a preset that does not fit is skipped and it is created with only those that fit attached · answer = `{agentId}` + the skipped (the same shape as the partial-application result of `agent.attachPreset`) · a failure after creation (a race at the edit-port step) is `INTERNAL` holding the number + the created agent id · reason (§1-5 (가) 1 ② · review 2026-10-31) · the old branch 「통째로 거절 — 아무것도 안 만들고 `CONFLICT`」 is withdrawn (§1-11a finding 7 closed) |
| `agent.duplicate {agent}` | Clone an agent — carries over the attached presets (default + preset field) · backend · mode · working folder · passthrough args · environment variables, and does not carry over the session id · conversation · running state · last-applied instructions hash · notice fingerprint (ADR-0341) (a new conversation — the working folder is not locked) · body = manager (§1-5 「복제」 · B13 · 1st edition) | New agent id · name 「<이름>(2)」 · (3) … (the app's existing name-issuing rule — no space · M12 · §1-5 「복제」) — the screen's agent-tree right-click 「복제」 is also this command (CLAUDE.md 「LLM-우선 제어」) |
| `agent.spawn` (as meant) | Bus · CLI launch = `Unattended` · **one request** (G15 — there is no pre-check command) | If blocked, an **error answer** — the four block reasons in the args field of `CommandError` (a fixed code + args per reason · the items involved · the presets that reference them · for a conflict, its conflict lines — §1-3 8 · §1-5 `LaunchBlocked`) · the CLI gives a non-zero exit code (M14 · M19 — acceptance criterion) · success answer = the notice list `notices` (G28 — the CLI prints it as is · it goes only to the caller and nothing is sent to the launched agent). When the user says 「고쳐 줘」, the LLM edits the file and `library.refresh` → launches again (user 2026-10-18). The activation the screen issues (wire) also receives the same reasons as a widened error answer (§1-8). *Landed in step 16* — block → `CONFLICT` + `args.reasons` · success rows `{code, args, line, text}` (shapes: §5-2 16 「Step 16 landed」). *Step 20 (`0badd007`)* — with `--cwd`, a block before the add → `CONFLICT` + `args.reasons`, nothing created; a failure after the add (incl. a block there) → `INTERNAL` + `args.agent_id` (+ `reasons`) — shell bus `agent.spawnInto` answers the same way and also carries `notices` (§5-2 20) |
| `library.emptyTrash {items?}` (name is a placeholder · **shell only**) | Empty the trash · permanent delete (2nd edition · people only — G12) — omitting `items` = empty everything (the screen sends the bundle ids that the trash view listed and the daemon empties only those bundles — an item deleted after the list stays in `.trash` · the arg name is implementation judgment · main 2026-10-31 — M83 · if there is neither `items` nor a bundle id it is refused — M83's guarantee whoever calls · main 2026-10-31 — M86) · if given, permanently deletes only that item · there is one root, so no root arg (G36) · hands over to the daemon PC's OS trash and **the OS decides** (whether it goes into the trash or is deleted as is — user 2026-10-31 G47 · withdraws the old 「엔진이 확정할 때만 영구 삭제」 · guard · platform permanent-delete function (M59 · M70)) · an item gone after the call = emptied (it does not carry whether it went into the trash — withdraws the old 「휴지통 확인 안 됨」 (M71)) · an item left because the OS refused (an open file · permissions etc.) is not deleted but left in `.trash` and reported with its item result (`{code, args}` · the original code is a diagnostic — main 2026-10-31 M62) (platform — §1-6 · §6-5 2) | **The daemon accepts only what comes from the shell connection and refuses what comes through the control routes (`engram` CLI · agent)** (M6 — no new wire variant) · it is not carried in the agent command table · priming · `engram help` · per-item result (P5 — the same partial-failure rule · emptying everything sends the bundle folder `<UTC>-<id>` whole (user 2026-10-31 G44) so the result item = a bundle — a bundle whose whole send failed is resent item by item, and that result carries the per-item results (R6) · §1-4 `store.rs` row · review 2026-10-31 R5) · **the refusal spot = the `caller` of `call_daemon_command` (`control/commands.rs:170`), the only entrance of the three surfaces** (flow review G17) — the two control routes are `None` · the bus is `Some(연결)` and a table handler cannot see that value, so the name check is placed there (the same place as `admit_input`) · it is declared in the daemon table (agent block) — the shape of putting it as a shell-block command and having the shell send it on to the daemon is not allowed (if the CLI reaches the shell name through a relay (`CallOutcome::Relay`), it comes in as `caller=Some(셸)`) · how to remove it from `engram help` · the discovery list = implementation judgment |

**Common args · answer shapes (공통 인자 · 답 꼴)**
- **Root generation** (M14) — commands that carry and edit library paths (`library.move` · `rename` · `copy` · `delete` · `restore` · `newFolder` · `newFile` · `relink` · `preset.add` · `remove` · `reorder` · `setBackend` · `save` · `agent.attachPreset` · `detachPreset` · `reorderPresets`) carry the root generation that `library.status` gave, and refuse it if it is an old generation after the root changed (code + args). **It is an optional arg** (M26) — if carried it is checked, and CLI · LLM calls that do not carry it apply to the current root. Arg name = implementation judgment.
- **`yes` four cases (yes 경우 넷)** (M21 · M24-5 · M106) — deleting a referenced item (for a folder, the references of everything inside are combined — P7) · 「기본」 detach · a rename that makes `SKILL.md` of a referenced skill folder no longer a skill · moving that `SKILL.md` out of the folder (`library.move`). If absent, `CONFLICT` + the references involved · the screen calls it with `yes=true` after the confirmation modal. For delete · `SKILL.md` rename · move out, the optional `detachRefs` (placeholder) also removes the references — default keep · two forms in that `CONFLICT` · the screen's [Remove references too and move] · [Remove references too and rename] · [Remove references too and move out] are `yes=true, detachRefs=true` (ADR-0339).
- **Folder entries in the existing answers (ADR-0341)** — `preset.get` · `preset.check` · `agent.presets` carry the `folders` list (path · status ok / not found / unreadable · chain for nested presets) and folder lines in the problem list. **`preset.get` returns the preset's own folder list normalized and deduplicated** (review round 2 — §1-2 normalizer · first place kept, as in resolution): a hand-edited file may spell one folder twice (`D:\Rules\` · `d:/rules`), and the screen builds its draft from `preset.get` and sends the whole list to `preset.save`, which would reject the duplicate under M85 — so the screen never sends one. The file text is not rewritten by the read; the next save writes the normalized list. `preset.save` and `preset.check` take an optional `folders` list: given = replaces the folder list like the other lists, stored normalized (§1-2) (a missing folder is legal content, like a 「찾을 수 없음」 line — M78-REVISED — so `save` does not repeat `add`'s existence check: an entry whose folder disappeared after it was added saves as is; a relative path is a mismatch that rejects the whole save — M78; **two entries that are the same folder after normalization** (same `path_key` — e.g. `D:\Rules\` and `d:/rules`) are a duplicate under M85 and reject the whole save; a cmd-unsafe path is legal content here, like a hand edit — it blocks claude with ② at launch, and the editor never sends one because its typo guard refused it — §1-10); omitted = the file's folder list stays as is (an LLM using the older shape does not wipe folder entries); the screen always sends it. `agent.spawn` blocks and notices carry folder lines (§1-3) · its notice list is empty when the agent has already been shown the same set (§1-5 「Notices once per agent」). `library.relink` · `move` · `rename` · `delete` answers never list folder entries. Root generation applies to folder edits as to any preset edit (they write the preset file).
- **`detachRefs` gate** (main 2026-11-01 M108 · ADR-0339 — so the LLM is not confused) — if only `detachRefs` is given without `yes`, it is the same as no `yes` (if there are references, `CONFLICT` + two forms) · if given to an operation outside those three (delete · a rename that makes `SKILL.md` of a skill folder no longer a skill · move), `INVALID_ARGUMENT` (code + args — it is not silently ignored) · whether it is one of those three is decided by the shape of the operation, not by whether references exist (so that the refusal does not split on a reference state the caller cannot see). **The operation shape of `detachRefs`** (main 2026-11-01 M110 — the same criterion · so the LLM is not confused) — this argument applies, per item within one operation, only to items of the following shapes: deleting an item · renaming a skill folder's `SKILL.md` · a move in which `SKILL.md` leaves from directly under that skill folder (a move into a subfolder of the same skill folder also counts, since `SKILL.md` is no longer directly under that folder — R12) · for an item of a matching shape, the reference only decides whether there is actually a line to remove (if there are no references, no line is removed and it is not a refusal) · other items of the same multi-path operation are not affected · `INVALID_ARGUMENT` applies only when the operation has no item of that shape at all.
- **Answer of an operation that removed references** (main 2026-11-01 M103 · M105 · ADR-0339) — it carries the removed paths (all paths the operation deleted — flow ④ (c)) · the changed presets · agents · the per-item result · the screen of the window that issued the command also removes the lines of those paths from the unsaved edit draft open in that window (§1-10 「편집 초안」).
- **Per-item result** (P5) — operations that handle several items (`library.move` · `copy` · `delete` · `restore` · `emptyTrash`) return per item what was done · what was skipped · failures (open · permissions — code + args) and leave what was done as done (Explorer rules — on a failure the screen offers [Retry] [Skip] [Cancel]).
- **First scan (첫 훑기)** (review 2026-10-31) — every command that reads `Catalog` (`library.*` read · `preset.get` · `preset.check` · `agent.presets` · tree ⛔ read · edit commands that carry the problem list · reference counts in the answer or use `Catalog` in attachment validation · `library.*` edits whose plan or validation reads `Catalog` — `library.rename` · `move` · `copy` · `delete` · `relink` · review 2026-10-31 R2) starts the first scan if it has not happened yet, or waits for the one in progress, and then answers — the same entrance as `library.list` (§1-4 flow ①) · this wait also attaches to a first scan that is already walking (since it only needs `Catalog` to exist) — `library.refresh` is different: it does not attach to a scan that has started walking and instead attaches to a queued generation or scans with its own ticket (flow ① 「잠금」 ⑤ · review 2026-10-31 R3) — but before the first scan, even if a folder is given it passes through this entrance (if there is no first scan, it starts the full first scan and attaches · if one is already walking, after it ends with its own ticket — when it wakes and 「첫 훑기 끝」 is still not so (that scan failed and did not go up), it passes through this entrance again instead of the ticket · R5 · the answer is that folder's share — scanning only the folder would turn references outside the folder into false 「찾을 수 없음」 · 「첫 훑기 끝」 = the full scan went up · it does not change with a folder scope · R4) · **an edit command waits at the method entrance** — before it is counted as a change in progress (before drawing a scan-generation ticket) · before taking the library lock (if reversed, an edit in progress would block that scan, and that scan's swap would wait for the write lock the edit holds and block itself — flow ① 「잠금」 ② · R2) · a wait that exceeds its deadline = §1-11a finding 17. A launch (`agent.spawn` · wire activation) is not this entrance — it reads the attached files directly from disk.
- **Blocks · notices** (G15 · M19 · G28) — block = an error answer (the `CommandError` args field — a command crate change · §1-8) · a non-blocking notice = a launch success answer · `agent.presets`'s separate list `notices` (`{code, args}` — §3).

**Withdrawn commands** — `library.moves` (move history — G34) · the shell `collection.*` eight (favorites — G34) · `agent.launchCheck` (pre-launch check — G15 · M21: a launch is one request and `agent.presets` already carries the problem list) · the old `agent.setCodexFiles` (codex file answer) · `library.ackRelink` · the root add · remove · rename commands from the multi-root days (G36).

**Shell commands (declared in the shell block · catalog 21 → 22)** — the client step's job. Names · shapes are re-settled after the server step in the direction of G19 · G22 · G23 (screen = a package that is turned off and on · the first case of a slot package · the in-slot view state is stored on the package side) (G20).

| Name | What it does |
|---|---|
| `slot.openUnique {content, target?}` | If a slot with the same content exists in any window, focus that slot (bring the window forward) · if not, fill the target (omitted = the existing rule for choosing a column place). Uniqueness itself is kept by the layout manager (§1-9) |
| `libraryView.open {number}` · `libraryView.navigate {number, folder}` · `libraryView.back` · `libraryView.forward` | Open a numbered slot · move folder · record (R14 — the recording rule is a pure function of the shell) |
| `libraryView.reveal {number?, path}` | Go to that item's place — expand the tree and select (R15 · R28) · omitted number = the Δ6 rule · double-click on an edit-field line · the magnifier (R28 — in the agent slot · inside the block window too) is also this (§1-10 「막힘 창」 — if no number is open, it opens that numbered slot · before the first scan, it waits and then selects) |
| `libraryView.select {number, paths}` · `libraryView.setLayout {number, widths, sort, filter}` · `libraryView.get {number}` | Selection · column width · sort · filter · current state — tree expansion (per number) · 「기본 제공 보기」 on/off (per number — G2) and the common collapse · expand (`library_collapse`) are also changed by this command group (command names are implementation judgment · PRD R10 · R11) |
| `agentPresets.open {agentId}` | Open one more agent preset slot (R30) — the field-header collapse · expand common to all agent slots (§1-1 · separate from `library_collapse`) is also changed by this command group (command names are implementation judgment) |

`libraryView.*` is **a shell command on top of the per-number view state the shell holds** (the 「레이아웃 명령」 side of R40). The shell changes the state and fires `library-view:updated {number, state}` only at the windows that hold a slot of that number. The screen also sends a person's gestures with the same command (invoke) and only draws the state it receives — no new global handle · webview registry command.

**Unsaved edit drafts and shell · bus commands** (user 2026-10-31 G54 — replaces the `CONFLICT` · `yes` gates of M82 · main 2026-10-31 — M90 · M93) — shell commands · the registry · bus commands (`slot.close` · `tab.close` · `window.close` · `libraryView.*` choice change · `tab.move` · `tab.dock` · `tab.detach` · `layout.setSlotContent` · `slot.assignAgent` · `libraryView.reveal` etc.) and the OS `CloseRequested` neither block nor ask about an unsaved draft — the draft stays in a separate draft file on the shell side and comes back alive as ● when you return to that preset at that number or reopen library n (key = M89-REVISED · M92 · §1-9 · §1-10 「편집 초안」) · `yes` is not added to the shell close command (the `yes` case is as in the 「`yes` 경우」 line above) · the premise of popout close (it cannot be cancelled — S21-slot-docking Q12 · the prevent_close check of `/qa`) is unchanged (M90). The save prompt is shown by the webview holding the draft before it calls the command inside the human-gesture handler (list = §1-10 「편집 초안」 ①). **Reading and writing the draft = an invoke-only shell command** — it is not put in the shell command table (the table stays 22 · it is not on the bus — G52) · the content is opaque to the shell and the frontend owns the shape (ADR-0270 as is — M93). There is no draft in the daemon or the bus (G52 — the daemon's share is only `preset.save` · `preset.check`). **Known gap** (M93) — an LLM cannot see, save or discard a person's edit draft (G52) · writing this gap into the CLAUDE.md 「LLM-우선 제어」 remaining-gaps is done at §5 15a, and the user confirms it then (a CLAUDE.md change).

**CLI** — the general form `engram <이름> --인자 값` reads the daemon's runtime table, so the CLI code does not change. A boolean stands without a value (`--yes`). The screen buttons are also the same command — they call it with `yes=true` after going through the confirmation modal. A blocked launch gives a non-zero exit code + a fixed code + args per reason (M14 · M19 — acceptance criterion) · the notice list of a launch success is printed as is (G28). The shell-only `library.emptyTrash` is not reachable by the CLI (M6).

**Help — grouped by topic (B12 · user 2026-10-19 — the top-level help is not bloated)** — at the top of `engram help` only one topic line (e.g. `library`) is added, and `engram help library` carries the settings · preset file format + an example containing `description:` + one sentence of 「설정 · 프리셋 파일을 만들거나 고칠 때 한 줄 설명을 적는다」. The priming (Engram's guide in 「기본」) carries the same content. The screen (help) is produced by the daemon (`/control/help`) — it has not been confirmed whether today's help contract accepts a topic (§6-2).

**Only people do this** — emptying the library trash and **permanent delete** are a shell-only command (`library.emptyTrash`) and are not in the agent command table · priming · `engram help` · the daemon refuses what comes by a control route (M6 — so that even if an agent deletes wrongly it can be restored). We accept that an agent could in theory reach the WS or delete `.trash` with its own tool (`rm` etc.) — no exception list · mechanism is added to 「LLM-우선 제어」 (G21 · P13 · M24-7). Delete (→ `.trash`) · trash view · restore (`library.restore`) are also done by the LLM · CLI. The screen path is §6-5 2.

### 1-8. wire · generation

- **Remove the old preset wire** — `ListPresets` · `CreatePreset` · `DeletePreset` · `RenamePreset` · `PresetListUpdated` · `PresetList` · domain `Preset` · `PresetId`.
- **Widen the wire activation reply** (G15 · M19 — a launch is one request · no old pre-check command) — a launch made by a person goes out from the screen as a wire activation (`SpawnProfile` etc.). When blocked, the failure reply (`AgentEvent::Error` — today only the `message` string) carries a **structured block reason** (§1-5 `LaunchBlocked` — a code plus arguments for each reason · the items involved · the path they came by), and the success reply (`Spawned`) carries the notice list `notices` (§1-3 6) — from that one reply the webview tells the block window from the notice popup (§1-10 · client step). The field name and shape (whether to add a field to the current variant or to keep a separate variant) are left to implementation. *Landed in step 16 (P4 `8200eae4`)* — fields on the existing variants: `AgentEvent::Error.launch_blocked` (optional — the `LaunchBlocked::to_args` object `{"reasons":[…]}`, free JSON so the protocol crate stays independent of the agent crate) on all three activation verbs · `Spawned.notices` (optional rows `{code, args, line, text}`) filled by `SpawnProfile` and `SpawnByCwd`; `Spawn` replies `Ack` and drops its notices. Absent = byte-identical old events · `PROTOCOL_VERSION` stays 8. No WS path records the notice fingerprint yet, and the Tauri carrier still folds `Error` to text — both are step 11's (§5-4 11).
- **An argument field on the bus error** (M19) — add to the command crate's `CommandError` an argument field that carries `{code, args}` (bus `agent.spawn` block · the referenced items that `CONFLICT` is caught on · the reason for a lock, etc. — §1-7 「공통 인자 · 답 꼴」 · §3). It crosses the wire in the envelope reply `CommandReply`, so it falls under the version bump below · how it fits the current 「받은 원문 보존」 rule of that type is left to implementation. **The control route (CLI) envelope is widened as well** (flow check G17) — a refusal from `/control/call` · `/control/agent` goes out as `ControlQueryResult::Error{code, hint}` (`control/ingress.rs:442`) and `catalog::refused` folds `CommandError` into just those two — the argument field is carried here too (the CLI prints the body it receives as is, so the CLI code stays unchanged · the outside code = §1-5 (가) 1). *Landed in step 16 (P1 `b454a359`)* — that citation is the pre-step-16 shape: now `ControlQueryResult::Error { code, hint, args }` (`control/ingress.rs`) and the body is `{status, code, hint, args?}` — `args` only when present, so a refusal without arguments is byte-identical; both `refused` folds (`control/catalog.rs` · `control/agent.rs`) carry `CommandError::args`. `CommandError` keeps the received-verbatim rule: a known code's non-empty object is typed, `null` / `{}` fold to absence, any other shape or `args` next to an unknown code stays in the received extras and is relayed verbatim · `set_code` drops `args`.
- **`PROTOCOL_VERSION` 7 → 8 — once on this branch** (the three above — removing the old preset wire · widening the activation reply · the argument field on the bus error). It is bumped in the first step that carries any of the three changes, and later steps use the same number (the same rule as the catalog below · step = §5).
- **The wire and screen shape of `cwd` stay as they are** — `CreateProfile.cwd` required · wire `AgentProfile.cwd` · `AgentInfo.cwd` · the meaning of bus `AgentRow.cwd` is the same as today (no empty value — the meaning "empty string = none" is the next topic, §8). Changing the working folder is the bus command `agent.setWorkspace` (§1-7), so no wire message is added — the changed value flows along today's path for refreshing the agent list (likely — confirm at implementation).
- There is no new wire message for the library — everything is a bus envelope (§1-7). The shell-only `library.emptyTrash` is also a bus envelope and creates no new wire variant (M6).
- agent catalog 8 → 9 · shell catalog 21 → 22 — **bump in the step that first changes the declarations** (agent = the step that first edits the library · preset commands or the old `agent.new` field · shell = the step that removes the `preset_palette` of `SlotContentKind` or first adds a shell command — step number = §5). Later steps on the same branch are before release and use the same number, but the generated artifacts (`commands.schema.json` · bindings) are regenerated in every step. If a parallel branch bumped first, +1 on top of it at merge.

### 1-9. shell (`src-tauri`)

> **The details of the client step are settled after the server step** (G20 — server first). The direction = G19 · G22 · G23: the screen is a package that can be turned on and off (the daemon feature is always on — G22-RESOLVED) · the first case of a 「슬롯 패키지」 (a package that adds a slot) · the in-slot view state goes to the store the package owns (G23-NOTE — main-session proposal · before user confirmation). What follows is the draft so far and will be realigned then.

- **Slot kind (`layout/types.rs`)** — `SlotContent::Library { number: u8 }` (a value outside 1~4 = an empty slot) · `SlotContent::AgentPresets { agent_id: String }`. `PresetPalette` is removed, and the old `{"type":"preset_palette"}` is folded into an empty slot on read. Also fix the bus `SlotContentKind` mirror · the persisted shape · the exhaustion check (`state/schema.rs` · `convert.rs`) together. It is an ordinary slot, so pop-out and docking (wt2) attach as they are (R42).
- **The layout manager keeps the uniqueness of `Library{n}`** — the single path that writes slot content (`write_slot_content` — `set_slot_content` · the bus `layout.setSlotContent` goes through it) refuses with `CONFLICT` if the same number already exists in any window, and restore (`from_persisted`) folds the second and later slots with the same number into an empty slot plus a restore warning. Moving a slot (`move_slot_to_new_view`) passes through, it does not duplicate. `slot.openUnique` is only a convenience on top of that (focus if present).
- **Per-number view state (new `library_view/` — authority)** — memory `[LibraryViewState; 4]` + the `state.json` optional field `library_views` (a field that still reads when missing — `version` not bumped · carried in the save-thread snapshot). The way to change it is the single shell command `libraryView.*` (shared by human gestures and the LLM — §1-7), and the recording rule (R14) is a pure function here. When it changes, `library-view:updated` is emitted to the windows that have a slot of that number, and when the selection changes, the summary `library-views:selection {number, kinds, touched_at}` is emitted to all windows — the edit area in another window decides ⇐ active (R24), and pressing ⇐ receives that number's selection path from the shell.
- **Edit draft file (user 2026-10-31 G54 · G54-ADD — replaces M82's shell-memory draft)** — the unsaved edit-area draft (four lists + `folders` + the unsaved marker + the draft number — M81) is written to a separate draft file in the Engram data folder on the shell side (e.g. `shell\state\drafts\` — not the library folder · not `state.json` · file name · shape · write timing = client step) · **reading and writing = invoke-only shell commands** (outside the shell command table — the table stays at 22 · not on the bus · the content is opaque to the shell · the shape is owned by the frontend — main 2026-10-31 — M93) · the shell does not depend on the agent crate (ADR-0270 unchanged · gate 4i) · **key = (daemon identity `daemon_id` · library root · library number · preset path)** (main 2026-10-31 — M89-REVISED · M92 · if the key were the number alone, the LLM switching presets would overwrite the previous preset's draft · the daemon identity has the same shape as the personal-data key — §7 12) — even if library n is switched from A to B (by the LLM — no prompt), A's draft file stays, and coming back to A at that number restores it with ● · reopening library n or restarting the app restores the preset last shown in the view state together with that pair's draft (● if there is a draft) · after the app dies, only if the screen restore was accepted, and if it is declined the draft is still not deleted — it appears when that key is opened (main 2026-10-31 — M94) · opening the same preset under two numbers gives two drafts — the last save wins (G53) · **base-edition hint** (main 2026-10-31 — M96 · replaces M91's file-identity share) — the draft file also records the base `rev` it started from (M21 — from `preset.get`), and on restore, if it differs from the current `rev`, the 「원본이 그 사이 바뀜」 hint is shown but the draft is attached anyway ([Save] is last save wins per G53) · it is not tied to the OS file identity of the original (ADR-0325 decision 5 uses identity only as a scan tag · the atomic write changes it on every save · and the client has no source for that value) · a draft whose daemon identity · root differs from the current library is not attached (M92) — it stays as an orphan, and how it is shown = client step · a delete reply received by the same window deletes that draft file (M96), and a rename · move reply moves the open draft's key · save target to the new path (and deletes the old draft file), and the view-state selection follows the same correspondence table (M91) · ghost prevention covers only a delete in the same window — if the LLM · another window · the outside deletes it and recreates it at the same path, it is attached with the hint (G53 · the general guarantee needs a stable identity per file — G38 · T-74 + the ADR-0325 amendment · not now · M96) · the sweep target = the drafts of presets that were deleted, renamed or moved (shape = client step · M91) · [Save] of a restored draft carries the **current** root generation of `library.status` (the generation is a memory value — M14 · M92) · deleted on [Save] success (only when the draft is still the sent number r — if it was edited further after sending, the file stays · M88), 「저장 안 함」 or [Revert] · the file also stays on moving a tab · attach · detach (human drag · bus `tab.move` · `tab.dock` · `tab.detach`) · shell · registry · bus command · OS `CloseRequested` (including Alt+F4 on a pop-out and closing from the taskbar) — no refusal · no `yes` gate · no prompt (§1-7 · M90) · **the save prompt is the webview's job** (main 2026-10-31 — M90) — the webview holding the draft raises it inside the human-gesture handler before calling the command (list = §1-10 「편집 초안」 ① — exhaustive · M95) · closing the whole dashboard (tray 「완전 종료」 — the main ✕ hides, so it does not close tabs · ADR-0026 · ADR-0229) does not prompt (the draft file stays and shows ● at the next start — G54 · M95).
- **(Deferred 2026-10-31 · G34) The collection module · its save file** — not in this edition (favorites were dropped · if it returns, it is on the client side — G33 · starting point = the deferral row in §1-1).
- **Agent slot collapse field (new)** — 「기본」 · 「작업 폴더」 · 「프리셋」 one set of header collapse states (common to all agent slots) · a `state.json` optional field (still reads when missing · separate from `library_collapse`) · when it changes, it is emitted to the windows that have an agent slot — the same shape as the per-number view state (user 2026-10-18 · the split is by session selection).
- **Local OS requests (new `commands/local_os.rs`)** — `os_pick_folder` (the library root · the 「찾아보기」 of the agent slot's 「작업 폴더」 field) · `os_open_path(abs)` · `os_reveal(abs)` — the shell Rust calls the dialog · opener plugin Rust API (the webview opener permission is not widened). For now always on (same PC — memo 63) · turned off for remote. The webview's two direct `open({directory:true})` calls (`agentCommands.ts:22` · `slotContentCommands.ts:73`) are also moved onto this path — the third one, `presetCommands.ts:76`, is removed together with the old preset palette, so it is not moved (M16 · review #18).
- **Block window (new — G13 · basic only — P11 · M15)** — when a launch made by a person is blocked (the webview receives the launch's error reply — a structured block reason · §1-8 — and requests it · one request — G15), the shell creates that agent's block window (§1-10 `LaunchBlockedWindow`) — a frameless window · **owner = the main window** (not global always-on-top — M15) · position = beside that row in the main window's agent tree (multi-monitor · DPI) · at most one per agent (reuse it if present) · window creation goes through the single webview environment finishing function (`WebviewEnv::finish` — ADR-0283) · the shell's window-destruction cleanup (where the output channel registrations are withdrawn) handles that window's label group (M15 · review #9) · fix a route (the name is left to implementation · M15) · close = × · Esc only. The command that raises it is added to the existing `agentPresets.*` group (name · shape left to implementation · no new global handle). Its other lifetime aspects (several blocks · reopening · [Run again] · the unblocked state · tray hiding · the reason that the working folder vanished) are the next topic (P11). Whether it can be raised = §6-2.
- **Setting key** — `confirm.detachDefaultPreset` (「다시 묻지 않기」 · default on). There is no settings window, so reverting is only through the `settings.set` command (T5 · E3).

> **The details of the client step are settled after the server step** (G20 — server first · there is no fake prototype that builds only the screen first — M25). | `AddItemsPopover` | 「+ 추가」 · the area `+` search popover (R25 · it has both the whole 「+ 추가」 and the area `+` — R23 · precedent = Unreal My Blueprint · `docs/research/global-vs-section-add-button-2026-10-19.md` · B4) — on open the cursor is in the search field · check = add (for a preset target it goes into the edit draft — the file is unchanged until saved · user 2026-10-31 G52 · reverses the immediate apply of memo 83 (2026-10-14) · for an agent target it is attached immediately — main 2026-10-31 — convention · the user's delegation G53) · already added/cycle/conflict disabled + the reason · for an agent target, presets only + a pinned top 「기본 제공」 list (one 「기본」 — a way to reattach a detached default · Δa) · **relink mode (1st edition — P1)** — the [Relink] on a 「찾을 수 없음 · [다시 연결] ×」 row (dotted) opens the same popover filtered to just that kind, with the old name filled in (the filling is our convenience — no peer basis) · nothing is linked before choosing · choosing one makes `library.relink` → 「N개 더 찾아 연결함」 one line (flow ② · B11 · replaces the old `RelinkPicker`) | @radix-ui/react-popover · @tanstack/react-virtual |
| `DetailFlyout` | Edit-area row detail card — drawn into the window's layout root through a portal and **is not clipped** (D11 confirmed 2026-10-18 · convention = Floating UI `flip()` before `shift()` (confirmed) · Radix · MUI · NSPopover use the same order · covering the reference element is the last resort). **Library 1~4** — the right-hand detail area is a column inside the window, so there is no overflow · the card for pressing a part row in the edit area attaches to the **left edge** of the right-hand area and its top = that row · it overlaps the center list (if the window is narrow, it covers the folder tree too). **Agent slot (edit component only — B15)** — ① it attaches **outside** the right side of the slot and overlaps the next slot ② if there is no room on the right, flip to outside the left of the slot (over the left neighbor slot) ③ if there is none on either side (the slot fills a pop-out window, etc.) it is pushed inside the window (covering the slot) — flip → shift → clamp. **Close** — Esc = the stacked cards, starting from the one opened last · × = that card · outside press = closes (2026-10-17) · pressing another part row = swaps the card · pressing the same row again = closes (R29 · E1 · B5). **Card block order** = the same as `LibraryDetail` (title → location line → content → references · B2). The old 「창 끝이면 일부 잘림 · 전부 창 밖일 때만 슬롯 안쪽」 (Δi) was removed | — |
| `LibraryDnd` | **Drag to move anywhere** (2nd edition · B6 · user 2026-10-19 「되는김에 다 되게해야지」) — dropping on a folder moves (one `library.move`). Source = list items (several — B7) · tree folders. Drop target = tree folders (slots of the same or another library) · list 📁 rows (the same or another slot) · the list of another slot (= that slot's current folder) — including tree → tree and list → list 📁. **There is one drag system** — list · tree · the edit area's ⋮⋮ ordering use the same library (react-arborist has its own drag (react-dnd) and the plan has dnd-kit elsewhere, so if both run together, a drag between slots does not know each other's drop targets — likely · the 0a research picks one). **Between different windows (pop-outs)** — the slots are in different webviews, so whether it works is unconfirmed (§6-5) — if it does not, only inside the same window works and that fact is written down (user confirmation 2026-10-19) · when a `SKILL.md` of a referenced skill is dropped so that it leaves directly below that skill folder (cut and paste is the same), the move confirmation of `DeleteConfirm` [Cancel] [Move and remove references] [Move] appears before the move (main 2026-11-01 M106 · ADR-0339) | One drag library (0a research — current candidates @dnd-kit/core · react-arborist's built-in react-dnd · §1-12) |
